Cakkhuviññāṇamattamevāti yattakaṃ cakkhuviññāṇaṃ rūpāyatane gahaṇamattaṃ, taṃmattameva me sabbaṃ cittaṃ bhavissatīti attho. “Rāgādirahenā”ti vā pāṭho. Diṭṭhaṃ nāma padatthato cakkhuviññāṇena diṭṭhaṃ rūpaṃ. Tatthevāti cakkhuviññāṇena diṭṭhamatte rūpe. Cittattayaṃ diṭṭhamattaṃ nāma cakkhuviññāṇaṃ viya rāgādivirahena pavattanato. Tenāha “yathā ta”nti ādi.

The ‘seen’ is so called because eye-consciousness is the seeing of the form base. Hence he said: ‘the cognizing of form in form.’ ‘Just the mere eye-consciousness’ means: ‘My entire mind will be just as much as the mere grasping of eye-consciousness in the form base.’ Or the reading is ‘through the absence of lust, etc.’ The ‘seen,’ according to the word's meaning, is the form seen by eye-consciousness. ‘Right there’ means in the form that is merely seen by eye-consciousness. The triad of consciousness is called ‘the merely seen’ because it proceeds like eye-consciousness, through the absence of lust, etc. Hence he said: ‘just as that,’ etc.

Manodvārāvajjanena [V 2.0021] viññātārammaṇaṃ viññātanti adhippetaṃ rāgādivirahena viññeyyato. Tenāha “yathā āvajjanenā”tiādi.

An object cognized by mind-door adverting is intended as ‘the cognized’ because it is to be cognized with an absence of lust and so on. Hence he said: ‘just as by adverting,’ etc.

Tadāti tasmiṃ kāle, na tato paṭṭhāyāti ayamettha atthoti dasseti. “Diṭṭhamatta”ntiādinā yesaṃ rāgādīnaṃ nivattanaṃ adhippetaṃ, te “tenā”ti ettha ta-saddena paccāmasīyantīti “tena rāgena vā ratto”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tatthāti visaye bhummaṃ, visayabhāvo ca visayinā sambandhavasena icchitabboti vuttaṃ “paṭibaddho”tiādi.

‘Then’ means at that time, not starting from then—this is the meaning he shows here. The lust and so on, the removal of which is intended by ‘the merely seen,’ etc., are referred back to here by the word ‘that’ (ta) in ‘by that’ (tena); thus it is said: ‘by that, impassioned by lust,’ etc. ‘There’ is a locative of sphere. And the state of being an object is to be understood by way of connection with the subject; thus it is said: ‘bound,’ etc.

Satīti rūpassa yathāsabhāvasallakkhaṇā sati muṭṭhā piyanimittamanasikārena anuppajjanato na dissati nappavattati. Ajjhosāti ajjhosāya. Gilitvā pariniṭṭhapetvā attaniyakaraṇena.

‘Mindfulness’: mindfulness, which has the characteristic of marking the true nature of form, is forgotten. Through attention to a pleasing sign, it does not arise; and because it does not arise, it is not seen and does not proceed. ‘Having clung’ means by clinging. Having swallowed, having brought to an end, by making it one's own.

Abhijjhā ca vihesā cāti karaṇatthe paccattavacananti āha – “abhijjhāya ca vihesāya cā”ti. Atthavasena vibhattipariṇāmoti āha – “abhijjhāvihesāhī”ti. Ācinantassāti vaḍḍhentassa. Paṭissatoti patissato sabbattha satiyā yutto. Sevato cāpīti ettha ca-saddo api-saddo ca nipātamattanti “sevantassa”icceva attho vutto.

‘Covetousness and vexation’ is a nominative in the sense of the instrumental; thus he said: ‘with covetousness and with vexation.’ It is a change of case ending according to the meaning; thus he said: ‘by covetousness and vexation.’ ‘Of one who accumulates’ means of one who increases. ‘Mindful’ means recollected, endowed with mindfulness everywhere. In ‘of one who cultivates and also,’ the word ‘and’ and the word ‘also’ are mere particles; the meaning is stated simply as ‘of one who cultivates.’

Mālukyaputtasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Mālukyaputta Sutta is finished.

3. Parihānasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Discourse on Decline

96. Parihānasabhāvanti [M 2.0303] anavajjadhammehi parihāyanasabhāvaṃ. Abhibhavitānīti abhibhūtāni nibbisevanabhāvākārena. Sarasaṅkappāti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ visaye anavaṭṭhitabhāvena saṅkappā. Saṃyojaniyāti saṃyojetabbā. Saṃyojanānañhi punappunaṃ uppattiyā okāsaṃ dento kilesajātaṃ adhivāseti nāma. Kileso eva kilesajātaṃ. Ārammaṇaṃ pana citte karonto adhivāseti nāma. Chandarāgappahānena na pajahati ārammaṇaṃ, kilesaṃ pana anuppattidhammatāpādanena eva. Abhibhavitaṃ āyatananti kathitaṃ adhivāsanādinā. “Kathañca, bhikkhave, parihānadhammo hotī”ti dhammaṃ pucchitvā taṃ vibhajantena bhagavatā “tañce bhikkhu adhivāsetī”tiādinā puggalena puggalādhiṭṭhānena dhammo dassito.

96. ‘The nature of decline’ means the nature of declining from faultless qualities. ‘Overcome’ means being overpowered in a manner of non-cultivation. ‘Wandering thoughts’ means thoughts that are unstable in regard to various objects. ‘Fettering things’ means things that are to be fettered. For by repeatedly giving an opportunity for the arising of the fetters, one is said to endorse the mass of defilements. The mass of defilements is simply defilement. But when one takes an object in the mind, one is said to endorse it. One does not abandon the object by abandoning desire and lust, but one abandons the defilement only by bringing it to a state of non-arising. The ‘overcome sense base’ has been explained by way of endorsing and so on. Having asked about the Dhamma with the words, ‘And how, bhikkhus, is one of a nature to decline?’ the Blessed One, when analyzing it, showed the Dhamma from the standpoint of a person by means of a person, with the words beginning, ‘If that bhikkhu endorses it….’

Parihānasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourse on Decline is finished.

4. Pamādavihārīsuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Discourse on Dwelling Heedlessly

97. Na [V 2.0022] pidahitvā cakkhundriyaṃ na pidahitvā sañchāditvā ṭhitassa. Byāsiñcatīti kilesehi visesena āsiñcati. Kilesatintanti kilesehi avassutaṃ. Dubbalapīti taruṇā na balappattā. Balavapīti ubbegā pharaṇappattā ca pīti. Darathappassaddhīti kāyacittadarathavūpasamalakkhaṇā passaddhi. Na uppajjanti paccayaparamparāya asiddhattā. “Kathañca, bhikkhave, pamādavihārī hotī”tiādinā puggalaṃ pucchitvā “pāmojjaṃ na hoti, pāmojjaṃ jāyatī”tiādinā ca, dhammena “pamādavihārī appamādavihārī”ti ca puggalo dassito.

97. 'Not having guarded the eye faculty' means of one who abides without having guarded or covered it. 'It drenches' means it drenches him especially with defilements. 'Soaked with defilements' means inundated by defilements. 'Weak joy' means tender, not yet having attained strength. 'Strong joy' means joy that is uplifting and has attained pervasion. 'Tranquility from distress' means tranquility characterized by the calming of bodily and mental distress. 'They do not arise' because the sequence of conditions is not established. Having asked about the person with the words beginning, 'And how, bhikkhus, is one a heedless dweller?' and by means of the Dhamma with the words beginning, 'there is no gladness... gladness is born,' the person is shown as a 'heedless dweller' and a 'heedful dweller'.

Pamādavihārīsuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Discourse on Dwelling Heedlessly is concluded.

5. Saṃvarasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Discourse on Restraint

98. Idanti “kathañca, bhikkhave, asaṃvaro”ti? Idaṃ vacanaṃ. Pahātabbadhammakkhānavasenāti pahātabbadhammasseva kathanaṃ vuttaṃ. “Kathañca, bhikkhave, asaṃvaro [M 2.0304] hotī”ti dhammaṃ pucchitvā “santi, bhikkhave, cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā”tiādinā dhammova vibhatto.

98. 'This' means this statement: 'And how, bhikkhus, is there non-restraint?' 'By way of explaining the things to be abandoned' means that only the teaching about things to be abandoned is stated. Having asked about the Dhamma with the words, 'And how, bhikkhus, is there non-restraint?' the Dhamma itself is analyzed with the words beginning, 'There are, bhikkhus, forms cognizable by the eye....'

Saṃvarasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Discourse on Restraint is concluded.

6. Samādhisuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Discourse on Concentration

99. Cittekaggatāyāti samathavasena cittekaggatāya. Parihāyamāneti tassa alābhena parihāyamāne. Kammaṭṭhānanti vipassanākammaṭṭhānaṃ, samathameva vā.

99. 'Unification of mind' means unification of mind by way of serenity. 'Declining' means declining through not gaining that. 'Meditation subject' means the meditation subject of insight, or serenity itself.

7. Paṭisallānasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Discourse on Seclusion

100. Kammaṭṭhānanti samathavipassanākammaṭṭhānaṃ.

100. 'Meditation subject' means the meditation subject of serenity and insight.

8-9. Paṭhamanatumhākaṃsuttādivaṇṇanā

8-9. Commentary on the First Discourse on 'Not Yours,' etc.

101-102. Upamaṃ parivāretvāti upamaṃ pariharitvā. Suddhikavasenāti upamāya vinā kevalameva.

101-102. 'Having encompassed it with a simile' means having avoided the simile. 'By way of the bare text' means just by itself, without the simile.

10. Udakasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Discourse concerning Udaka

103. Udakoti [V 2.0023] tassa nāmaṃ. Vedaṃ ñāṇaṃ. Sabbaṃ jitavāti sabbaji. Avakhatanti nikhataṃ. Ucchādanadhammoti ucchādetabbasabhāvo. Parimaddanadhammoti parimadditabbasabhāvo. Parihatoti pariharito. Odanakummāsūpacayucchādanaparimaddanapadehīti vattabbaṃ. Ucchādanaṃ vā parimaddanamattamevāti katvā na gahitaṃ.

103. 'Udaka' was his name. 'Veda' means knowledge. 'Having conquered all' means the All-conqueror. 'Dug down' means excavated. 'Of a nature to be scoured away' means having a nature that should be scoured away. 'Of a nature to be rubbed out' means having a nature that should be rubbed out. 'Abandoned' means given up. It should be stated: 'by means of the accumulation, scouring, and rubbing away of rice and gruel.' Or, thinking it is just a matter of scouring and rubbing, it was not included.

Udakasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Discourse concerning Udaka is concluded.

Saḷavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter of the Sixes is concluded.

Dutiyo paṇṇāsako.

The Second Fifty.

11. Yogakkhemivaggo

11. The Chapter on Security from Bondage

1. Yogakkhemisuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on Security from Bondage

104. Catūhi [M 2.0305] yogehīti kāmayogādīhi catūhi yogehi. Kheminoti khemavato kusalino. Kāraṇabhūtanti kattabbaupāyassa kāraṇabhūtaṃ. Pariyāyati pavattiṃ nivattiñca ñāpetīti pariyāyo, dhammo ca so pariyattidhammattā pariyāyo cāti dhammapariyāyo, taṃ dhammapariyāyaṃ. Yasmā pana so tassādhigamassa kāraṇaṃ hoti, tasmā vuttaṃ “dhammakāraṇa”nti. Yuttinti samathavipassanādhammānīti vā catusaccadhammānīti vā. “Tasmā”ti padaṃ uddharitvā – “kasmā”ti kāraṇaṃ pucchanto “kiṃ akkhātattā, udāhu pahīnattā”ti vibhajitvā pucchi. Yasmā pana chandarāgappahānaṃ yogakkhemibhāvassa kāraṇaṃ, na kathanaṃ, tasmā “pahīnattā”tiādi vuttaṃ.

104. 'By four yokes' means by the four yokes beginning with the yoke of sensual pleasure. 'Secure' means of one who is secure, skillful. 'Being the cause' means being the cause of the means for what should be done. A 'teaching' (pariyāya) is so called because it makes known both continuation and cessation; and since it is the Dhamma to be mastered, it is a 'teaching of the Dhamma' (dhammapariyāya). And since it is the cause for the attainment of that, it is therefore called 'the cause in the Dhamma.' 'Principle' (yutti) means the principles of serenity and insight, or the principles of the Four Noble Truths. Omitting the word 'therefore,' he asks for the reason, dividing the question: 'Is it because it has been declared, or because it has been abandoned?' Since the abandoning of desire and lust is the cause for the state of security from bondage, not the declaration of it, it is therefore said, 'because it has been abandoned,' and so on.

Yogakkhemisuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourse on Security from Bondage is concluded.

2-10. Upādāyasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-10. Commentary on the Upādāya Sutta and so on.

105-113. Vedanāsukhadukkhanti vedanāsaṅkhātaṃ sukhañca dukkhañca kathitaṃ. “Ajjhattaṃ sukhaṃ dukkha”nti vuttattā vimuttisukhassa ca saḷāyatanadukkhassa ca kathitattā vivaṭṭasukhaṃ cettha kathitamevāti [V 2.0024] sakkā viññātuṃ. Kāmaṃ khandhiyavagge khandhavasena desanā āgatā, na āyatanavasena. Ettha pana vattabbaṃ atthajātaṃ khandhiyavagge vuttanayamevāti.

105-113. By 'pleasant and painful feeling' is meant what is designated as pleasant feeling and painful feeling. Since it is said, 'internal pleasure and pain,' and since the happiness of liberation and the suffering of the six sense bases have been spoken of, it can be understood that the happiness that transcends the round has also been spoken of here. It is true that in the Division on the Aggregates the teaching was presented in terms of the aggregates, not in terms of the sense bases. Here, however, the subject matter to be discussed is in the very same way as stated in the Division on the Aggregates.

Upādāyasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Upādāya Sutta and so on is concluded.

Yogakkhemivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Yogakkhemi Vagga is concluded.

12. Lokakāmaguṇavaggo

12. The Chapter on the World and Sensual Pleasures

1-2. Paṭhamamārapāsasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-2. Commentary on the First Sutta on Māra's Snare and so on.

114-115. Āvasati ettha kilesamāroti āvāso. Kāmaguṇaajjhattikabāhirāni āyatanāni. Kilesamārassa āvāsaṃ gato [M 2.0306] vasaṃ gato. Tividhassāti papañcasaññāsaṅkhātassa tividhassapi mārassa. Tato eva devaputtamārassapi vasaṃ gatoti sakkā viññātuṃ.

114-115. A 'dwelling' (āvāsa) is so called because Māra as the defilements (kilesamāra) dwells therein. The internal and external sense bases are the strands of sensual pleasure. One who has gone to the dwelling of Māra as the defilements has come under his control. By 'of the threefold' is meant of the threefold Māra designated as perceptions of proliferation. From this it can be understood that one has also come under the control of Māra the devaputta.

Paṭhamamārapāsasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the First Sutta on Māra's Snare and so on is concluded.

3. Lokantagamanasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Sutta on Going to the End of the World

116. Lokiyanti ettha sattakāyabhūtagāmādīti loko, cakkavāḷo. Saṅkhāro pana lujjanapalujjanaṭṭhena loko. Antanti osānaṃ. Sabbaññutaññāṇena saṃsanditvāti sabbaññutaññāṇagatiyā samānetvā avirodhetvā. Thomessāmīti pasaṃsissāmi.

116. The 'world' (loka) is so called because the host of beings, the collection of elements, and so on, are seen therein; it is the world-system. Formations, however, are 'world' in the sense of perishing and dissolving. 'End' means conclusion. 'Having compared it with the knowledge of omniscience' means having made it consistent with the course of the knowledge of omniscience, without contradiction. 'I will extol' means I will praise.

Evaṃsampattikanti evaṃsampajjanakaṃ evaṃpassitabbaṃ idaṃ mama ajjhesanaṃ. Tenāha “īdisanti attho”ti. Jānaṃ jānātīti sabbaññutaññāṇena jānitabbaṃ jānāti eva. Na hi padesaññāṇe ṭhito jānitabbaṃ sabbaṃ jānāti. Ukkaṭṭhaniddesena hi avisesaggahaṇena ca “jāna”nti iminā niravasesaṃ ñeyyajātaṃ pariggayhatīti tabbisayāya jānanakiriyāya sabbaññutaññāṇameva karaṇaṃ bhavituṃ yuttaṃ. Pakaraṇavasena “bhagavā”ti padasannidhānena ca ayamattho vibhāvetabbo. Passitabbameva passatīti dibbacakkhu-paññācakkhu-dhammacakkhu-buddhacakkhu-samantacakkhu-saṅkhātehi ñāṇacakkhūhi passitabbaṃ passati eva. Atha vā jānaṃ jānātīti yathā aññe [V 2.0025] savipallāsā kāmarūpapariññāvādino jānantāpi vipallāsavasena jānanti, na evaṃ bhagavā. Bhagavā pana pahīnavipallāsattā jānanto jānāti eva, diṭṭhidassanassa abhāvā passanto passati evāti attho.

By 'of such attainment' is meant: 'This request of mine is to be accomplished thus, to be seen thus.' Therefore he said: 'The meaning is “of such a kind.”' 'Knowing, he knows' means: he truly knows with the knowledge of omniscience what is to be known. For one established in partial knowledge does not know all that is to be known. For by the supreme designation and by the non-specific inclusion, this word 'knowing' comprehends the entire field of knowables without remainder; therefore, for the act of knowing that has this as its object, it is proper that the knowledge of omniscience alone should be the instrument. This meaning should be clarified by way of the context and by the proximity of the word 'Blessed One.' 'He sees only what is to be seen' means: he truly sees with the eyes of knowledge—designated as the divine eye, the wisdom eye, the Dhamma eye, the Buddha eye, and the universal eye—what is to be seen. Alternatively, 'knowing, he knows' means: whereas other proponents of the full understanding of the sense-sphere and form-sphere worlds, although they know, know with distortion, it is not so for the Blessed One. The Blessed One, on the other hand, because he has abandoned distortion, in knowing, truly knows; because of the absence of seeing views, in seeing, he truly sees. This is the meaning.

Dassanapariṇāyakaṭṭhenāti yathā cakkhu sattānaṃ dassanatthaṃ pariṇeti sādheti, evaṃ lokassa yāthāvadassanasādhanatopi dassanakiccapariṇāyakaṭṭhena cakkhubhūto, paññācakkhumayattā vā sayambhūñāṇena paññācakkhuṃ bhūto pattoti vā cakkhubhūto. Ñāṇabhūtoti etassa ca evameva attho daṭṭhabbo. Dhammā vā bodhipakkhiyā, tehi uppannattā lokassa ca taduppādanato anaññasādhāraṇaṃ vā dhammaṃ patto adhigatoti dhammabhūto. “Brahmā”vuccati seṭṭhaṭṭhena maggañāṇaṃ, tena uppannattā lokassa [M 2.0307] ca taduppādanato tañca sayambhūñāṇena pattoti brahmabhūto. Catusaccadhammaṃ vadatīti vattā. Ciraṃ saccapaṭivedhaṃ pavattento vadatīti pavattā. Atthaṃ nīharitvāti dukkhādiatthaṃ uddharitvā. Paramatthaṃ vā nibbānaṃ pāpayitā. Amatasacchikiriyaṃ sattesu uppādento amataṃ dadātīti amatassa dātā. Bodhipakkhiyadhammānaṃ tadāyattabhāvato dhammassāmī. Punappunaṃ yācāpento bhāriyaṃ karonto garuṃ karoti nāma, tathā duviññeyyaṃ katvā kathentopi.

By 'in the sense of being a guide for vision': just as the eye guides beings for the purpose of seeing, so too, because he is an instrument for the world's vision of things as they are, he has become the Eye in the sense of being a guide for the task of vision. Or, because he consists of the eye of wisdom, or because he has become one who has attained the eye of wisdom through self-originated knowledge, he has become the Eye. By 'become knowledge': the meaning of this should be understood in the same way. Or, the Dhamma consists of the qualities pertaining to enlightenment; because they have arisen from him and because he produces them for the world, or because he has attained and realized the Dhamma that is common to no other, he has become the Dhamma. 'Brahmā' is said of the path knowledge in the sense of 'best'; because it has arisen from him and because he produces it for the world, and because he has attained it with self-originated knowledge, he has become Brahmā. Because he speaks the Dhamma of the Four Truths, he is a speaker. Because he speaks while setting in motion for a long time the penetration of the truths, he is a proclaimer. 'Having brought out the meaning' means having extracted the meaning of suffering and so on. Or, he is one who makes others attain the ultimate goal, Nibbāna. Because he generates in beings the realization of the Deathless, he gives the Deathless; thus he is the Giver of the Deathless. Because the qualities pertaining to enlightenment are dependent on him, he is the Lord of the Dhamma. By making them request again and again, by making it a burden, he makes it revered; similarly, also by speaking after having made it difficult to understand.

Cakkhunā vijjamānena lokasaññī hoti, na tasmiṃ asati. Na hi ajjhattikāyatanavirahena lokasamaññā atthi. Tenāha “cakkhuñhi loko”tiādi. Appahīnadiṭṭhīti asamūhatasakkāyadiṭṭhiko ghanavinibbhogaṃ kātuṃ asakkonto samudāyaṃ viya avayavaṃ “loko”ti sañjānāti ceva maññati ca. Tathāti iminā “lokoti sañjānāti ceva maññati cā”ti padattayaṃ ākaḍḍhati. Tassāti puthujjanassa, cakkavāḷalokassa vā. Cakkhādimeva hi sahokāsena “cakkavāḷo”ti puthujjano sañjānāti. Gamanenāti padasā gamanena. Na sakkā tesaṃ anantattā. Lujjanaṭṭhenāti abhisaṅkhāralokavasena lokassa antaṃ dassetuṃ vuttaṃ. Tassa anto nāma nibbānaṃ. Taṃ pattuṃ sakkā sammāpaṭipattiyā pattabbattā. Cakkavāḷalokassa pana anto nāma, natthi tassa gamanena appattabbattā.

When the eye exists, one has perception of the world; not when it is absent. For without the internal sense base, there is no designation 'world.' Hence it is said: 'The eye, indeed, is the world,' and so on. 'One with unabandoned view' means one whose identity view is uneradicated, who is unable to analyze the compact mass, and who perceives and conceives the aggregate as 'the world.' 'Thus' draws in the three words 'perceives and conceives it as “world.”' 'Of that' means of the worldling, or of the world-system. For the worldling perceives the eye and so on, together with the space it occupies, as the 'world-system.' 'By going' means by going on foot. It is not possible because of their infinitude. 'In the sense of perishing' is said to show the end of the world by way of the world of formations. Its end is Nibbāna. That can be reached, since it is attainable by right practice. But as for the end of the world-system, there is none, since it is not to be reached by going.

Imehi padehīti imehi vākyavibhāgehi padehi. Tāni pana akkharasamudāyalakkhaṇānīti āha “akkharasampiṇḍanehī”ti. Pāṭiyekkaakkharehīti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ pade paṭiniyatasannivesehi visuṃ visuṃ cittena gayhamānehi akkharehīti attho.

'By these words' means by these divisions of speech, by these words. And since those are characterized by being a collection of letters, it is said: 'by the collections of letters.' 'By each individual letter' means by letters individually apprehended by the mind, in the specific arrangement fixed in each word. This is the meaning.

Gamanaṭṭhena “paṇḍā” vuccati paññā, tāya ito gato pattoti paṇḍito, paññavā. Mahāpaññatā [V 2.0026] nāma paṭisambhidāvasena veditabbāti āha “mahante atthe”tiādi. Yathā tanti ettha tanti nipātamattaṃ paṭhamavikappe, dutiyavikappe pana paccāmasananti āha “taṃ byākata”nti.

In the sense of 'going,' wisdom is called 'paṇḍā'; because by means of it one has gone from here and attained, one is a 'paṇḍita,' a wise person. 'Great wisdom' is to be understood by way of the analytical knowledges, as it is said, 'in great matters,' and so on. Just as 'tanti' here is merely a particle in the first alternative, but in the second alternative it is a reference, so it is said, 'that is explained.'

Lokantagamanasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Sutta on Going to the End of the World is concluded.

4. Kāmaguṇasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Sutta on Sensual Pleasures

117. Cetasoti [M 2.0308] karaṇe sāmivacanaṃ. Cittena saṃphusanaṃ nāma anubhavoti āha “cittena anubhūtapubbā”ti. Ututtayānurūpatāvasena pāsādattayaṃ, taṃ vasena tividhanāṭakabhedo. Manorammatāmattena kāmaguṇaṃ katvā dassitaṃ, na kāmavasena. Na hi abhinikkhamanato uddhaṃ bodhisattassa kāmavitakkā bhūtapubbā. Tenāha māro pāpimā –

117. The word `cetasā` is in the instrumental sense. 'Contact with the mind' means experience; thus it is said: 'previously experienced by the mind.' In conformity with the three seasons there were three palaces, and on account of them a threefold division of entertainment. This was presented as a sensual pleasure merely because of its delightfulness, not out of sensual desire. For after his renunciation, sensual thoughts never arose for the Bodhisatta. Therefore Māra the Evil One said:

“Satta vassāni bhagavantaṃ, anubandhiṃ padāpadaṃ;

Otāraṃ nādhigacchissaṃ, sambuddhassa satīmato”ti. (su. ni. 448);

For seven years I followed the Blessed One step by step, but I found no opening in the mindful, Perfectly Enlightened One.

Metteyyo nāmātiādi anāgatārammaṇadassanamattaṃ, na bodhisattassa evaṃ uppajjatīti. Attā piyāyitabbarūpo etassāti attarūpo, uttarapade purimapadalopenāti “attano hitakāmajātikenā”ti attho vutto. Attarūpenāti vā pītisomanassehi gahitasabhāvena tuṭṭhapahaṭṭhena udaggudaggena. Appamādoti appamajjanaṃ kusaladhammesu akhaṇḍakāritāti āha “sātaccakiriyā”ti. Avossaggoti cittassa kāmaguṇesu avossajjanaṃ pakkhandituṃ appadānaṃ. Purimo vikappo kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ karaṇavasena dassito, pacchimo akusalānaṃ akaraṇavasena. Dve dhammāti appamādo satīti dve dhammā. Appamādo sati ca tathā pavattā cattāro kusaladhammakkhandhā veditabbā. Kattabbāti pavattetabbā.

The words 'the one named Metteyya,' etc., refer merely to a vision with a future object; it is not that a Bodhisatta arises in this way. He is `attarūpo` because the self is of a nature to be cherished by him; the meaning 'by one whose nature is to desire his own welfare' is stated with the elision of the first word in the latter part of the compound. Alternatively, `attarūpena` means with a nature seized by rapture and joy, pleased and delighted, utterly uplifted. `Appamādo` (heedfulness) is non-negligence, unbroken application regarding wholesome states; thus it is called 'constant activity.' `Avossaggo` (non-relinquishing) is the non-relinquishing of the mind to sensual pleasures, not giving it leave to rush into them. The first alternative is shown in terms of performing wholesome states, the second in terms of not performing unwholesome ones. 'Two states' means heedfulness and mindfulness—these two states. Heedfulness and mindfulness, when they have thus occurred, should be understood as the four aggregates of wholesome states. `Kattabbā` means 'should be developed.'

Tasmiṃ āyataneti tasmiṃ nibbānasaññite kāraṇe paṭivedhe. Taṃ kāraṇanti channaṃ āyatanānaṃ kāraṇaṃ. Saḷāyatanaṃ nirujjhati etthāti saḷāyatananirodho vuccati nibbānaṃ. Tenāha “nibbānaṃ. Taṃ sandhāyā”tiādi. Nibbānasminti nibbānamhi.

`In that basis` means: in that cause, in the penetration designated as Nibbāna. The sixfold sense base ceases here, thus Nibbāna is called the cessation of the sixfold sense base. Therefore it is said: 'Nibbāna, with reference to that,' and so on. `In Nibbāna` means in Nibbāna.

Kāmaguṇasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sutta on Sensual Pleasures is finished.

5-6. Sakkapañhasuttādivaṇṇanā

5-6. Commentary on the Sakkapañha Sutta, etc.

118-119. Diṭṭheti [V 2.0027] paccakkhabhūte. Dhammeti upādānakkhandhadhamme. Tattha hi attāti bhavati saññā diṭṭhi cāti attabhāvasaññā. Tenāha – “diṭṭheva [M 2.0309] dhammeti imasmiṃyeva attabhāve”ti. “Tannissita”nti ettha taṃ-saddena heṭṭhā abhinandanādipariyāyena vuttā taṇhā paccāmaṭṭhāti āha – “taṇhānissita”nti. Taṃ upādānaṃ etassāti tadupādānaṃ. Tenāha – “taṃgahaṇa”ntiādi. Taṇhupādānasaṅkhātaṃ gahaṇaṃ etassāti taṃgahaṇaṃ. Chaṭṭhaṃ uttānameva pañcame vuttanayattā.

118-119. `Diṭṭhe` means in what is directly perceived. `Dhamme` means in the states that are the aggregates subject to clinging. For in them there arise the perception and the view 'self,' that is, the perception of a self-entity. Therefore he says: '`diṭṭhe'va dhamme` means in this very self-entity.' Here in the word `tannissitaṃ`, by the pronoun `taṃ`, craving—spoken of below with terms such as 'delighting'—is referred to; thus he says `taṇhānissitaṃ`. That clinging belongs to it, hence `tadupādānaṃ`. Therefore he says: `taṃgahaṇaṃ` (that grasping), etc. The grasping designated as craving and clinging belongs to it, hence `taṃgahaṇaṃ`. The sixth is clear because of the method stated in the fifth.

Sakkapañhasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sakkapañha Sutta, etc., is finished.

7. Sāriputtasaddhivihārikasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Sutta on Sāriputta's Co-resident

120. Ghaṭessatīti pubbenāparaṃ ghaṭitaṃ sambandhaṃ karissati. Vicchedanti brahmacariyassa virodhipaccayasamuppattiyā ucchedaṃ.

120. `Ghaṭessati` means: it will connect the later with the earlier, making a bond. `Vicchedaṃ` means: the cutting off of the holy life through the arising of opposing conditions.

8. Rāhulovādasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Sutta of Advice to Rāhula

121. Ye dhammā sammadeva bhāvitā bahulīkatā vimuttiyā arahattassa sacchikiriyāya saṃvattanti, te saddhādayo sambhārā vimuttiparipācaniyāti adhippetā. Paripācentīti paripākaṃ pariṇāmaṃ gamenti. Dhammāti kāraṇabhūtā dhammā. Visuddhikāraṇavasenāti visuddhikāraṇatāvasena, sā pana saddhindriyādīnaṃ kāraṇato visuddhi. Yathā nāma jātisampannassa khattiyakumārassa vipakkhavigamena pakkhasaṅgahena pavattiṭṭhānasampattiyā ca parisuddhi hoti, evamevaṃ daṭṭhabbāti dassento “vuttaṃ heta”ntiādimāha.

121. Those states that, when rightly developed and cultivated, conduce to liberation, to the realization of arahantship, are meant as the requisites beginning with faith that bring liberation to maturity. `Paripācenti` (they bring to maturity) means they bring to ripeness, to fruition. `Dhammā` means causal states. 'By way of being the cause of purity' means because of being the cause of purity; and that is the purity that results from the faculties of faith and so on as its cause. Just as a well-born khattiya youth becomes pure through the departure of his rivals, the gathering of his supporters, and the attainment of a secure position, so it should be understood here. Showing this, he says: 'For this has been said,' etc.

Assaddhādayopi puggalā saddhādīnaṃ yāvadeva parihānāya honti, saddhādayo pāripūriyāva, tathā pasādaniyasuttantādipaccavekkhaṇā, pasādaniyasuttantā nāma sampasādanīyasuttādayo. Sammappadhāneti sammappadhānasuttante. Satipaṭṭhāneti cattāro satipaṭṭhāne. Jhānavimokkheti jhānāni ceva vimokkhe ca uddissa pavattasuttante. Gambhīrañāṇacariyeti khandhāyatanadhātupaṭiccasamuppādapaṭisaṃyuttasuttante.

Persons lacking faith and so on lead only to the decline of faith and other such qualities, while those with faith lead to their fulfillment. Likewise, reflection on inspiring suttas, etc. Inspiring suttas are suttas like the Sampasādanīya Sutta. `Sammappadhāne` refers to the suttas on the Right Exertions. `Satipaṭṭhāne` refers to the four establishments of mindfulness. `Jhānavimokkhe` refers to the suttas concerning the jhānas and liberations. `Gambhīrañāṇacariya` refers to the suttas connected with the aggregates, sense bases, elements, and dependent origination.

Kalyāṇamittatādayoti kalyāṇamittatā sīlasaṃvaro abhisallekhakathā vīriyārambho nibbedhikapaññāti [V 2.0028] ime kalyāṇamittādayo pañca dhammā, ye “idha, meghiya, bhikkhu kalyāṇamitto hotī”tiādinā udāne [M 2.0310] (udā. 31) kathitā. Lokaṃ volokentassāti āyasmato rāhulassa tāsañca devatānaṃ indriyaparipākaṃ passantassa. Tato yena andhavanaṃ, tattha divāvihārāya mahāsamāgamo bhavissatīti. Tathā hi vakkhati “ettakāti gaṇanāvasena paricchedo natthī”ti.

`Kalyāṇamittatādayo` (good friendship and so on) refers to these five states: good friendship, virtuous restraint, talk on effacement, arousal of energy, and penetrative wisdom. These five states beginning with good friendship are spoken of in the Udāna (Ud 4.1): 'Here, Meghiya, a bhikkhu has good friends,' etc. `Lokaṃ volokentassa` (surveying the world) means: seeing the ripening of the faculties of the Venerable Rāhula and of those devas. Then, where the Blind Men's Grove is, there will be a great gathering for the day's abiding. For it will be said: 'There is no limit by way of counting.'

Rukkhapabbatanissitā bhūmaṭṭhakā, ākāsacārivimānavāsino antalikkhaṭṭhakā. Dhammacakkhunti veditabbāni catusaccadhammānaṃ dassanaṭṭhena.

Those depending on trees and mountains are earth-dwelling deities; those dwelling in mansions that travel in the sky are deities of the intermediate space. The 'Dhamma eye' should be understood as the vision of the dhammas of the Four Noble Truths.

Rāhulovādasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sutta of Advice to Rāhula is finished.

Lokakāmaguṇavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on the World and Sensual Pleasures is finished.

13. Gahapativaggo

13. The Chapter on the Householder

1-3. Vesālīsuttādivaṇṇanā

1–3. Commentary on the Vesālī Sutta and Others

124-126. Dvīsūti imasmiṃ gahapativagge paṭhamadutiyesu tatiye ca vuttatthameva pāṭhajātaṃ apubbaṃ natthīti attho.

124–126. In this Chapter on the Householder, the text of the first, second, and third suttas is simply what has been stated in meaning; there is nothing new. This is the meaning.

4-5. Bhāradvājasuttādivaṇṇanā

4–5. Commentary on the Bhāradvāja Sutta and Others

127-128. Kāmaṃ aññepi pabbajitā yuttakāle piṇḍaṃ ulamānā carantiyeva, ayaṃ pana odarito, teneva kāraṇena pabbajitoti dassento “piṇḍaṃ ulamāno”tiādimāha. Ghaṃsantovāti bhūmiyaṃ ghaṃsanto eva pattaṃ ṭhapeti. Parikkhīṇanti samantato parikkhīṇaṃ. Nāḷiko …pe… jātaṃ, atirekapattaṭṭhapanassa paṭikkhittattā aññaṃ na gaṇhāti. “Indriyabhāvananti cakkhādipañcindriyabhāvana”nti keci vadanti, tathā vipassanābhinivesaṃ katvā uparivipassanaṃ vaḍḍhitvāti adhippāyo. Apare pana “saddhāpañcamānaṃ indriyānaṃ vasena vipassanābhinivesaṃ katvā tena sukhena abhiññāpahānānaṃ sampādanavasena indriyaṃ bhāvetvā”ti vadanti.

127–128. Indeed, other mendicants also wander for alms at the proper time, but this one is a glutton. To show that he went forth for this very reason, the text says, 'seeking alms,' and so on. `Ghaṃsanto` means: he places the bowl on the ground while scraping it. `Parikkhīṇaṃ` means: completely depleted. A reed ...pe... has grown: because keeping an extra bowl is forbidden, he does not take another. Some say: 'Development of the faculties means the development of the five faculties beginning with the eye'; the intention is that, having applied himself to insight, one develops higher insight. Others, however, say: 'Having applied himself to insight by means of the five faculties with faith as the fifth, one develops the faculties by accomplishing, through that happiness, the abandonments by means of direct knowledge.'

Upasaṅkamatīti [M 2.0311][V 2.0029] ettha yathā so rājā upasaṅkami, taṃ āgamanato paṭṭhāya dassetuṃ “thero kirā”tiādi āraddhaṃ. Mahāpānaṃ nāma aññaṃ kammaṃ akatvā pānapasuto hutvā sattāhaṃ tadanurūpaparijanassa surāpivanaṃ. Tenāha “mahāpānaṃ nāma pivitvā”ti. Sālithusehīti rattasālithusehi. Ḍayhamānaṃ viya kipillikadaṃsanajātāhi dukkhavedanāhi. Mukhasattīhi vijjhiṃsu vallabhatāya. Itthilolo hi so rājā.

`Upasaṅkamati` (approaches): Here, to show how that king approached, the passage beginning, 'The elder, it is said...' was started in order to show this from the moment of his arrival. The 'great drinking' is the drinking of liquor for seven days, being devoted to drinking without doing any other work, along with a retinue suitable for that. Therefore it is said, 'having engaged in the great drinking.' `Sālithusehi` (with rice husks) means: with red rice husks. As if being burnt: by the painful feelings that arose from ant bites. They pierced him with mouth-spears out of affection, for that king was wanton in regard to women.

Paveṇinti tesaṃ samādānapaveṇiṃ brahmacariyapabandhaṃ. Paṭipādentīti sampādenti. Garukārammaṇanti garukātabbaārammaṇaṃ, avītikkamitabbārammaṇanti attho. Assāti rañño. Cittaṃ anotarantanti pasādavīthiṃ anotarantaṃ anupagacchantaṃ. Viheṭhetunti vibādhituṃ.

`Paveṇiṃ` (tradition) means: their tradition of undertaking, the continuity of the holy life. `Paṭipādenti` (they maintain) means: they accomplish. `Garukārammaṇaṃ` (a weighty object) means: an object to be respected, an object not to be transgressed; this is the meaning. `Assa` (his) means: the king's. `Cittaṃ anotarantanti` (not entering the mind) means: not entering, not reaching the path of confidence. `Viheṭhetuṃ` (to harass) means: to oppress.

Lobhassa aparāparuppattiyā bahuvacanavasena “lobhadhammā”ti vuttaṃ. Uppajjantītipi attho yeva, yasmā uppajjamāno lobhadhammo attano hetupaccaye pariggahāpento jānāpento viya sahati pavattatīti. Imameva kāyanti ettha samūhatthe eva kāya-saddo gabbhāsayādiṭṭhānesu uppajjanadhammasamūhavisayattā, itare pana kāyūpalakkhitatāya “kāyo”ti veditabbā. Uttānameva heṭṭhā vuttanayattā.

Because of the successive arising of greed, the plural 'states of greed' is used. The meaning is simply 'they arise,' because when a state of greed is arising, it proceeds by overpowering, as if making known and grasping its own causes and conditions. `Imameva kāyaṃ` (this very body): Here the word 'body' has the meaning of a collection, as it pertains to the collection of phenomena liable to arise in such places as the womb. The others, however, should be understood as 'body' because they are characterized by the body. The meaning is plain because of the method stated below.

Bhāradvājasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Bhāradvāja Sutta and others is concluded.

6. Ghositasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Ghosita Sutta

129. Rūpā ca manāpāti nīlādibhedā rūpadhammā ca manasā manuññā piyarūpā saṃvijjanti, idañca sukhavedanīyassa phassassa sabhāvadassanatthaṃ. Evaṃ “rūpā ca manāpā upekkhāvedaniyā”ti etthāpi yathārahaṃ vattabbaṃ. Cakkhuviññāṇa…pe… phassanti vuttaṃ. Upanissayakoṭiyā hi cakkhuviññāṇasampayuttaphasso sukhavedanīyo, na sahajātakoṭiyā. Tenāha – “ekaṃ phassaṃ paṭicca javanavasena sukhavedanā uppajjatī”ti. Sesapadesūti “saṃvijjati kho, gahapati, sotadhātū”ti āgatesu pañcasu koṭṭhāsesu.

129. `Rūpā ca manāpā` (and agreeable forms) means: there exist forms of different kinds such as blue, etc., which are pleasing to the mind, lovely. This is for the purpose of showing the nature of contact that is to be felt as pleasant. Similarly, in the case of 'and agreeable forms to be felt with equanimity,' this should be stated as appropriate. Eye-consciousness ...pe... contact is said. For contact associated with eye-consciousness is to be felt as pleasant from the standpoint of decisive support condition, not from the standpoint of conascence condition. Hence it is said: 'Dependent on one contact, a pleasant feeling arises by way of impulsion.' `Sesapadesu` (in the remaining passages) means: in the five sections that come in the passage, 'There exists, householder, the ear-element...'

Tevīsati [M 2.0312] dhātuyo kathitā channaṃ dvārānaṃ vasena vibhajjagahaṇena. Vatthunissitanti hadayavatthunissitaṃ. Pañcadvāre vīsati, manodvāre tisso evaṃ tevīsati.

Twenty-three elements are stated by way of a distributive grasp based on the six doors. `Vatthunissitaṃ` (based on a physical basis) means: based on the heart-basis. Twenty are in the five doors, three in the mind-door; thus twenty-three.

Ghositasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Ghosita Sutta is concluded.

7-8. Hāliddikānisuttādivaṇṇanā

7–8. Commentary on the Hāliddikāni Sutta and Others

130-131. Taṃ [V 2.0030] itthetanti cakkhunā yaṃ rūpaṃ diṭṭhaṃ, taṃ itthanti attho. Taṃ sukhavedaniyanti taṃ sukhavedanāya upanissayakoṭiyā paccayabhūtaṃ cakkhuviññāṇañceva, yo ca yathārahaṃ upanissayakoṭiyā vā, anantaro ce anantarakoṭiyā vā, sahajāto ce sampayuttakoṭiyā vā, sukhavedanāya paccayo phasso. Taṃ sukhavedaniyañca phassaṃ paṭicca uppajjati sukhavedanāti yojanā. Esa nayo sabbattha sabbesu sesesu sattasu vāresu. Manodhātuyeva vā samānāti abhidhammanayena. Suttantanayena pana suññataṭṭhena nissattanijjīvaṭṭhena ca manodhātusamaññaṃ labhateva. Aṭṭhamaṃ uttānameva heṭṭhā vuttanayattā.

130–131. `Taṃ itthaṃ` (that which is desirable) means: that form which is seen with the eye is desirable; this is the meaning. `Taṃ sukhavedaniyaṃ` (that which is to be felt as pleasant) refers to both eye-consciousness, which has become a condition for pleasant feeling from the standpoint of decisive support, and contact, which is a condition for pleasant feeling as appropriate—from the standpoint of decisive support, or if immediate, from the standpoint of proximity, or if co-nascent, from the standpoint of association. The construction is: 'Dependent on that contact which is to be felt as pleasant, a pleasant feeling arises.' This is the method everywhere in all the remaining seven cases. Or, according to the Abhidhamma method, it is the same as the mind-element. But according to the Suttanta method, it certainly obtains the designation 'mind-element' in the sense of emptiness and in the sense of being without a soul or life. The eighth is plain because of the method stated above.

Hāliddikānisuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Hāliddikāni Sutta and others is concluded.

9. Lohiccasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Lohicca Sutta

132. Tepi māṇavakātveva vuttā, na brāhmaṇakumārā eva. Seleyyakānīti aññamaññaṃ silissanalaṅghanakīḷanāni.

132. They too were called youths, not just brahmin youths. `Seleyyakāni` refers to games of mutually embracing and jumping over.

Upaṭṭhānavasena ibhaṃ harantīti ibbhā, hatthigopakā. Te pana nihīnakuṭumbassa bhoggaṃ upādāya gahapatibhāvaṃ upādāya “gahapatikā”tipi vuccantīti āha “gahapatikā”ti. Kaṇhāti kaṇhābhijātikā. Raṭṭhaṃ bharantīti yasmiṃ raṭṭhe vasanti, tassa raṭṭhassa baliṃ bharaṇena, attano vā kuṭumbassa bharaṇena bharatā. Pariyāyantāti parito saṃcarantā kīḷanti.

'Ibbhā' are elephant-keepers (hatthigopakā), so called because they handle elephants (ibhaṃ) by way of service (upaṭṭhānavasena). Although they are from lowly families, since they acquire wealth and the status of householders, they are also called 'householders' (gahapatikā). 'Kaṇhā' means of dark birth. 'Raṭṭhaṃ bharanti' means they are 'bharatā,' supporters, because they support the country in which they live by paying its taxes, or they support their own families. 'Pariyāyanti' means they wander about and play.

Sīlajeṭṭhakāti sīlappadhānā. Ye purāṇaṃ saranti, te sīluttamā ahesuṃ. Dvārāni cakkhādidvārāni.

'Foremost in virtue' means pre-eminent in virtue. Those who remembered the ancient ways were supreme in virtue. 'Doors' means the sense doors, such as the eye and so on.

Apakkamitvā [M 2.0313] apetā virahitā hutvā. Visamānīti vigatasamāni duccaritasabhāvāni. Nānāvidhadaṇḍā nānāvidhadaṇḍanipātā.

Having departed: having gone away, having become separated. 'Uneven things' (visamāni) means things devoid of evenness, having the nature of misconduct. 'Various punishments' means various inflictions of punishment.

Anāhārakāti kiñci abhuñjanakā. Paṅko viya paṅko, malaṃ. Dantapaṅko purimapadalopena paṅkoti vuttoti “paṅko nāma dantamala”nti vuttaṃ. Ajehi kātabbakānaṃ akopetvā karaṇaṃ samādānavasena vataṃ. Esa nayo sesesupi. Kohaññaṃ nāma attani vijjamānadosaṃ paṭicchādetvā [V 2.0031] asantaguṇapakāsanāti āha – “paṭicchanna…pe… kohaññañcevā”ti. Parikkhārabhaṇḍakavaṇṇāti parikkhārabhaṇḍā kappakāti. Te ca kho attano jīvikatthāya āmisakiñjakkhassa attanibandhanatthāya amocanatthāya katā.

'Without food' means not eating anything. Like mud, it is mud, filth. Because 'dantapaṅko' (tooth-mud) is called 'paṅko' by eliding the first word, it is said: '“paṅko” means tooth-filth.' The goat-vow is the undertaking to do what is done by goats without getting angry. This is the method in the other cases as well. 'Hypocrisy' (kohaññaṃ) is concealing one's existing faults and proclaiming non-existent virtues; thus it is said: 'concealed...pe...and hypocrisy.' 'The description of requisites and articles' means allowable requisites and articles. And these are made for the sake of one's own livelihood, for the purpose of binding oneself to the slightest material gain, for the purpose of non-liberation.

Akhilanti cetokhilarahitaṃ brahmavihāravasena. Tenāha “mudu athaddha”nti.

'Without barrenness' (akhilaṃ) means devoid of mental barrenness by means of the divine abodes. Therefore it is said: 'soft and not stiff.'

Adhimuttoti abhirativasena yuttapayutto. Parittacittoti parito khaṇḍitacitto. Appamāṇacittoti ettha “ko aya”nti paṭikkhituṃ sakkuṇeyyacitto.

'Resolved upon' (adhimutto) means yoked and engaged by way of delight. 'Of limited mind' (parittacitto) means one whose mind is fragmented all around. 'Of immeasurable mind' (appamāṇacitto) here means a mind that is able to reject the thought, 'Who is this?'

Lohiccasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Lohicca Sutta is finished.

10. Verahaccānisuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Verahaccāni Sutta

133. Brāhmaṇiṃ dhammasavanāya codento māṇavako “yagghe”ti avoca. Tenāha “yaggheti codanatthe nipāto”ti.

133. The youth, urging the brahmin woman to listen to the Dhamma, said, 'Yagghe.' Therefore it is said: 'Yagghe is a particle with the sense of urging.'

Verahaccānisuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Verahaccāni Sutta is finished.

Gahapativaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Householder Chapter is finished.

14. Devadahavaggo

14. The Devadaha Chapter

1. Devadahasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Devadaha Sutta

134. Manaṃ ramayantāti āpāthagatā manassa ramaṇavasena piyāyitabbatāvasena pavattantā.

134. 'Delighting the mind': when they come into range, they proceed in such a way as to be cherished because they delight the mind.

2. Khaṇasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Khaṇa Sutta

135. Chaphassāyatanikāti [M 2.0314] chahi phassāyatanehi aniṭṭhasaṃvedaniyā. Chadvāraphassapaṭiviññattīti chahipi dvārehi ārammaṇassa paṭisaṃvedanā hotiyeva sabbaso dukkhānubhavanatthaṃ. Tāvatiṃsapuranti [V 2.0032] sudassanamahānagaraṃ. Abhāvo nāma natthi sabbathā sukhānubhavanato. Nirayeti iminā duggati bhavasāmaññena itarāpāyāpi gahitā eva. Maggabrahmacariyavāsaṃ vasituṃ na sakkāti iminā pana sabbesampi acchindikaṭṭhānānaṃ gahaṇaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Imevāti imasmiṃ manussaloke eva. Apāyopi paññāyati apāyadukkhasadisassa dukkhassa kadāci paṭisaṃvedanato. Saggopi paññāyati devabhogasadisasampattiyā kadāci paṭilabhitabbato.

135. 'Relating to the six sense bases' means what is to be felt as undesirable through the six sense bases. 'The experiencing of contact at the six doors' means that the experiencing of an object through all six doors occurs entirely for the purpose of experiencing suffering. 'The city of the Tāvatiṃsa gods' means the great city Sudassana. 'Absence' means there is none, because there is no experiencing of happiness at all. 'In hell': by this word, because of the general nature of a woeful state, the other planes of woe are also included. 'One cannot live the holy life of the path': by this, the inclusion of all states that offer no opportunity should be understood. 'Here itself' means only in this human world. 'A plane of woe is also discerned' because suffering similar to the suffering of the planes of woe is sometimes experienced. 'Heaven is also discerned' because prosperity similar to the enjoyments of the gods is sometimes to be obtained.

Ayaṃ kammabhūmīti ayaṃ manussaloko purisathāmakaraṇāya kammabhūmi nāma tāsaṃ yogyaṭṭhānabhāvato. Tattha padhānakammaṃ dassento “idha maggabhāvanā”ti āha. Ṭhānānīti kāraṇāni. Saṃvejaniyānīti saṃvegajananāni bahūni jātiādīni. Tathā hi jāti, jarā, byādhi, maraṇaṃ, apāyabhavaṃ, tatthapi nirayūpapattihetukaṃ, tiracchānupapattihetukaṃ, asūrakāyūpapattihetukaṃ, atīte vaṭṭamūlakaṃ, anāgate vaṭṭamūlakaṃ, paccuppanne āhārapariyeṭṭhimūlakanti bahūni saṃvegavatthūni paccavekkhitvā saṃvegajāto sañjātasaṃvego yoniso padhānamanuyuñjassu. Saṃvegāti saṃvegamāpajjassu.

'This is the field of action': this human world is called the field of action because it is a suitable place for making a man's effort. There, showing the principal action, it says, 'Here there is the development of the path.' 'Occasions' means causes. 'Inspiring urgency' means the many things that generate a sense of urgency, such as birth and so on. For there are many grounds for urgency to reflect upon: birth, aging, sickness, death, existence in the plane of woe; and within that, what is caused by rebirth in hell, rebirth as an animal, or rebirth in the realm of asuras; what is rooted in the past round of existence, what is rooted in the future round, and what is rooted in the present search for food. Having reflected on these, with a sense of urgency arisen, one should wisely apply oneself to the striving. 'Be stirred' means one should acquire a sense of urgency.

Khaṇasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Khaṇa Sutta is finished.

3. Paṭhamarūpārāmasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the First Sutta on Delight in Forms

136. Sammuditā sammodappattā, pamoditā sañjātapamodā. Dukkhāti dukkhavanto sañjātadukkhā. Tenāha “dukkhitā”ti. Sukhaṃ etassa atthīti sukho, sukhī. Tenāha “sukhito”ti. Yattakā rūpādayo dhammā loke atthīti vuccati. Passantānanti saccapaṭivedhena sammadeva passantānaṃ. “Paccanīkaṃ hotī”ti vatvā taṃ paccanīkabhāvaṃ dassetuṃ “loko hī”tiādi [M 2.0315] vuttaṃ. Asubhāti “āhū”tipadaṃ ānetvā sambandho. Sabbametanti “sukhaṃ diṭṭhamariyebhi…pe… tadariyā sukhato vidū”ti ca vuttaṃ. Sabbametaṃ nibbānameva sandhāya vuttaṃ. Nibbānameva hi ekantato sukhaṃ nāma.

136. 'Delighted' means having attained delight; 'joyful' means with joy arisen. 'Suffering' means possessing suffering, with suffering arisen; therefore it is said, 'afflicted.' 'Happiness exists for this one,' thus 'sukho,' happy (sukhī); therefore it is said, 'happy.' It is said that whatever phenomena such as form and so on exist in the world... 'Of those who see': of those who see rightly through the penetration of the truth. Having said, 'It is contrary,' in order to show that state of contrariness, the passage beginning 'for the world...' was stated. 'As unlovely': the connection is made by supplying the word 'they say.' All this—that is, the passage, 'what is seen by the noble ones as happiness... that the noble ones know as happiness'—was spoken with reference to Nibbāna alone. For Nibbāna alone is exclusively called happiness.

Pañcanavutipāsaṇḍino tesañca pāsaṇḍibhāvo papañcasūdaniṭṭhakathāyaṃ pakāsito eva. Kilesanīvaraṇena nivutānanti kilesakhandhā kilesanīvaraṇaṃ, tena nivāritānaṃ. Nibbānadassanaṃ nāma ariyamaggo, tena tassa paṭivijjhanañca kāḷameghaavacchāditaṃ viya candamaṇḍalaṃ.

The ninety-five sectarians and their sectarian nature have been explained in the Papañcasūdanī, the commentary on the Majjhima Nikāya. 'Of those hindered by the hindrance of defilements': the aggregate of defilements is the hindrance of defilements; of those obstructed by it. The vision of Nibbāna is the noble path, and its penetration by that path is like the disk of the moon covered by a black cloud.

Paricchinditvāti [V 2.0033] asubhabhāvaparicchindanena sammāviññāṇadassanena ca paricchinditvā. Maggadhammassāti ariyamaggadhammassa.

'Having determined': having determined by the demarcation of the state of unloveliness and by the vision of right understanding. 'Of the Dhamma of the path' means of the Dhamma of the noble path.

Anupannehīti anu anu avihāya paṭipannehi. Ko nu añño jānituṃ arahati, añño na jānātīti dasseti.

'By those who have followed': by those who have practiced following step-by-step without abandoning the path. It shows: 'Who else is fit to know? Another does not know.'

Paṭhamarūpārāmasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the First Sutta on Delight in Forms is finished.

4-12. Dutiyarūpārāmasuttādivaṇṇanā

4-12. Commentary on the Second Discourse on Delight in Forms, etc.

137-145. Suddhikaṃ katvā gāthābandhanena vinā kevalaṃ cuṇṇiyapadavaseneva. Tathā tathāti ajjhattikāni bāhirāni ca āyatanāni aniccalakkhaṇena dukkhānattalakkhaṇehi ca yojetvā dassanavasena.

137-145. Purely by way of prose passages, without metrical composition. 'Thus and thus' means connecting the internal and external sense bases with the characteristic of impermanence and with the characteristics of suffering and not-self, by way of seeing.

Dutiyarūpārāmasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Second Discourse on Delight in Forms, etc., is concluded.

Devadahavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Devadaha Chapter is concluded.

15. Navapurāṇavaggo

15. The Chapter on the New and the Old

1. Kammanirodhasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on the Cessation of Kamma

146. Sampati vijjamānassa cakkhussa taṃnibbattassa kammassa ca adhippetattā “na cakkhu purāṇaṃ, kammameva purāṇa”nti vatvā yathā tassa cakkhussa purāṇapariyāyo [M 2.0316] vutto, taṃ dassento āha – “kammato panā”tiādi. Paccayanāmenāti purimajātisaṃsiddhattā “purāṇa”nti vattabbassa paccayabhūtassa kammassa nāmena. Evaṃ vuttanti “purāṇakamma”nti evaṃ vuttaṃ. Paccayehi abhisamāgantvā katanti taṇhāvijjādipaccayehi abhimukhabhāvena samāgantvā samecca nibbattitaṃ. Cetanāyāti kammacetanāya. Pakappitanti abhisamīhitaṃ. Vedanāyāti attānaṃ nissāya ārammaṇaṃ katvā pavattāya vedanāya. Vatthūti nibbattikāraṇaṃ pavattaṭṭhānanti vipassanāpaññāya passitabbaṃ. Kammassa nirodhenāti kilesānaṃ anuppādanirodhasiddhena kammassa nirodhena. Vimuttiṃ phusatīti arahattaphalavimuttiṃ pāpuṇāti. Ārammaṇabhūto nirodho nibbānaṃ “kammanirodho”ti [V 2.0034] vuccati, “kammaṃ nirujjhati etthā”ti katvā. “Jhāyatha, bhikkhave, mā pamādatthā”ti vuttattā “pubbabhāgavipassanā kathitā”ti vuttaṃ.

146. Because both the presently existing eye and the kamma that produced it are intended, it is said, 'The eye is not old, kamma itself is old.' To show how the term 'old' is stated for that eye, he said: 'But from kamma,' etc. 'By the name of the condition': by the name of the kamma that has become the condition, which should be called 'old' because it was accomplished in a previous birth. 'Thus it is said': thus it is called 'old kamma.' 'Made by coming together with conditions': produced by coming together and assembling face-to-face with conditions such as craving and ignorance. 'By volition': by kamma-volition. 'Determined': intended. 'By feeling': by the feeling that occurs depending on oneself and taking an object. 'The basis': the cause of its production and the place of its occurrence; this is to be seen with insight-wisdom. 'By the cessation of kamma': by the cessation of kamma that is accomplished through the non-arising-cessation of the defilements. 'Touches liberation': one attains the liberation that is the fruit of arahantship. The cessation that has become the object, Nibbāna, is called 'the cessation of kamma,' because 'kamma ceases here.' Since it is said, 'Meditate, bhikkhus, do not be heedless,' it is said, 'The preliminary part of insight has been spoken of.'

Kammanirodhasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourse on the Cessation of Kamma is concluded.

2-5. Aniccanibbānasappāyasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-5. Commentary on the Discourses beginning with the Aniccanibbānasappāyasutta.

147-150. Nibbānassāti nibbānādhigamassa, kilesanibbānasseva vā. Upakārapaṭipadanti upakārāvahaṃ paṭipadaṃ. Catūsūti dutiyādīsu catūsu. Nibbānasappāyā paṭipadā desitāti katvā “saha vipassanāya cattāro maggā kathitā”ti vuttaṃ.

147-150. 'Of Nibbāna': for the attainment of Nibbāna, or of the Nibbāna of the defilements. 'The helpful practice': the practice that brings help. 'In the four': in the four suttas beginning with the second. Because the practice suitable for Nibbāna was taught, it is said: 'The four paths together with insight have been spoken of.'

Aniccanibbānasappāyasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourses beginning with the Aniccanibbānasappāyasutta is concluded.

6-7. Antevāsikasuttādivaṇṇanā

6-7. Commentary on the Discourses beginning with the Antevāsikasutta (The Pupil).

151-152. Anta-saddo samīpatthe vattati “udakantaṃ vananta”ntiādīsu, kileso pana atiāsanne vasati abbhantaravuttitāyāti “antevāsika”nti vutto vibhattialopena yathā “vanekusalo, kūlerukkhā”ti. Tenāha – “anantevāsikanti antovasanakilesavirahita”nti. Ācaraṇakakilesavirahitanti samudācaraṇakilesarahitaṃ. Anto [M 2.0317] assa vasantīti assa puggalassa anto abbhantare citte vasanti pavattanti. Te etaṃ adhibhavantīti te kilesā etaṃ puggalaṃ abhibhavitvā attano vase vattenti. Tenāha – “ajjhottharanti sikkhāpenti vā”ti. Tehi ācariyehīti tehi kilesasaṅkhātehi satte attano gatiyaṃ ṭhapentehi ācariyehi. Sattamaṃ heṭṭhā kathitanayamevāti yasmā heṭṭhā khandhavasena desanā āgatā, idha āyatanavasenāti ayameva viseso.

151-152. The word 'anta' is used in the sense of proximity, as in 'udakantaṃ' (the water's edge) and 'vanantaṃ' (the forest's edge). But a defilement dwells extremely near, because it occurs internally; hence it is called 'antevāsika' (one who dwells within), with elision of the case ending, as in 'vanekusalo' (skilled in the forest) and 'kūlerukkhā' (trees on the bank). Therefore he said: 'Anantevāsika means one who is free from the defilements that dwell within.' 'Free from defilements related to conduct' means free from manifest defilements. 'They dwell within him' means: those defilements reside and operate within the mind of that individual. 'They overpower him' means: those defilements dominate that person and keep him under their control. Hence he said: 'They overwhelm or they instruct him.' 'By those teachers': by those teachers designated as defilements, which establish beings in their own destination. The seventh sutta is just in the way previously explained: since the teaching was formerly given by way of the aggregates, here it is by way of the sense bases; this is the only difference.

Antevāsikasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourses beginning with the Antevāsikasutta is concluded.

8. Atthinukhopariyāyasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Atthinukhopariyāyasutta

153. Pariyāyati parigacchati phalaṃ etassāti pariyāyo hetūti āha – “yaṃ pariyāyanti yaṃ kāraṇa”nti. Paccakkhadiṭṭhe aviparīte atthe pavattasaddhā paccakkhasaddhā yathā “sammāsambuddho bhagavā, svākhāto dhammo”ti (ma. ni. 1.288) ca. Evaṃ kirāti iti kirāya uppanno saddahanākāro saddhāpatirūpako. Etanti “aññatreva saddhāyā”ti etaṃ vacanaṃ[V 2.0035]. Rucāpetvāti kiñci atthaṃ attano matiyā rocetvā. Khamāpetvāti tasseva vevacanaṃ, cittaṃ tathā khamāpetvā. Tenāha – “atthetanti gahaṇākāro”ti. Paramparāgatassa atthassa evaṃ kirassāti anussavanaṃ. Kāraṇaṃ cintentassāti yuttiṃ cintentassa. Kāraṇaṃ upaṭṭhātīti “sādhū”ti attano cittassa upatiṭṭhati. Atthetanti “etaṃ kāraṇaṃ evamayamattho yujjatī”ti cittena gahaṇaṃ. Ākāraparivitakkoti yuttiparikappanā. Laddhīti nicchayena gahaṇaṃ, sā ca kho diṭṭhi ayāthāvaggahaṇena aññāṇamevāti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Tanti laddhiṃ. Atthesāti esā laddhi mama uppannā atthi yuttarūpā hutvā upalabbhati. Evaṃ gahaṇākāro diṭṭhinijjhānakkhanti nāma, paṭhamuppannaladdhisaṅkhātāya diṭṭhiyā nijjhānaṃ khamanākāro diṭṭhinijjhānakkhanti nāma. Pañca ṭhānānīti yathāvuttāni saddhādīni pañca kāraṇāni. Muñcitvā aggahetvā. Heṭṭhimamaggavajjhānaṃ rāgādīnaṃ abhāvaṃ sandhāya “natthi me ajjhattaṃ rāgadosamoho”ti ayaṃ sekkhānaṃ paccavekkhaṇā, sabbaso abhāvaṃ sandhāya asekkhānanti āha – “sekkhāsekkhānaṃ paccavekkhaṇā kathitā”ti. “Santaṃ [M 2.0318] vā ajjhatta”ntiādinā sekkhānaṃ, “asantaṃ vā ajjhatta”ntiādinā asekkhānaṃ paccavekkhaṇā kathitāti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

153. A 'pariyāya' (method) is a cause because the fruit goes around or comes to it; thus he said: 'That which is a method is a cause.' 'Direct faith' is faith that occurs in regard to a meaning that is directly seen and not distorted, as in: 'The Blessed One is a perfectly enlightened one, the Dhamma is well-proclaimed' (M I 288), etc. 'Thus by hearsay': an appearance of faith, a semblance of faith, that has arisen from hearsay. 'This': this statement, 'apart from faith.' 'Having approved': having approved of some meaning with one's own opinion. 'Having made acceptable': a synonym for that; having made the mind accept it thus. Therefore he said: 'This is a way of grasping.' 'Hearsay' is oral tradition regarding a meaning that has come down in succession. 'Of one thinking of a reason': of one thinking of a rationale. 'A reason occurs': it occurs to one's own mind as 'good.' 'This is': the grasping with the mind, 'This reason is so, this meaning is fitting.' 'Reflection on aspects' is deliberation on a rationale. 'A tenet' is a grasping with certainty; but that view, because it is an incorrect grasping, should be seen as simply ignorance. 'That': the tenet. 'This is': 'This tenet that has arisen in me exists; it is found to be reasonable.' Such a way of grasping is called 'acceptance through pondering a view'; 'acceptance through pondering a view' is the mode of accepting by pondering a view designated as a tenet that has first arisen. 'The five grounds': the five causes, faith and so on, as stated. 'Having released': not having grasped. With reference to the absence of lust, etc., which are to be abandoned by the lower paths, 'There is no lust, hatred, or delusion internally in me' is the reviewing of a trainee; with reference to their absence in every way, it is for a non-trainee. Thus he said: 'The reviewing of trainees and non-trainees has been spoken of.' It should be seen that the reviewing of trainees is spoken of with the words beginning 'Whether there is internally...,' and the reviewing of non-trainees with the words beginning 'Whether there is not internally...'

Atthinukhopariyāyasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Atthinukhopariyāyasutta is concluded.

9-10. Indriyasampannasuttādivaṇṇanā

9-10. Commentary on the Indriyasampanna Sutta and others

154-155. “Sampannasīlā, bhikkhave, viharathā”tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.64) viya paripuṇṇattho idha sampanna-saddoti āha “paripuṇṇindriyo”ti. Indriyehi samannāgatattā paripuṇṇindriyo nāma hotīti sambandho. Evaṃ sati samannāgamasampatti vuttā hotīti āsaṅkanto “cakkhādīni vā”tiādimāha. Taṃ sandhāyāti dutiyavikappena vuttamatthaṃ sandhāya. Heṭṭhā khandhiyavagge khandhavasena desanā āgatā, idha āyatanavasenāti āha “vuttanayamevā”ti.

154-155. As in such passages as (MN 1.64) 'Dwell, bhikkhus, accomplished in virtue,' here the word 'accomplished' has the meaning of 'complete'; thus it is said, 'complete in faculties.' The connection is that one is called 'complete in faculties' because one is endowed with the faculties. This being so, suspecting that the accomplishment of being endowed has been stated, he said, 'or the eye, etc.' This refers to the meaning stated by the second alternative. Below, in the Chapter on the Aggregates, the teaching was presented by way of the aggregates; here it is by way of the sense bases. Thus it is said, 'in the very way it was stated.'

Indriyasampannasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Indriyasampanna Sutta and others is concluded.

Navapurāṇavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Navapurāṇavagga is concluded.

Tatiyo paṇṇāsako.

The Third Fifty.

16. Nandikkhayavaggo

16. The Chapter on the Destruction of Delight

1-4. Ajjhattanandikkhayasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-4. Commentary on the Ajjhattanandikkhaya Sutta and others

156-159. Atthatoti [V 2.0036] sabhāvato. Ñāṇena ariyato ñātabbato attho, sabhāvoti. Evañhi abhijjanasabhāvo nandanaṭṭhena nandī, rañjanaṭṭhena rāgo. Vimuttivasenāti vimuttiyā adhigamavasena. Etthāti imasmiṃ paṭhamasutte. Dutiyādīsūti dutiyatatiyacatutthesu. Uttānameva heṭṭhā vuttanayattā.

`Atthato` means: by way of its nature. The meaning (`attho`) is what is to be known by noble wisdom; this is its nature (`sabhāvo`). For thus, delight (`nandī`) has the nature of coveting in the sense of rejoicing; lust (`rāgo`) has the nature of attachment in the sense of being impassioned. `Vimuttivasena` means: by way of the attainment of liberation. `Ettha` means: in this first sutta. `Dutiyādīsu` means: in the second, third, and fourth. It is self-evident, as the method has been stated below.

Ajjhattanandikkhayasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Ajjhattanandikkhaya Sutta and others is concluded.

5-6. Jīvakambavanasamādhisuttādivaṇṇanā

5-6. Commentary on the Jīvakambavanasamādhi Sutta and others

160-161. Samādhivikalānaṃ [M 2.0319] cittekaggataṃ labhantānaṃ, paṭisallānavikalānaṃ kāyavivekañca cittekaggatañca labhantānanti yojanā. Pākaṭaṃ hotīti vibhūtaṃ hutvā upaṭṭhāti. Okkhāyati paccakkhāyatīti catusaccadhammānaṃ vibhūtabhāvena upaṭṭhānassa kathitattā vuttaṃ – “dvīsupi…pe… kathitā”ti.

160-161. The construction is: for those deficient in concentration who attain one-pointedness of mind, and for those deficient in seclusion who attain bodily seclusion and one-pointedness of mind. `Pākaṭaṃ hoti` means: it becomes manifest and appears. `Okkhāyati paccakkhāyati`: this is said because the appearance of the phenomena of the Four Noble Truths in a manifest state has been spoken of; thus it is said: 'in both ...pe... stated.'

Jīvakambavanasamādhisuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Jīvakambavanasamādhi Sutta and others is concluded.

7-9. Koṭṭhikaaniccasuttādivaṇṇanā

7-9. Commentary on the Koṭṭhikaanicca Sutta and others

162-164. Aniccānupassanādayo eva vimuttiparipācaniyā dhammā nāma. Therassa tadā saddhādīni indriyāni na paripākaṃ upagatāni, thero imāhi desanāhi indriyaparipākamagamāsi.

The contemplations of impermanence and so forth are indeed the factors that bring liberation to maturity. At that time, the elder’s faculties, such as faith, had not reached maturity, but through these teachings, the elder attained maturity of the faculties.

Koṭṭhikaaniccasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Koṭṭhikaanicca Sutta and others is concluded.

10-12. Micchādiṭṭhipahānasuttādivaṇṇanā

10-12. Commentary on the Micchādiṭṭhipahāna Sutta and others

165-167. Pāṭiyekkanti [V 2.0037] visuṃ visuṃ. Vuttanayenevāti heṭṭhā vuttanayeneva apubbassa vattabbassa abhāvā.

`Pāṭiyekkaṃ` means individually, separately. `Vuttanayeneva` means by the very method previously stated, as there is nothing new to be said.

Micchādiṭṭhipahānasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Micchādiṭṭhipahāna Sutta and others is concluded.

Nandikkhayavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Nandikkhayavagga is concluded.

17. Saṭṭhipeyyālavaggo

17. The Chapter of the Sixty Repetitions

1-60. Ajjhattaaniccachandasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-60. Commentary on the Ajjhattaaniccachanda Sutta and others

168-227. “Yaṃ, bhikkhave, aniccaṃ, tatra vo chando pahātabbo”tiādinā tesaṃ tesaṃ puggalānaṃ ajjhāsayavasena saṭṭhi suttāni kathitāni[M 2.0320], tāni ca peyyālanayena desanaṃ āruḷhānīti “saṭṭhipeyyālo nāma hotī”ti vuttaṃ. Tenāha “yāni panetthā”tiādi.

By the passage beginning, 'Bhikkhus, you should abandon desire for whatever is impermanent,' sixty suttas were taught in accordance with the dispositions of those various persons. And since these have been presented by the repetition method, it is called 'The Sixty Repetitions.' Therefore he said, 'But those here...' and so on.

Ajjhattaaniccachandasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Ajjhattaaniccachanda Sutta and others is concluded.

Saṭṭhipeyyālavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Saṭṭhipeyyālavagga is concluded.

18. Samuddavaggo

18. The Chapter on the Ocean

1. Paṭhamasamuddasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the First Sutta on the Ocean

228. Yadi “duppūraṇaṭṭhena samuddanaṭṭhenā”ti iminā atthadvayena sāgaro “samuddo”ti vuccati, cakkhussevetaṃ nippariyāyato yujjatīti dassetuṃ “yadī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tattha duppūraṇaṭṭhenāti pūretuṃ asakkuṇeyyabhāvena. Samuddanaṭṭhenāti sabbaso uparūparipakkhittagamanena. Mahāgaṅgādimahānadīnaṃ mahatā udakoghena anusaṃvaccharaṃ anupakkhandamānopi hi samuddo pāripūriṃ na gacchati, yañca bhūmipadesaṃ ottharati, taṃ samuddabhāvaṃ neti, abhāvaṃ vā pāpuṇāti apayāte samuddodake, taṃ vā anudakabhāvapattiyā atathameva hoti. Kāmañcesa duppūraṇaṭṭho samuddanaṭṭho [V 2.0038] sāgare labbhati, tathāpi taṃ dvayaṃ cakkhusmiṃyeva visesato labbhatīti dassento “tassa hī”tiādimāha. Samosarantanti sabbaso nīlādibhāgehi osarantaṃ, āpāthaṃ āgacchantanti attho. Kātuṃ na sakkoti duppūraṇīyattā. Sadosagamanena gacchati sattasantānassa dussanato. Dussanaṭṭhatā cassa cakkhudvārikataṇhāvasena veditabbā. Yathā samudde aparāparaṃ parivattamāno ūmiyā vego samuddassāti vuccati, evaṃ cakkhusamuddassa purato aparāparaṃ parivattamānaṃ nīlādibhedaṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ cakkhussāti vattabbataṃ arahati anaññasādhāraṇattāti vuttaṃ “rūpamayo vego”ti. Asamapekkhiteti sammādassane rūpe manāpabhāvaṃ amanāpabhāvañca gahetvā, “idaṃ nāma mayā diṭṭha”nti anupadhārentassa kevalaṃ samūhaghanavasena gaṇhantassa gahaṇaṃ asamapekkhanaṃ. Sahatīti adhibhavati, taṃnimittaṃ kañci vikāraṃ nāpajjati.

228. The passage beginning `yadi` ('if') is stated to show that if the great ocean is called `samudda` for two reasons—'because it is hard to fill' and 'because it swells up'—this applies unequivocally to the eye. Here, `duppūraṇaṭṭhena` means because it is impossible to fill. `Samuddanaṭṭhenāti` means because it constantly swells up from all sides. For even though the great rivers such as the Ganges flow into it year after year with a great flood of water, the ocean does not become full. And whatever region of land it covers, it turns into ocean, or when the ocean water recedes it ceases to be, or having become devoid of water it is as if it were not so. Although these two meanings, being hard to fill and swelling up, are found in the great ocean, to show that these two are found in a special way in the eye, he says the passage beginning `tassa hi`. `Samosarantaṃ` means flowing in from all sides with their portions of blue, etc.; the meaning is coming into range. It cannot be done because it is hard to fill. It proceeds with a faulty going because it corrupts the continuity of a being. And its corrupting nature should be understood by way of the craving that arises at the eye-door. Just as the force of the waves constantly turning over in the ocean is called the ocean's, so the flood of forms, with its divisions of blue, etc., constantly turning over before the ocean of the eye, deserves to be called the eye's because it is not shared by anything else. This is what is meant by 'a flood of forms.' Regarding `asamapekkhite`: 'not considering' is the grasping of one who, in the act of seeing correctly, does not apprehend the pleasant and unpleasant aspects of forms and does not reflect, 'This has been seen by me,' but grasps them only as a compact mass. `Sahati` means one overcomes; one does not undergo any alteration on account of that object.

Ūmīti [M 2.0321] vīciyo. Āvaṭṭo āvaṭṭanavasena pavattaṃ udakaṃ. Gāharakkhasamakarādayo gāharakkhaso. Yathā samudde ūmiyo uparūpari vattamānā attani patitapuggalaṃ ajjhottharitvā anayabyasanaṃ āpādenti, tathā āvaṭṭagāharakkhasā. Evamete rāgādayo kilesā sayaṃ uppannakasatte ajjhottharitvā anayabyasanaṃ āpādenti, kilesuppattinimittatāya sattānaṃ anayabyasanāpattihetubhūtassa ūmibhayassa ārammaṇavasena cakkhusamuddo “saūmisāvaṭṭo sagāho sarakkhaso”ti vutto.

`Ūmi` means waves. `Āvaṭṭo` is water that proceeds by swirling. `Gāharakkhaso` refers to seizing demons, sea monsters, etc. Just as in the ocean, waves, constantly rolling, overwhelm a person who has fallen in and bring them to misfortune and ruin, so too do whirlpools and seizing demons. In the same way, these defilements such as lust overwhelm beings in whom they have arisen and bring them to misfortune and ruin. Because it is a sign for the arising of defilements and a cause for beings to fall into misfortune and ruin, the ocean of the eye is called 'with waves, with whirlpools, with seizing creatures, with demons' by way of being an object of the fear of waves.

Ūmibhayanti ettha bhāyati etasmāti bhayaṃ, ūmīva bhayaṃ ūmibhayaṃ. Kujjhanaṭṭhena kodho. Sveva cittassa ca abhimaddanavasenuppādanatthena daḷhaṃ āyāsanaṭṭhena upāyāso. Ettha ca anekavāraṃ pavattitvā satte ajjhottharitvā sīsaṃ ukkhipituṃ adatvā anayabyasananipphādanena kodhūpāyāsassa ūmisadisatā daṭṭhabbā. Tathā kāmaguṇā kilesābhibhūte satte māne viya rūpādivisayasaṅkhāte attani saṃsāretvā yathā tato bahibhūte nekkhamme cittampi na uppajjati, evaṃ āvaṭṭetvā byasanāpādanena āvaṭṭasadisatā daṭṭhabbā. Yadā pana gāharakkhaso ārakkharahitaṃ attano gocarabhūmigataṃ purisaṃ abhibhuyya gahetvā agocare ṭhitampi gocaraṃ netvā bheravarūpadassanādinā attano upakkamaṃ kātuṃ asamatthaṃ katvā anvāvisitvā vaṇṇabalabhogaāyusukhehi viyojetvā mahantaṃ anayabyasanaṃ āpādeti, evaṃ mātugāmopi yonisomanasikārarahitaṃ avīrapurisaṃ attano rūpādīhi palobhanavasena abhibhuyya gahetvā vā vīrajātiyampi itthikuttabhūtehi attano hāvabhāvavilāsehi itthimāyāya anvāvisitvā vā avasaṃ attano upakāradhamme sīlādayo sampādetuṃ asamatthaṃ karonto guṇavaṇṇādīhi viyojetvā mahantaṃ anayabyasanaṃ āpādeti, evaṃ mātugāmassa gāharakkhasasadisatā daṭṭhabbā. Ūmibhayanti lakkhaṇavacanaṃ[V 2.0039]. Yathā hi ūmi bhāyitabbaṭṭhena bhayaṃ, evaṃ āvaṭṭagāharakkhasāpīti ūmiādibhayena sabhayanti attho veditabbo. Antaṃ avasānaṃ gato, evaṃ pāraṃ nibbānaṃ gatoti vuccati.

Regarding `ūmibhayaṃ` (wave-fear): one fears on account of it, thus it is fear; like a wave, it is fear—wave-fear. Anger (`kodho`) has the meaning of being angry. And that same thing, in the sense of producing oppression of the mind and in the sense of strong vexation, is distress (`upāyāso`). Here, the similarity of anger and distress to waves should be seen in that, having arisen repeatedly and overwhelmed beings, not allowing them to lift their heads, they bring about misfortune and ruin. Likewise, the similarity of sensual pleasures to whirlpools should be seen in that, having caused beings who are overcome by defilements to wander about in what is reckoned their own domain, that is, the sense fields of forms, etc., they whirl them around and bring them to ruin, so that a thought of renunciation, which is external to that, does not even arise. Furthermore, just as a seizing demon, having overpowered and seized a man who has entered its unprotected feeding ground, leads him into its feeding ground even if he stands outside it, renders him unable to make his own effort by showing him terrifying forms, etc., and then, having possessed him, deprives him of complexion, strength, wealth, life, and happiness, and brings him to great misfortune and ruin; so too a woman, having overpowered and seized an unmanly man who lacks wise attention by means of her alluring forms, etc., or having possessed even one of heroic birth with her feminine wiles, coquetry, and charms, renders him powerless and unable to accomplish his own beneficial qualities such as virtue, deprives him of his good qualities, complexion, etc., and brings him to great misfortune and ruin. Thus the similarity of a woman to a seizing demon should be seen. `Ūmibhayaṃ` is a term of characteristic. For just as a wave is fear in the sense that it is to be feared, so too are whirlpools and seizing demons. Thus the meaning to be understood is that it is fearful with the fear of waves and so on. One who has gone to the end (`antaṃ`), the conclusion, is said to have gone to the far shore (`pāraṃ`), Nibbāna.

Paṭhamasamuddasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the First Sutta on the Ocean is concluded.

2-3. Dutiyasamuddasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-3. Commentary on the Second Discourse on the Ocean, etc.

229-230. Kilesānaṃ [M 2.0322] allabhāvūpanayananti āha “temanaṭṭhenā”ti. Ariyasāvaketi anāgāmino. Te hi kāmabhavavasena atintatāya na samunnā. Tayo maccūti kilesābhisaṅkhāradevaputtamāre. Tīhi upadhīhīti kilesābhisaṅkhārakāmaguṇānaṃ vasena tīhi upadhīhi. Khandhupadhinā pana so na nirūpadhi saupādisesasseva nibbānassa adhigatattā. Gatoti paṭipattigamanena gato.

'Leading to the moist state of the defilements' is explained as 'by way of moistening.' 'Noble disciple' means a non-returner. For they are not arrogant, being unsated in regard to sensual existence. 'The three deaths' means the defilements, formations, and the deva's son Māra. 'By the three substrates' means by the three substrates in terms of the defilements, formations, and sensual qualities. However, in regard to the substrate of the aggregates, he is not without substrate, because of having attained Nibbāna with a residue remaining. 'Gone' means gone by the going of the practice.

Dutiyasamuddasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Second Discourse on the Ocean, etc., is finished.

4-6. Khīrarukkhopamasuttādivaṇṇanā

4-6. Commentary on the Discourse with the Simile of the Milk-Tree and the like

231-233. Appahīnaṭṭhenāti maggena asamugghāṭitabhāvena. Atthīti vijjati. Sati paccaye vijjamānakiccakaraṇato pariyuṭṭhānaṃ appakaṃ mūsikāvisaṃ viya parittaṃ nāma hoti appānubhāvattā. Evarūpāpīti appakāpi. Assāti appahīnakilesassa. Adhimattānanti iṭṭhānaṃ rajanīyānaṃ, vatthuvasena parittakampi iṭṭhārammaṇaṃ adhimattameva. Tenāha “nakhapiṭṭhippamāṇampī”tiādi. Daharotiādīni tīṇipi padāni. Ābhindeyyāti bhindeyya. Taṃ ubhayanti taṃ cakkhurūpanti ubhayampi āyatanaṃ.

'In the sense of not being abandoned' means by the path, by way of not being eradicated. 'There is' means it exists. When the condition is present, because its function is performed, the obsession is slight; like a little mouse poison, it is called 'minor' because of its weak power. 'Even of such a kind' means even a slight one. 'Of him' means of one whose defilements are not abandoned. 'Excessive' means of desirable, passion-inducing things; in terms of the object, even a small desirable object is indeed excessive. Therefore it is said: 'even the size of the back of a fingernail,' etc. 'Young,' and so on, are the three terms. 'Might break' means it would break. 'That both' means both those sense bases, the eye and form.

Khīrarukkhopamasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourse with the Simile of the Milk-Tree and the like is finished.

7. Udāyīsuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Udāyī Sutta

234. Itipīti imināpi kāraṇena. Aniccenāti aniccabhāvena anattalakkhaṇaṃ kathitaṃ. Yasmā hetupaccayā viññāṇassa uppatti sati ca uppāde nirodhena bhavitabbaṃ, uppādavayavantatāya aniccaṃ viññāṇaṃ, yadi ca attā siyā paccayehi vinā sijjheyya, na ca tathāssa siddhi, tasmā “viññāṇaṃ anattā”ti aniccatāya anattatā kathitā.

234. 'Thus also' means for this reason too. 'By the impermanent': the characteristic of not-self is stated by way of the nature of impermanence. Because the arising of consciousness is dependent on cause and condition, and when there is arising there must be cessation, consciousness is impermanent because it possesses arising and passing away. And if it were a self, it would be established without conditions, but its establishment is not so. Therefore, 'consciousness is not-self'—its not-self nature is stated through its impermanence.

Udāyīsuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Udāyī Sutta is finished.

8. Ādittapariyāyasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Fire Sermon

235. Kilesānaṃ [V 2.0040][M 2.0323] anu anu byañjanato paribyattiyā uppattipaccayabhāvato anubyañjanaṃ, hatthapādādiavayavāti āha – “hatthā sobhanā”tiādi. Nimittaggāhoti kilesuppattiyā nimittabhūto gāho. Saṃsandetvā gahaṇanti avayave samodhānetvā “itthipuriso”tiādinā ekajjhaṃ gahaṇaṃ. Vibhattigahaṇanti vibhāgena anavasesaggahaṇaṃ. Kumbhīlasadisoti kumbhīlagāhasadiso. Tenāha – “sabbameva gaṇhātī”ti hatthapādādīsu taṃ taṃ koṭṭhāsaṃ vibhajitvā gahaṇaṃ rattapāsadiso jalūkagāhasadiso. Ekajavanavārepi labbhantīti idaṃ cakkhudvārānusārena uppannamanodvārikajavanaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

235. 'Secondary characteristic' is so called because it is a condition for the arising of defilements through the clear expression that manifests them again and again; it refers to limbs such as hands and feet, thus it is said: 'beautiful hands,' etc. 'Grasping the sign' is the grasp that becomes a sign for the arising of defilements. 'Grasping by connecting' means grasping as a whole by combining the parts, as in 'a woman, a man,' etc. 'Grasping by distinguishing' means grasping by division without remainder. 'Like a crocodile' means like the grasp of a crocodile. Therefore it is said, 'it seizes everything.' Grasping each part of the hands, feet, etc., by dividing them is like a heated stone and like the grasp of a leech. 'They are obtained even in a single impulsion process'—this is said in reference to the mind-door impulsion that arises in accordance with the eye-door.

Nimittassādena ganthitanti yathāvutte nimitte assādagāhena ganthitaṃ sambaddhaṃ. Bhavaṅgenevāti mūlabhavaṅgeneva. Kilesabhayaṃ dassentoti tathā kilesuppattiyā sati akusalacittena antaritaṃ ce maraṇacittaṃ bhaveyya, ekantato niraye vā tiracchānayoniyā vā uppatti siyāti kilesānaṃ bhāyitabbaṃ dassento. “Samayavasena vā evaṃ vutta”nti vatvā tamatthaṃ vivaranto “cakkhudvārasmiñhī”tiādimāha. Rattacittaṃ vāti rāgavasena rattacittaṃ vā. Duṭṭhacittena kathaṃ ārammaṇarasānubhavananti? Domanassavedanuppatti eva tassa ārammaṇarasānubhavanaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Imassa samayassāti maraṇasamayassa.

'Tied by gratification in the sign' means tied, connected, by grasping the gratification in the sign as described. 'By the life-continuum itself' means by the root life-continuum.
