269. Cūḷā pana assa mahatī atthi savisesā, tasmā “moḷiyasīvako”ti vuccati. Channaparibbājakoti kambalādinā kopīnapaṭicchādakaparibbājako. Pittapaccayānīti pittahetukāni[V 2.0074]. “Tisso vedanā”ti vatvā tāsaṃ sambhavaṃ dassetuṃ “katha”ntiādi vuttaṃ. Kusalavedanā uppajjati pittapaccayā. Pittabhesajjaṃ karissāmīti bhesajjasambharaṇatthañceva tadatthaṃ āmisakiñjakkhasambharaṇatthañca pāṇaṃ hanatīti yojanā. Majjhatto bhesajjakaraṇe udāsīno.

269. He had a particularly large and prominent topknot, hence he was called 'Moḷiyasīvaka.' 'Channaparibbājaka' refers to a wanderer who covers his loincloth with a woolen blanket or the like. 'Pittapaccayāni' means caused by bile. Having stated 'three feelings,' 'how,' etc., is stated to show their origin. A wholesome feeling arises because of bile. The construction is that he kills a living being to prepare bile medicine, both for the purpose of gathering medicinal ingredients and for gathering the material requisites for that purpose. One is indifferent in the preparation of medicine.

Tasmāti yasmā pittādipaccayahetukanti attano ca lokassa ca paccakkhaṃ atidhāvanti ye samaṇā vā brāhmaṇā vā, tasmā tesaṃ micchā. Pittādīnaṃ tiṇṇampi samodhānasannipāte jātāni sannipātikāni. Purimautuno visadiso utuvipariṇāmoti āha “visabhāgaututo jātānī”ti[M 2.0361]. Anudako thaddhalūkhabhūmivibhāgo jaṅgaladeso, vuttavipariyāyena anupadeso veditabbo. Malayaṃ himasītabahulo, itaro uṇhabahulo.

'Therefore'—because feelings are caused by bile, etc., those ascetics or brahmins who disregard the direct perception of themselves and the world are therefore wrong. Feelings arising from the combination and confluence of all three—bile, etc.—are called 'sannipātika.' A change of season unlike the previous one is a change to a dissimilar season; hence it is said, 'arisen from a dissimilar season.' 'Anudako' is a waterless, hard, and rough area of land, a wilderness region, while 'anupadesa' should be understood as the opposite of what has been said. 'Malaya' is a region abounding in snow and cold, while the other abounds in heat.

Attano pakaticariyānaṃ visamaṃ kāyassa pariharaṇavasena, jātāni pana asayhasahanaadesaakālacaraṇādinā veditabbānīti āha “mahābhāravahanā”tiādi. Parassa upakkamato nibbattāni opakkamikānīti āha – “ayaṃ coro vā”tiādi. Kevalanti bāhirapaccayaṃ anapekkhitvā kevalaṃ teneva. Tenāha “kammavipākatova jātānī”ti. Sakkā paṭibāhituṃ patīkārena. Lokavohāro nāma kathito pittasamuṭṭhānādisamaññāya lokasiddhattā. Kāmaṃ sarīrasannissitā vedanā kammanibbattāva, tassā pana paccuppannapaccayavasena evamayaṃ lokavohāroti vuttañceva gahetvā paravādapaṭisedho katoti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

Feelings arising from one's own irregular bodily conduct are to be understood through such things as enduring the unendurable, being in unsuitable places, and untimely wandering; hence the mention of 'bearing heavy burdens,' etc. Feelings arising from the attack of others are 'opakkamika'; hence it is said, 'this thief,' etc. 'Kevala' means solely, without dependence on an external cause, but solely by that itself. Hence the statement 'arising solely from kamma-result.' It is able to be warded off by countermeasures. 'Lokavohāra' means conventional speech, so called because designations such as 'bile-originated,' etc., are established in the world. Though feelings dependent on the body are indeed produced by kamma, this conventional speech is used here based on present causes; it should be seen that, taking this into account, a refutation of others' views has been made.

Sīvakasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Sīvaka Sutta is concluded.

2-10. Aṭṭhasatasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-10. The Commentary on the Aṭṭhasata Sutta, etc.

270-278. Vedanānaṃ aṭṭhādhikaṃ sataṃ, tassa aṭṭhasatassa tabbhāvassa pariyāyo kāraṇaṃ ettha atthīti aṭṭhasatapariyāyo, suttaṃ. Tenāha “aṭṭhasatassa kāraṇabhūta”nti. Dhammakāraṇanti pariyattidhammabhūtaṃ kāraṇaṃ. Kāyikāti pañcadvārakāyikā. Tenāha “kāmāvacareyeva labbhantī”ti, kāmabhūmikāti attho. Arūpāvacare natthi, tibhūmikāti attho. Tenāha – “arūpe tikacatukkajjhānaṃ uppajjati, tañca kho lokuttaraṃ, na lokiya”nti. Itarā upekkhāvedanā. Upavicaranti upecca pajjantīti attho. Taṃsampayuttānanti vicārasampayuttānaṃ.

270-278. There are one hundred and eight feelings. Because the discourse (pariyāya) which is the reason for the existence of that one hundred and eight is herein, it is the Aṭṭhasatapariyāya Sutta. Therefore, it is said, 'being the cause of one hundred and eight.' 'The cause of the Dhamma' means the cause that is the Dhamma of learning. 'Bodily' means bodily pertaining to the five doors. Therefore, it is said, 'they are obtainable only in the sensuous realm,' meaning pertaining to the sensuous plane. It does not exist in the formless realm, meaning pertaining to the three planes. Therefore, it is said: 'in the formless realm, the third and fourth jhānas arise, but that is supramundane, not mundane.' The other is the feeling of equanimity. 'They investigate' means they approach and occur. 'Associated with that' means associated with investigation.

Paṭilābhatoti paṭiladdhabhāvato. Samanupassatoti paccavekkhato passato. Atītaṃ khaṇattayātikkamena atikkantaṃ, niruddhappattiyā niruddhaṃ, pakativijahanena vipariṇataṃ. Samanussaratoti [V 2.0075] cintayato. Gehassitanti kāmaguṇanissitaṃ. Kāmaguṇā hi idha gehanissitadhammena gehapariyāyena vuttā.

'From acquisition' means from the state of having acquired. 'He considers' means he observes and sees. The past is what has passed, having gone beyond the three moments; ceased, upon reaching cessation; transformed, by the abandoning of its nature. 'He recollects' means he thinks about. 'Attached to home' means dependent on sensual qualities. For here, sensual qualities are spoken of in terms of dependence on home, by way of the expression 'home.'

Vipariṇāmavirāganirodhanti [M 2.0362] vipariṇāmanaṃ virajjanalakkhaṇaṃ nirujjhanañca viditvā. Pubbeti atīte. Etarahīti idāni vattamānā. Sammappaññāya passatoti vipassanāpaññāya ceva maggapaññāya ca yāthāvato passato. Ussukkāpetunti vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā maggapaṭivedhaṃ pāpetuṃ. Nibbānaṃ uddissa pavattitattā nekkhammassitasomanassāni nāma. Lokāmisapaṭisaṃyuttānanti kāmaguṇanissitānaṃ. Tadāyatananti taṃ āyatanaṃ taṃ kāraṇaṃ arahattaṃ. Anuttaresu vimokkhesūti ariyaphaladhammesu. Pihanti adhigamicchaṃ.

'Having known change, fading away, and cessation' means having known changing, the characteristic of fading away, and ceasing. 'Formerly' means in the past. 'Now' means at present, occurring. 'He sees with right wisdom' means seeing things as they really are with the wisdom of insight and the wisdom of the path. 'He arouses zeal' means establishing insight to bring about the penetration of the path. Because they occur aiming towards Nibbāna, they are called 'gladness dependent on renunciation.' 'Connected with worldly bait' means dependent on sensual qualities. 'That base' means that ground, that cause, which is Arahantship. 'In the unsurpassed liberations' means in the states of noble fruition. 'He longs' means he desires to attain.

Upekkhāti somanassarahitaaññāṇupekkhā. Bālyayogato bālassa, tato eva mūḷhassa puthujjanassa. Kilesodhīnaṃ maggodhīhi ajitattā anodhijinassa. Sattamabhavādito uddhaṃ pavattanavipākassa ajitattā avipākajinassa. Anekādīnave vaṭṭe ādīnavassa ajānanena anādīnavadassāvino. Paṭipattipaṭivedhabāhusaccābhāvena assutavato puthujjanassa. Rūpaṃ sā nātivattati na atikkamati ñāṇasampayuttābhāvato. Sabbasaṅgāhakoti sabbadhamme saṅgaṇhanako. Tatiyādīni yāva dasamā heṭṭhā vuttanayattā uttānatthāneva.

'Equanimity' means the ignorant equanimity that is devoid of joy. From association with childishness, it refers to the foolish, deluded ordinary person. Because the floods of defilements are not conquered by the floods of the path, he is one who has not conquered the floods. From the seventh existence upwards, because the ongoing result is not conquered, he is one who has not conquered the result. Because of not knowing the danger in the round of existence with its many dangers, he is one who sees no danger. Because of the lack of extensive learning in practice and penetration, he is an unlearned ordinary person. That equanimity does not transcend form; it does not surpass it, due to the absence of association with knowledge. 'All-embracing' means that which encompasses all phenomena. The third and so on up to the tenth are clear in meaning, according to the method explained before.

Aṭṭhasatasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Aṭṭhasata Sutta and others is concluded.

11. Nirāmisasuttavaṇṇanā

11. The Commentary on the Nirāmisa Sutta

279. Ārammaṇato sampayogato ca kilesāmisena sāmisā. Nirāmisāyāti nissakkavacanaṃ. Nirāmisatarāvāti ekaṃsavacanaṃ tassā tathā nissametabbatāya. Sā hi pīti sabbaso santakilesāmise santāne pavattiyā accantasabhāvadhammārammaṇavisayatāya sayampi sātisayaṃ santasabhāvākārena pavattiyā nirāmisāyapi nirāmisatarā vuttā. Tenāha “nanu cā”tiādi. Idāni tamatthaṃ upamāya sādhetuṃ “yathā hī”tiādimāha. Appaṭihārikanti paṭiharaṇarahitaṃ appaṭihāraṃ, kenaci anāvaṭanti attho. Sesavāresūti sukhavāraupekkhāvāresu.

279. They are worldly (sāmisa) due to the worldly bait of the defilements, with respect to their object and association. The word 'nirāmisāya' is a definitive statement. The word 'nirāmisatarā' is a categorical statement, because it is to be understood as completely so. For that joy, arising in a continuum where the worldly bait of defilements is completely calmed, having as its object a state of an utterly peaceful nature, and itself arising with an exceedingly peaceful nature, is called 'more unworldly' even than the unworldly. Therefore, he says, 'Is it not so?' and so forth. Now, to establish that meaning by means of a simile, he says, 'Just as...' and so on. 'Appaṭihārika' means without resistance, without obstruction; it means not barred by anything. 'In the remaining instances' means in the instances of pleasure and equanimity.

Vimokkhavāro pana na atidiṭṭho itarehi visadisattā. Tenāha “vimokkhavāre panā”tiādi. Rūpapaṭisaṃyuttoti bhāvitarūpapaṭisaṃyutto. Sāmiso nāma yasmā sāmisarūpapaṭibaddhavutti [V 2.0076] ceva sāmisarūpapaṭibhāgañca[M 2.0363], tasmā “rūpāmisa”nti vuccati, tena rūpāmisena sāmiso nāma. Arūpāmisassa abhāvato arūpapaṭisaṃyutto vimokkho nirāmiso nāma.

However, the section on liberations was not extensively discussed by others due to its distinctiveness. Hence, it is said, 'Now, as for the section on liberations,' and so on. 'Connected with form' means connected with developed form. It is called 'material' because its mode of occurrence is bound to material form and it partakes of material form; therefore, it is called 'material form,' and by that material form, it is called 'material.' Since there is no material aspect of the formless, the liberation connected with the formless is called 'non-material.'

Nirāmisasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Nirāmisa Sutta is completed.

Aṭṭhasatapariyāyavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Aṭṭhasatapariyāyavagga is completed.

Vedanāsaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Connected Discourses on Feeling is finished.

3. Mātugāmasaṃyuttaṃ

3. The Connected Discourses on Women

1. Paṭhamapeyyālavaggo

1. The First Abbreviation Chapter

1-2. Mātugāmasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-2. Commentary on the Mātugāma Sutta and others

280-281. Aguṇaṅgehīti [M 2.0364][V 2.0077] aguṇakoṭṭhāsehi. Rūpayatīti rūpaṃ, sarīrarūpaṃ. Sarīrarūpaṃ pāsaṃsaṃ etassa atthīti rūpavā, tappaṭikkhepena na ca rūpavā, sampannarūpo na hotīti attho. Ñātikulato aññato vā āgatāya bhogasampadāya abhāvena na bhogasampanno. Dussīloti nissīlo. Nissīlatāya eva cassā pubbuṭṭhāyitādiācārābhāvo vutto. Ālasiyoti ālasiyatāya yutto. Pajañcassa na labhatīti pajābhāvasīsena tassā parivārahāni vuttā. Parivattetabbanti purisavasena parivattetabbaṃ.

280-281. By 'aguṇaṅgehi' is meant with sections of non-virtue. By 'rūpa' is meant form, bodily form. 'One who possesses a praiseworthy bodily form is rūpavā'; by its negation, 'na ca rūpavā,' the meaning is one is not endowed with a complete form. Not being wealthy due to the absence of riches acquired from relatives or elsewhere. 'Dussīlo' means immoral. Because of this immorality, her absence of conduct such as rising early is mentioned. 'Ālasiyo' means endowed with laziness. 'Pajañcassa na labhati' means she does not obtain progeny; under the heading of 'absence of offspring,' her loss of retinue is stated. 'Parivattetabbaṃ' means it should be adapted for the case of a man.

Mātugāmasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Mātugāma Sutta and others is completed.

3. Āveṇikadukkhasuttavaṇṇanā

3. The Commentary on the Āveṇikadukkha Sutta

282. Āveṇitabbato mariyādāya visuṃ asādhāraṇato passitabbato āveṇikāni. Paṭipacceke puggale niyuttānīti pāṭipuggalikāni. Paricārikabhāvanti upaṭṭhāyikabhāvaṃ.

282. Āveṇikāni (distinctive) because they are to be seen as exclusive, as a boundary, as separate, as uncommon. They are individually assigned to persons, hence called pāṭipuggalikāni (individual). The state of being an attendant means the state of providing service.

Āveṇikadukkhasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Āveṇikadukkha Sutta is finished.

4. Tīhidhammehisuttādivaṇṇanā

4. The Commentary on the Tīhidhammehi Sutta and others

283-303. Maccheramalapariyuṭṭhitenāti macchariyamalena abhibhūtena. Tenāti kassaci kiñci adānena ca. Etaṃ “maccheramalapariyuṭṭhitenā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Vilokento vicarati issāpakatacittatāya. Pañcamādīni yāva ekādasamā uttānatthāneva.

283-303. 'Maccheramalapariyuṭṭhitena' means overwhelmed by the stain of miserliness. 'Tena' means by not giving anything to anyone. This is what is meant by 'overwhelmed by the stain of miserliness' and so on. He wanders looking around because of a mind affected by envy. The fifth and so on up to the eleventh are clear in meaning.

Tīhidhammehisuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Tīhidhammehi Sutta and others is completed.

3. Balavaggo

3. The Chapter on Strengths

1. Visāradasuttavaṇṇanā

1. The Commentary on the Visārada Sutta

304. Rūpasampatti [M 2.0365][V 2.0078] rūpabalaṃ taṃsamaṅgino upatthambhakabhāvato. Esa nayo sesesupi. Balāni hi sattānaṃ vuḍḍhiyā upatthambhakapaccayo hoti, yathā taṃ āhāro. Tenāha – “imānī”tiādi.

304. Accomplishment of form is strength of form, because it is a supportive factor for those possessing it. This is the method for the rest as well. Strengths indeed are a supporting condition for the growth of beings, just as food is. Therefore, he said: 'These,' etc.

Visāradasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Visārada Sutta is completed.

2-10. Pasayhasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-10. Commentary on the Pasayha Sutta and others

305-313. Abhibhavitvā sabbaṃ antojanaṃ sāmikañca. Nāsentīti nāsanaṃ adassanaṃ nenti nīharantīti attho.

305-313. Having overcome all the household and the master. By 'nāsenti' is meant destruction; the meaning is that they lead to disappearance, they remove.

Pasayhasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Pasayha Sutta and others is completed.

Mātugāmasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Connected Discourses on Women is concluded.

4. Jambukhādakasaṃyuttaṃ

4. The Jambukhādaka Connected Discourses

1. Nibbānapañhasuttavaṇṇanā

1. The Commentary on the Nibbāna Questions Sutta

314. Nibbānaṃ [M 2.0366][V 2.0079] āgammāti ettha āgammāti sabbasaṅkhārehi nibbinnassa visaṅkhāraninnassa gotrabhunā vivaṭṭitamānasassa maggena sacchikaraṇenāti attho. Sacchikiriyamānañhi taṃ adhigantvā ārammaṇapaccayabhūtañca paṭicca adhipatipaccayabhūte ca tasmiṃ paramassāsabhāvena vimuttasaṅkhārassa paramagatibhāvena ca patiṭṭhānabhūte patiṭṭhāya khayasaṅkhāto maggo rāgādike khepetīti taṃsacchikaraṇābhāve rāgādīnaṃ anuppattinirodhagamanābhāvato “nibbānaṃ āgamma rāgo khīyatī”ti vuttaṃ.

314. 'Nibbānaṃ āgamma' – Here, 'āgamma' means the direct realization by the Path of one disenchanted with all formations, inclined toward the unformed, whose mind has been turned away by the change-of-lineage consciousness. For, when it is being realized, having reached it—which serves as object-condition and also as dominant-condition—one stands upon it as the ultimate state of peace, because of the liberation of formations, and as a ground of support, because of being the ultimate destination. Then the Path, reckoned as destruction, destroys lust and other defilements; thus, in the absence of that realization, because of the non-attainment of the cessation and destruction of lust and other defilements, it is said: 'By resorting to Nibbāna, lust is destroyed.'

Imināva suttenāti imināva jambukhādakasuttena. Kilesakkhayamattaṃ nibbānanti vadeyya “rāgakkhayo”tiādinā sutte āgatattā. “Kilesakkhayamatta”nti avisesena vuttattā āha “kassā”tiādi. Addhā attanoti vakkhati “parassa kilesakkhayena parassa nibbānasampatti na yuttā”ti. Nibbānārammaṇakaraṇena gotrabhukkhaṇe kilesakkhayappattitā ca āpannāti āha – “kiṃ pana tesu akhīṇesuyevā”tiādi. Nanu ārammaṇakaraṇamattena kilesakkhayo anuppattoti na sakkā vattuṃ. Cittañhi atītānāgatādisabbaṃ ālambanaṃ karoti, na nipphannamevāti. Gotrabhūpi maggena yā kilesānaṃ anuppattidhammatā kātabbā, taṃ ārabbha pavattissatīti? Na, appattanibbānassa nibbānārammaṇañāṇābhāvato. Na hi aññe dhammā viya nibbānaṃ, taṃ pana atigambhīrattā appattena ālambituṃ na sakkā, tasmā tena gotrabhunā pattabbena tikālikasabhāvātikkantagambhīrabhāvena bhavitabbaṃ, kilesakkhayamattataṃ vā icchato gotrabhuto puretaraṃ nipphannena kilesakkhayena bhavitabbaṃ. Appattakilesakkhayārammaṇakaraṇe hi sati gotrabhuto puretaracittānipi ālambeyyunti.

By this very sutta—by this very Jambukhādaka Sutta. 'Merely the destruction of defilements is Nibbāna'—one might say, because it is stated in the sutta with phrases like 'the destruction of lust,' and so on. 'Merely the destruction of defilements'—since it is spoken of generally, he says, 'Whose?' and so on. 'Surely, for oneself'—this implies, 'The destruction of another’s defilements does not lead to another’s attainment of Nibbāna.' And because the attainment of the destruction of defilements occurs at the moment of change-of-lineage (gotrabhū) by taking Nibbāna as the object, he says, 'But what if those defilements are not yet destroyed?' and so on. But can it be said that merely by taking an object, the destruction of defilements is attained? For the mind takes all objects—past, future, and so on—but not necessarily what is attained. Similarly, must the change-of-lineage (gotrabhū) arise in relation to the destruction of defilements that is to be accomplished by the path? No, because one who has not yet attained Nibbāna lacks the knowledge that takes Nibbāna as its object. Unlike other phenomena, Nibbāna is too profound to be taken as an object by one who has not yet attained it. Therefore, it must be something to be attained by that change-of-lineage, possessing a profound nature that transcends the three temporal states, or, if one insists that it is merely the destruction of defilements, then the destruction of defilements must already have been accomplished prior to the change-of-lineage. For if the object is taken before the destruction of defilements is attained, then even minds prior to the change-of-lineage could take it as their object.

Tasmātiādi vuttasseva atthassa nigamanaṃ. Taṃ panetaṃ nibbānaṃ. Rūpino dhammā arūpino dhammātiādīsūti ādisaddena lokuttaraanāsavādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Arūpadhammādibhāvaggahaṇena cassa [V 2.0080] parinipphannatā dīpitā. Tenāha “na kilesakkhayamattamevā”ti. Kilesakkhayamattatāya [M 2.0367] hi sati nibbānassa bahutā āpajjati “yattakā kilesā khīyanti, tattakāni nibbānānī”ti. Abhāvassabhāvato gambhīrādibhāvo asaṅkhatādibhāvo ca na siyā, vutto ca so nibbānassa, tasmāssa paccetabbo parinipphannabhāvo. Yasmā ca sammutisaccārammaṇaṃ saṅkhatadhammārammaṇaṃ vā samucchedavasena kilese pajahituṃ na sakkoti, yato mahaggatañāṇaṃ vipassanāñāṇaṃ vā kilesavikkhambhanavasena tadaṅgavasena vā pajahati, tasmā ariyamaggañāṇassa sammutisaccasaṅkhatadhammārammaṇehi viparītasabhāvena ārammaṇena bhavitabbaṃ. Tathā hi taṃ samucchedavasena kilese pajahīti evaṃ parinipphannāsaṅkhatasabhāvaṃ nibbānanti niṭṭhamettha gantabbanti.

Therefore, this is a conclusion of the meaning stated thus. That, indeed, is Nibbāna. In the terms 'material phenomena, immaterial phenomena,' etc., the word 'etc.' should be understood to include the supramundane and the taintless states. And by grasping the nature of immaterial phenomena, etc., its perfected nature is indicated. Hence it is said, 'Not merely the destruction of defilements.' For if Nibbāna were merely the destruction of defilements, it would become manifold—'as many defilements are destroyed, so many Nibbānas there are.' The profound nature, the unconditioned nature, etc., would not exist if it were merely an absence. And since that nature is declared to be the nature of Nibbāna, its perfected nature must be accepted. Moreover, because an object of conventional truth or conditioned phenomena cannot lead to the abandonment of defilements by eradication, whereas exalted knowledge or insight knowledge abandons defilements by suppression or by temporary substitution, therefore, the noble path knowledge must have an object of a nature contrary to conventional truth and conditioned phenomena. For it abandons defilements by eradication. Thus, it should be concluded here that Nibbāna has the nature of the perfected and unconditioned.

Nibbānapañhasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Nibbāna Questions Sutta is finished.

3-15. Dhammavādīpañhasuttādivaṇṇanā

3–15. Commentary on the Dhammavādīpañha Sutta and So Forth

316-328. Pahāya gatattāti ariyamaggena jahitvā ñāṇagamanena gatattā. Suṭṭhu gatāti sammā gatā paṭipannāti sugatā. Parijānanatthanti tīhi pariññāhi parijānanatthaṃ. Dukkhasaṅkhātoti “dukkha”nti saṅkhātabbo viditabbo ca dukkhasabhāvo dhammo dukkhadukkhatā. Yasmā dukkhavedanāvinimuttasaṅkhatadhamme sukhavedanāya ca yathā idha saṅkhāradukkhatā vipariṇāmadukkhatāti dukkhapariyāyo niruppateva, tasmā dukkhasabhāvo dhammo ekena dukkhasaddena visesetvā vutto “dukkhadukkhatā”ti. Sesapadadvayeti saṅkhāradukkhatā vipariṇāmadukkhatāti etasmiṃ padadvaye. Saṅkhārabhāvena dukkhasabhāvo saṅkhāradukkhatā. Sukhassa vipariṇāmanena dukkhasabhāvo vipariṇāmadukkhatā.

316–328. ‘Having gone by abandoning’ (pahāya gatattā) means having abandoned by the Noble Path and having gone by the going of knowledge. ‘Well-gone’ (suṭṭhu gatā) means well-gone, rightly practiced, thus ‘Sugata.’ ‘For the purpose of full understanding’ (parijānanatthaṃ) means for the purpose of fully understanding by means of the three kinds of full understanding. ‘Designated as suffering’ (dukkhasaṅkhāto) means a phenomenon whose nature is suffering, which should be designated and known as ‘suffering,’ is the suffering of suffering (dukkha-dukkhata). Because even in conditioned phenomena free from painful feeling, and in pleasant feeling, the exposition of suffering as the suffering of formations (saṅkhāra-dukkhata) and the suffering of change (vipariṇāma-dukkhata) is indeed established here, therefore, a phenomenon whose nature is suffering is specifically stated as ‘the suffering of suffering’ (dukkha-dukkhata) by the single word ‘suffering.’ In the remaining two terms (sesapadadvaye): that is, in these two terms, the suffering of formations and the suffering of change. A phenomenon whose nature is suffering due to its state as a formation is the suffering of formations. A phenomenon whose nature is suffering due to the change of pleasure is the suffering of change.

Dhammavādīpañhasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Dhammavādīpañha Sutta and so forth is concluded.

16. Dukkarapañhasuttavaṇṇanā

16. Commentary on the Dukkarapañha Sutta

329. Pabbajjāyāti [M 2.0368] pabbajitapaṭipattiyaṃ. Dhammānudhammappaṭipanno bhikkhūti dhammānudhammaṃ paṭipajjamāno bhikkhu. Pātanti pāto. Nacirassanti khippameva. Tenāha “lahuyevā”ti.

329. ‘In the going forth’ (pabbajjāya) means in the practice of one who has gone forth. ‘A monk practicing in accordance with the Dhamma’ (dhammānudhammappaṭipanno bhikkhu) means a monk who practices in accordance with the Dhamma. ‘Morning’ (pātaṃ) means dawn. ‘Not long’ (nacirassa) means quickly. Hence it is said, ‘very quickly’ (lahuyeva).

Dukkarapañhasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Dukkarapañha Sutta is concluded.

Jambukhādakasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Jambukhādaka Saṃyutta is concluded.

5. Sāmaṇḍakasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Sāmaṇḍaka Saṃyutta

330-331. Imināva [V 2.0081] nayenāti yo jambukhādakasaṃyutte atthanayo, imināva nayena. Iminā hi dve saṃyuttāni pāḷito atthato ca aññamaññaṃ sadisānevāti dasseti.

330–331. ‘In this very way’ (imināva nayena) means by the same method of explanation as in the Jambukhādaka Saṃyutta. By this it is shown that the two Saṃyuttas are similar to each other in both the Pāli text and the meaning.

Sāmaṇḍakasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sāmaṇḍaka Saṃyutta is concluded.

6. Moggallānasaṃyuttaṃ

6. The Moggallāna Saṃyutta

1-8. Paṭhamajjhānapañhasuttādivaṇṇanā

1–8. Commentary on the First Jhāna Question Sutta and so forth

332-339. Kāmasahagatesu [M 2.0369][V 2.0082] saññāmanasikāresu upaṭṭhahantesu byāpādādisahagatāpi saññāmanasikārā yathāpaccayaṃ upaṭṭhahantiyevāti vuttaṃ “kāmasahagatāti pañcanīvaraṇasahagatā”ti. Nīvaraṇānañhettha nidassanamattametaṃ, yadidaṃ kāmaggahaṇaṃ. Pahīnāvasesā cettha nīvaraṇā veditabbā, tasmā pañcaggahaṇaṃ na kattabbaṃ. Tassāti mahāmoggallānattherassa santato upaṭṭhahiṃsu aciṇṇavasitāya. Tenāha “hānabhāgiyaṃ nāma ahosī”ti. Ārammaṇa…pe… sahagatanti vuttaṃ itaresaṃ abhāvato.

332–339. When perceptions and mental advertences associated with sensual desires (kāmasahagata) arise, perceptions and mental advertences associated with ill will and so forth also arise according to conditions. Thus it is said, ‘associated with sensual desires’ means ‘associated with the five hindrances.’ Here, this taking up of ‘sensual desire’ is merely an indication of the hindrances. Here, the hindrances should be understood as remaining unabandoned; therefore, the taking up of ‘five’ should not be done. ‘For him’ (tassa) means for the venerable Mahāmoggallāna; they arose in his mental continuity due to unpracticed mastery. Hence it is said, ‘it was a state of decline’ (hānabhāgiyaṃ nāma ahosī). ‘The object… associated’ (ārammaṇa…pe… sahagataṃ)—this is said because the others are absent.

Paṭhamajjhānapañhasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the First Jhāna Question Sutta and so forth is concluded.

9. Animittapañhasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Signless Question Sutta

340. Vipassanāsamādhiṃyeva, na phalasamādhiṃ. Chejjakiccaṃ na nipphajjati avicchedavasena appavattanato. Nikantīti vipassanaṃ assādentī pavattataṇhā. Sādhetuṃ nāsakkhi upakkiliṭṭhattā. Nimittānusārīti niccasukhaattanimittānaṃ apūraṇato rāgadosamohanimittānipettha nimittāneva. Vuṭṭhāna…pe… samādhinti etena yāva maggo nādhigato, tāva vuṭṭhānagāminivipassanamanuyuñjantopi yathārahaṃ animittaṃ appaṇihitaṃ suññataṃ cetosamādhiṃ anuyutto viharatīti vattabbataṃ arahatīti dasseti. Vipassanāsampayuttanti vipassanāsaṅkhātañāṇasampayuttaṃ. Cetosamādhinti cittasīsena vipassanāsamādhimāha. Uparimaggaphalasamādhinti paṭhamamaggasamādhissa pageva adhigatattā.

340. This refers only to insight concentration (vipassanāsamādhi), not fruition concentration (phalasamādhi). The cutting-off function (chejjakicca) is not accomplished because it does not proceed without interruption. ‘Attachment’ (nikanti) is the craving that proceeds while savoring insight. Due to being defiled, it was not able to achieve its goal. ‘Following signs’ (nimittānusārī): because the signs of permanence, pleasure, and self are not fulfilled, here the signs are merely the signs of greed, hatred, and delusion. ‘Emerging… concentration’ (vuṭṭhāna…pe… samādhiṃ): by this it is shown that as long as the path has not been attained, even while practicing insight leading to emergence, one is worthy of being called one who dwells devoted, as is appropriate, to the signless, desireless, and empty concentration of mind (cetosamādhi). ‘Associated with insight’ (vipassanāsampayuttaṃ) means associated with the knowledge designated as insight. ‘Concentration of mind’ (cetosamādhiṃ) refers to insight concentration, with the mind as the principal. ‘Concentration of the higher paths and fruitions’ (uparimaggaphalasamādhiṃ): because the concentration of the first path has already been attained.

Animittapañhasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Signless Question Sutta is concluded.

10-11. Sakkasuttādivaṇṇanā

10–11. Commentary on the Sakka Sutta and so forth

341-342. Aveccappasādenāti [M 2.0370][V 2.0083] vatthuttayaṃ yāthāvato ñatvā uppannapasādena, maggenāgatapasādenāti attho. So pana kenaci asaṃhāriyo asampavedhīti āha “acalappasādenā”ti. Abhibhavanti attano puññānubhāvena.

341–342. ‘With confidence based on understanding’ (aveccappasādena) means with confidence that has arisen after knowing the three objects as they truly are; the meaning is, with confidence that has come by way of the path. Since this confidence cannot be shaken or made to tremble by anyone, it is called ‘unshakable confidence’ (acalappasādena). ‘They overcome’ (abhibhavanti) means by the power of their own merit.

Sakkasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sakka Sutta and so forth is concluded.

Moggallānasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Moggallāna Saṃyutta is concluded.

7. Cittasaṃyuttaṃ

7. The Citta Saṃyutta

1. Saṃyojanasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Saṃyojana Sutta

343. Bhogagāmanti [M 2.0371][V 2.0084] bhoguppattigāmaṃ. Pavattatīti appaṭihataṃ hutvā pavattati paṭisambhidappattiyā.

343. ‘Wealth Village’ (bhogagāmaṃ) means a village where wealth arises. ‘Flows on’ (pavattati) means it flows on unhindered, due to the attainment of the analytical knowledges.

Saṃyojanasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Saṃyojana Sutta is concluded.

2. Paṭhamaisidattasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the First Isidatta Sutta

344. Avisāradattāti pañhaṃ byākātuṃ veyyattiyābhāvena asamatthattā. Upāsako theresu byākātuṃ asakkontesu sayaṃ byākātukāmo pucchati, na vihesādhippāyo. Paṭhamavacanena abyākaronte disvāva punappunaṃ pucchitaṃ viheso viya hotīti katvā vuttaṃ “vihesetī”ti.

344. ‘Due to lack of confidence’ (avisāradattā) means due to being unable, from a lack of competence, to answer the question. The lay follower, seeing that the elders were unable to answer, wished to answer it himself and so he asked, not with the intention of causing annoyance. It was said, ‘annoys’ (viheseti), because after seeing them not answer his first words, his repeated questioning was like annoyance.

Paṭhamaisidattasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the First Isidatta Sutta is concluded.

3. Dutiyaisidattasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Second Isidatta Sutta

345. Avantiyāti avantiraṭṭhe, taṃ pana majjhimapadesato dakkhiṇadisāyaṃ. Tenāha “dakkhiṇāpathe”ti. Adhippāyenāti tassa vacanassa anumodanādhippāyena vadati, na pana tato kiñci gahetukāmatādhippāyena.

345. ‘In Avanti’ means in the Avanti country, and that is to the south of the Middle Country. Hence it is said, ‘on the Southern Road.’ ‘With intention’ means he speaks with the intention of approving that statement, not with the intention of wishing to take anything from it.

Dutiyaisidattasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Second Isidatta Sutta is concluded.

4. Mahakapāṭihāriyasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Mahākapāṭihāriya Sutta

346. Tesaṃ anujānantoti tesaṃ dāsakammakarānaṃ sesake yathāruci vicāraṇaṃ anujānanto. Kudhitanti balavatā sūriyasantāpena saṅkuthitaṃ[M 2.0372]. Tenāha “heṭṭhā”tiādi. Atitikhiṇanti ativiya tikkhadhātukaṃ. Asambhinnapadanti [V 2.0085] aññattha anāgatattā tipiṭake avomissakapadaṃ, idheva āgatapadanti attho. Paṭilīyamānenāti paṭikaṃsena visesanena vilīyamānena kāyena.

346. 'Permitting them' means permitting those servants and workers to move about as they please among the leftovers. 'Scorched' means shriveled by the intense heat of the sun. Therefore, it is said, 'below,' etc. 'Excessively sharp' means of an extremely sharp nature. 'An unmixed term' means a term not found elsewhere, an unmixed term in the Tipiṭaka, meaning a term occurring only here. 'Withdrawing' means with a body dissolving through intense shrinking.

Ettha cāti etasmiṃ adhiṭṭhāniddhikaraṇe. Abbhamaṇḍapaṃ katvāti samantato chādanavasena maṇḍapaṃ viya meghapaṭalaṃ uppādetvā. Devoti megho. Ekamekaṃ phusitakaṃ phusāyatu jālavinaddhaṃ viya vassatu. Evaṃ vuttappakārena nānāparikammaṃ nānādhiṭṭhānaṃ ekato parikammaṃ ekato adhiṭṭhānanti catukkamettha sambhavatīti dasseti “ettha cā”tiādinā. Yathā tathāti yathāvuttesu catūsu pakāresu yena vā tena vā pakārena karontassa. Kataparikammassāti “evaṃ vā evaṃ vā hotū”ti pavattitaparikammacittassa. “Parikammānantarenāti adhiṭṭhānacittuppādanatthaṃ samāpannapādakajjhānato vuṭṭhāya adhiṭṭhānacittassa ekāvajjanavīthiyaṃ pavattaparikammaṃ sandhāya vutta”nti vadanti.

'Here too' means in this undertaking of the psychic power of resolution. 'Having created a cloud-pavilion' means having produced a mass of clouds like a pavilion for the purpose of providing shade all around. 'The god' means the cloud. 'Let it rain drop by drop; let it rain as if interwoven in a net.' In this way, by saying 'here too,' etc., it shows that a fourfold method is possible here: various preparations and various resolutions; one preparation and one resolution. 'In whatever way' means for one who acts by whichever of the four methods mentioned. 'For one who has made the preparation' means for one whose preparatory thought has proceeded, thinking, 'Let it be thus or thus.' They say: 'Immediately after preparation' is said in reference to the preparation that occurs in a single thought-process of adverting for the mind of resolution, after emerging from the jhāna that is the basic attainment, for the purpose of producing the mind of resolution.

Mahakapāṭihāriyasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Mahākapāṭihāriya Sutta is concluded.

5. Paṭhamakāmabhūsuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the First Kāmabhū Sutta

347. Elaṃ vuccati doso, taṃ etassa natthīti nelaṃ, taṃ aṅgaṃ etassāti nelaṅgo, suvisuddhasīlaguṇo. Tenāha “nelaṅganti kho, bhante, sīlānametaṃ adhivacana”nti. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana dosābhāvameva dassetuṃ “nelaṅgoti niddoso”ti vuttaṃ. Etaṃ bhikkhuṃ āgacchantanti mahākappinattheraṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Attano diṭṭhena kathesīti attano sabbaññutaññāṇena paccakkhato upalakkhitena atthena kathesi. Ayaṃ pana nayaggāhenāti ayaṃ pana gahapati asutvā kevalaṃ nayaggāhena āha.

347. A fault is called `ela`. He does not have that, thus he is `nela` (faultless). That is a quality (`aṅga`) of his, thus he is `nelaṅgo` (one with the quality of faultlessness), possessing the virtue of perfectly pure moral conduct. Therefore, it is said, 'Indeed, venerable sir, "one with the quality of faultlessness" is a designation for moral conduct.' However, in the commentary, to show the very absence of faults, it is said, '`Nelaṅgo` means faultless.' 'This bhikkhu coming' is said in reference to the Elder Mahākappina. 'He spoke according to what was seen by himself' means he spoke with the meaning directly perceived by his own omniscient knowledge. 'But this one by grasping a method' means this householder, without having heard, spoke merely by grasping a method.

Paṭhamakāmabhūsuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the First Kāmabhū Sutta is concluded.

6. Dutiyakāmabhūsuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Second Kāmabhū Sutta

348. Nirodhaṃ [M 2.0373] valañjeti anāgāmibhāvato. Ime saṅkhārāti ime “kāyasaṅkhāro, vacīsaṅkhāro, cittasaṅkhāro”ti vuccamānā tayo saṅkhārā. Saddatopi, atthatopi aññamaññaṃ missā, tato eva āluḷitā ākulā, avibhūtā dubbibhāvanā, duddīpanā asaṅkarato dīpetuṃ dukkarā. Tathā hi kusalacetanā eva “kāyasaṅkhāro”tipi [V 2.0086] vuccati, “vacīsaṅkhāro”tipi, “cittasaṅkhāro”tipi. Assāsapassāsāpi katthaci “kāyasaṅkhāro”ti, vitakkavicārāpi “vacīsaṅkhāro”ti vuccanti, saññāvedanāpi “cittasaṅkhāro”ti vuccanti. Tena vuttaṃ “tathā hī”tiādi. Tattha ākaḍḍhitvā gahaṇaṃ ādānaṃ, sampattassa hatthe karaṇaṃ gahaṇaṃ, gahitassa vissajjanaṃ muñcanaṃ, phandanaṃ copanaṃ pāpetvā nipphādetvā. Hanusañcopananti kāyaviññattivasena pubbabhāge hanusañcopanaṃ katvā. Evañhi vacībhedakaraṇaṃ. Evaṃ imetiādi yathāvuttassa atthassa nigamanaṃ.

348. 'He partakes of cessation' means from the state of a non-returner. 'These formations' refers to the three formations called 'bodily formation, verbal formation, and mental formation.' Both in sound and in meaning, they are mixed with one another, and for that reason they are stirred up, confused, indistinct, difficult to distinguish, and difficult to illuminate, being hard to explain without confusion. For thus, wholesome volition itself is called 'bodily formation,' 'verbal formation,' and 'mental formation.' In-and-out breaths are also somewhere called 'bodily formation'; applied and sustained thoughts are also called 'verbal formation'; perception and feeling are also called 'mental formation.' Therefore, it is said, 'For thus,' etc. Therein, drawing in is taking; placing in the hand what has arrived is grasping; releasing what has been grasped is letting go; having caused agitation and movement, it is producing. 'Moving the jaw' means having first moved the jaw by way of bodily intimation; for in this way, the differentiation of speech occurs. 'Thus, these,' etc., is the conclusion for the meaning as stated.

Padatthaṃ pucchati itarasaṅkhārehi padatthato visesaṃ kathāpessāmīti. Kāyanissitāti ettha kāyanissitatā ca nesaṃ tabbhāvabhāvitāya veditabbā, na kāyassa nissayapaccayatāvasenāti dassento “kāye sati honti, asati na hontī”ti āha. Kāyoti cettha karajakāyo daṭṭhabbo. Cittanissitāti cittaṃ nissāya taṃ nissayapaccayabhūtaṃ labhitvā uppannā.

'He asks about the meaning of the term' with the intention, 'I will get him to speak about the distinction in the meaning of the term from the other formations.' 'Dependent on the body': here their dependence on the body should be understood as being conditioned by that state, not merely in the sense of the body being a support condition. Showing this, he said, 'They exist when the body exists; when it does not exist, they do not exist.' And here, 'body' should be seen as the body born of action. 'Dependent on the mind' means, having depended on the mind, they arise having obtained it as a support condition.

“Samāpajjāmī”ti padassa samīpe vuccamānaṃ “samāpajjissa”nti padaṃ āsannānāgatakālavācī eva bhavituṃ yuttaṃ, na itaranti āha – “padadvayena nevasaññānāsaññāyatanasamāpattikālo kathito”ti. Tayidaṃ tassa tathā vattabbatāya vuttaṃ, na pana tassa tathā cittapavattisambhavato. Samāpannepi eseva nayo. Purimehīti “samāpajjissaṃ samāpajjāmī”ti dvīhi padehi. Pacchimenāti “samāpanno”ti padena.

He says: The word `samāpajjissaṃ` ('I will attain'), being spoken near the word `samāpajjāmi` ('I am attaining'), should rightly be an indicator of the near future, not otherwise. Thus it is said: 'The time of the attainment of the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception is described by two words.' This is said because it should be spoken of in that way, but not because the proceeding of consciousness is possible in that way. This same method applies also to one who has attained. 'By the former' means by the two words 'I will attain' (`samāpajjissaṃ`) and 'I am attaining' (`samāpajjāmi`). 'By the latter' means by the word 'attained' (`samāpanno`).

Bhāvitaṃ hoti uppāditaṃ hoti nirodhasamāpannatthāya. Anupubbasamāpattisamāpajjanasaṅkhātāya nirodhabhāvanāya taṃ cittaṃ bhāvitaṃ hoti. Tenāha “yaṃ ta”ntiādi. Dutiyajjhāneyevāti dutiyajjhānakkhaṇe nirujjhati. Tattha anuppajjanato anuppādanato heṭṭhā nirodhoti adhippeto. Catunnaṃ arūpakkhandhānaṃ tajjā parikammasiddhā yā appavatti[M 2.0374], tattha “nirodhasamāpattisaññā”ti, yā nesaṃ tathā appavatti. Sā “antonirodhe nirujjhatī”ti vuttā.

It is developed, it is produced, for the purpose of attaining cessation. That mind is developed by the development of cessation, which is described as the attainment of the successive attainments. Therefore, he says, 'That which...,' etc. 'In the second jhāna itself' means it ceases at the moment of the second jhāna. There, 'cessation below' is intended because of non-arising and non-production. That non-occurrence of the four formless aggregates which is born from that and accomplished by preparation—that is 'the perception of the attainment of cessation'; that is their non-occurrence in that way. It is said of this: 'It ceases in the inner cessation.'

“Cittasaṅkhāro niruddho”ti vacanato tadaññesaṃ anirodhaṃ icchantānaṃ vādaṃ dassento “cittasaṅkhāro niruddhoti vacanato”tiādiṃ vatvā tattha atippasaṅgadassanamukhena taṃ vādaṃ nisedhetuṃ “te vattabbā”tiādimāha. Abhinivesaṃ akatvāti yathāgate byañjanamatte abhinivesaṃ akatvā. Ācariyānaṃ nayeti ācariyānaṃ paramparāgate dhammanaye dhammanettiyaṃ ṭhatvā.

Showing the view of those who, from the statement 'the mental formation has ceased,' desire the non-cessation of other things, he said, 'Because it is said, "the mental formation has ceased,"' etc. Then, in order to refute that view by way of showing the over-extension, he said, 'They should be told...,' etc. 'Without adhering' means without adhering to the mere letter as it has come down. 'In the method of the teachers' means standing in the method of the Dhamma, the guideline of the Dhamma, that has come down in the lineage of the teachers.

Kiriyamayapavattasminti [V 2.0087] parittabhūmakakusalākusaladhammapabandhe vattamāne. Tasmiñhi vattamāne kāyika-vācasika-kiriyasampavatti hoti, dassana-savanādivasena ārammaṇaggahaṇe pavattamāneti attho. Tenāha – “bahiddhārammaṇesu pasāde ghaṭṭentesū”ti. Makkhitāni viyāti puñchitāni viya honti ghaṭṭanāya vibādhitattā. Etenāyaṃ ghaṭṭanā pañcadvārikaviññāṇānaṃ vegasā uppajjanāya na ārammaṇanti dasseti. Tenevāha – “antonirodhe pañca pasādā ativiya virocantī”ti.

'When proceeding as functional' means when the continuity of wholesome and unwholesome states of the limited plane is proceeding. For when that is proceeding, the proceeding of bodily and verbal actions occurs; the meaning is: when the grasping of objects is proceeding by way of seeing, hearing, etc. Therefore, he says: 'When external objects strike the sensitive faculties...' 'As if smeared' means they are as if wiped, because of being obstructed by the impact. By this he shows that this impact is not an object for the swift arising of the five-door consciousnesses. For that very reason he says: 'In the inner cessation, the five sensitive faculties shine forth exceedingly.'

Tato paraṃ sacittako bhavissāmīti idaṃ atthato āpannaṃ gahetvā vuttaṃ – “ettakaṃ kālaṃ acittako bhavissāmī”ti eteneva tassa atthassa siddhattā. Yaṃ evaṃ bhāvitanti ettha visuṃ cittassa bhāvanā nāma natthi, addhānaparicchedaṃ pana katvā nirodhasamāpattatthāya kātabbaparikammabhāvanāya eva tassa sijjhanato.

'After that, I will be with mind'—this is said having grasped what is implied in meaning, since that meaning is established by this very statement: 'For such a time I will be without mind.' As for 'That which is thus developed,' here there is no separate development of the mind, since it is accomplished only by the development of the preparation to be done for the sake of the attainment of cessation, after having made a delimitation of the time period.

Sā panesā nirodhakathā. Dvīhi balehīti samathavipassanābalehi. Tayo ca saṅkhārānanti tiṇṇaṃ kāyavacīcittasaṅkhārānaṃ paṭippassaddhiyā. Soḷasahi ñāṇacariyāhīti aniccānupassanādīnaṃ aṭṭhannaṃ anupassanāñāṇānaṃ, aṭṭhannañca maggaphalañāṇānaṃ vasena imāhi soḷasahi ñāṇappavattīhi. Navahi samādhicariyāhīti aṭṭha samāpattiyo aṭṭha samādhicariyā, tāsaṃ upacārasamādhi samādhibhāvasāmaññena ekā samādhicariyāti evaṃ navahi samādhicariyāhi. Vasībhāvatāpaññāti vasībhāvatāsaṅkhātā paññā. Sabbākārena visuddhimagge kathitā, te tāva ākārā [M 2.0375] tiṭṭhantu, sarūpamattassa panassa vattabbanti āha – “ko panāyaṃ nirodho nāmā”ti. Yadi khandhānaṃ appavatti, atha kimatthametaṃ jhānasukhādiṃ viya samāpajjantīti āha “saṅkhārāna”ntiādi.

This is the discussion on cessation. With two powers: the powers of serenity and insight. And the three formations: through the subsiding of the three formations of body, speech, and mind. With sixteen modes of knowledge: through these sixteen occurrences of knowledge, namely, the eight knowledges of insight beginning with the contemplation of impermanence, and the eight knowledges of the path and fruition. With nine modes of concentration: the eight attainments are eight modes of concentration, and the access concentration, by way of having the general characteristic of being a state of concentration, is one mode of concentration—thus, with nine modes of concentration. Mastery-knowledge: wisdom reckoned as mastery. All aspects have been discussed in the Path of Purification. Let those aspects stand as they are. But what should be spoken of here is just its essential nature—so it is said: "What, then, is this so-called cessation?" If it is the non-occurrence of the aggregates, then why does one attain to it like the bliss of jhāna, etc.? To this, it is said: "Of the formations," etc.

Phalasamāpatticittanti arahattaṃ anāgāmiphalacittaṃ. “Tato paraṃ bhavaṅgasamaye”ti vuttattā āha “kiṃ pana…pe… na samuṭṭhāpetī”ti. Samuṭṭhāpeti rūpasamuppādakattā. Imassāti nirodhaṃ samāpannabhikkhuno. Sā na samuṭṭhāpetīti sā catutthajjhānikā phalasamāpatti na samuṭṭhāpeti assāsapassāse. Phalasamāpattiyā catutthajjhānikabhāvo kathaṃ nicchitoti āha – “kiṃ vā etenā”tiādi. Vakkhamānākārenapi parihāro hotīti. Santasamāpattitoti nirodhasamāpattimeva sandhāya vadati. Abbohārikā honti atisukhumasabhāvattā, sañjīvattherassa pubbe samāpattikkhaṇe assāsapassāsā abbohārikabhāvaṃ gacchanti. Tena vuttaṃ “bhavaṅgasamayenevetaṃ kathita”nti.

The attainment of fruition consciousness is the fruit-consciousness of arahantship or non-returning. "After that, during the life-continuum moment"—because this is said, he asks, "But what... does it not produce?" It produces, because it causes the arising of form. "Of this one"—referring to the monk who has attained cessation. "That does not produce"—meaning that that fourth jhāna fruition-attainment does not produce in-and-out breaths. How is it determined that the fruition-attainment is of the fourth jhāna? He says, "Or what is this?" and so on. Even by the manner to be spoken of, there is a resolution. "Attainment of peace"—this refers specifically to the attainment of cessation. They are unobservable—due to their extremely refined nature. For the elder Sañjīva, previously at the moment of attainment, the in-and-out breaths became unobservable. Therefore, it is said, "This was spoken of with reference to the life-continuum moment."

Kiriyamaya [V 2.0088] …pe… uppajjatīti kasmā vuttaṃ? Nanu bhavaṅguppattikālampi vitakkavicārā uppajjantevāti? Kiñcāpi uppajjanti, vacīsaṅkhāralakkhaṇappattā pana na hontīti imamatthaṃ dassetuṃ “kiṃ bhavaṅga”ntiādi vuttaṃ.

"Why is it said that it is functional... and arises?" "Do not initial and sustained thought arise even at the time of the arising of the life-continuum?" "Although they do arise, they do not attain the characteristic of verbal formation. To show this meaning, 'What is the life-continuum?' etc., was said."

Suññato phassotiādayo saguṇenapi ārammaṇenāti ārammaṇabhūtametaṃ. Suññatā nāma phalasamāpatti niccasukhaattasabhāvato suññattā. “Suññato phasso”ti vuttaṃ vuttanayena suññasabhāvattā. Animittā nāma phalasamāpatti rāganimittādīnaṃ abhāvato. Appaṇihitā nāma phalasamāpatti rāgapaṇidhiādīnamabhāvato. Sesaṃ vuttanayameva. Tenāha “animittappaṇihitesupi eseva nayo”ti. Rāganimittādīnanti ettha ādi-saddena saṅkhāranimittassapi saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Yadaggena phalasamāpattisamphasso suññato nāma, tadaggena phalasamāpattipi suññatā nāma, phassasīsena pana desanā āgatāti tathā vuttaṃ. Animittappaṇihitesupi eseva nayo.

"Contact is empty" and so on, even with an object endowed with qualities—this is the object. Emptiness is the fruition attainment because it is empty of permanence, happiness, and self-nature. "Contact is empty" is said because of its empty nature, according to the method already stated. The signless is the fruition attainment due to the absence of signs such as the sign of lust. The undirected is the fruition attainment due to the absence of desires such as craving. The rest should be understood in the way already stated. Hence it is said, "The same method applies to the signless and the undirected." Here, in "signs such as lust," the sign of formations should be understood as included by the word "etc." To the extent that contact in the fruition attainment is called empty, to that extent the fruition attainment itself is called emptiness. However, the teaching is presented with contact as the principal point, hence it is stated thus. The same method applies to the signless and the undirected.

Āgamanaṃ ettha, etāyāti vā āgamanikā, sā eva āgamaniyā ka-kārassa ya-kāraṃ katvā. Vuṭṭhāti nimittato maggassa uppādanena. Animittā nāma niccanimittassa ugghāṭanato. Ettha ca vuṭṭhānameva pamāṇaṃ, na [M 2.0376] pariggahadassanāni. Appaṇihitā nāma sukhapaṇidhiyā paṭipakkhato. Suññatā nāma attadiṭṭhiyā ujupaṭipakkhattā sattasuññatāya sudiṭṭhattā. Maggo animitto nāma vipassanāgamanato. Phalaṃ animittaṃ nāma maggāgamanato. Vikappo āpajjeyya āgamanassa vavatthānassa abhāvena, vipassanāya animittādināmalābho avavatthitoti adhippāyo. Tena vuttaṃ “tasmā”tiādi. Yasmā pana sā maggavuṭṭhānakāle evarūpāpi hotīti tassa vasena maggaphalānaṃ viya phassassapi pavattirūpattā yathāvutto vikappo anavasaroti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Tayo phassā phusantīti puggalabhedavasena vuttaṃ. Na hi ekaṃyeva puggalaṃ etasmiṃ khaṇe tayo phassā phusanti. “Tividho phasso phusatī”ti vā bhavitabbaṃ. Yasmā pana “nirodhaphalasamāpattiyā vuṭṭhitassā”tiādi yassa yathāvuttā tayo eva phassā sambhavanti, tassa anavasesaggahaṇavaseneva vuttaṃ “tayo phassā phusantī”ti.

Here, arrival (āgamanaṃ) means coming to this state, or because of that, it is called the arrival (āgamanikā); that very arrival, by changing the 'ka' to 'ya,' becomes 'āgamaniyā.' Emergence (vuṭṭhāti) refers to the arising of the path from its cause. 'Signless' (animittā) means the removal of the sign of permanence. Here, emergence itself is the measure, not the grasping and seeing. 'Desireless' (appaṇihitā) is the opposite of longing for happiness. 'Emptiness' (suññatā) is directly opposed to the view of self, because the emptiness of beings is clearly seen. The path is called 'signless' because of the arrival of insight. The fruit is called 'signless' because of the arrival of the path. An objection might arise that since the arrival and its establishment are not defined, the gaining of the name 'signless,' etc., by insight is not established—this is the intention. Therefore, it is said, "thus," etc. Moreover, since insight is also of such a nature at the time of the emergence of the path, it should be understood that, by virtue of this, contact also occurs in the same way as the path and fruit, and so the aforementioned objection is inapplicable. "Three contacts touch" is said with reference to the distinction among individuals. For it is not that a single person is touched by three contacts at that very moment. Alternatively, it should be stated as, "A threefold contact touches." Furthermore, since for one "who has emerged from the attainment of cessation," etc., for whom the three contacts occur as described, it is said, "three contacts touch," by way of taking them all inclusively.

Nibbānaṃ viveko nāma sabbasaṅkhāravivittabhāvato. Tasmiṃ viveke ekanteneva ninnapoṇattā eva hi te paṭippassaddhasabbussukkā uttamapurisā catunnaṃ khandhānaṃ appavattiṃ anavasesaggahaṇalakkhaṇaṃ nirodhasamāpattiṃ samāpajjantīti.

Nibbāna is called seclusion because it is the state of being separated from all formations. In that seclusion, those supreme persons, being exclusively inclined towards it, with all their anxieties subsided, attain the attainment of cessation—the non-occurrence of the four mental aggregates—characterized by the non-arising of any remainder.

Dutiyakāmabhūsuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Second Kāmabhū Sutta is completed.

7. Godattasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Godatta Sutta

349. Nesanti [V 2.0089] appamāṇacetovimutti-ākiñcaññacetovimuttisaññitānaṃ jhānānaṃ. Atthopi nānāti ānetvā yojanā. Pharaṇaappamāṇatāya “appamāṇā cetovimuttī”ti laddhanāmaṃ brahmavihārajjhānanti āha “bhūmantarato”tiādi. Ākiñcaññā cetovimuttīti ākiñcaññāyatanajjhānanti āha “bhūmantarato”tiādi. Vipassanāti aniccānupassanā, sabbāpi vā. Pamāṇakaraṇo nāma yassa sayaṃ uppajjati, tassa guṇābhāvadassanavasena pamāṇakaraṇato.

349. "Of these" refers to the jhānas designated as the immeasurable liberation of mind and the liberation of mind of nothingness. The meaning is also diverse, and the explanation is given by connecting it to the text. The jhāna of the divine abodes is called 'the immeasurable liberation of mind' due to boundless pervasion, as he says, "from the earth's surface," etc. The 'nothingness liberation of mind' refers to the jhāna of the base of nothingness, as he says, "from the earth's surface," etc. Insight is the contemplation of impermanence, or all of it. The 'measure-maker' is one for whom it arises spontaneously, because it makes a measure by seeing the absence of qualities.

Pharaṇaappamāṇatāyāti [M 2.0377] pharaṇavasena appamāṇagocaratāya. Nibbānampi appamāṇameva pamāṇagocarānaṃ kilesānaṃ ārammaṇabhāvassapi anāgamanato. Khalanti khale pasāritasālisīsādibhaṇḍaṃ. Kiñcehīti maddassu. Tenāha “maddanaṭṭho”ti. Ārammaṇabhūtaṃ, palibuddhakaṃ vā natthi etassa kiñcananti akiñcanaṃ, akiñcanameva ākiñcaññaṃ.

"Due to the boundless pervasion" means having an immeasurable range through pervasion. Even Nibbāna is immeasurable, because defilements, which have measurable objects, do not come to it as an object. 'Khalanti' (they thresh) refers to the goods such as rice ears spread out on the threshing floor. "Kiñcehi" means "crush it." Therefore, it is said, "the meaning is crushing." Having nothing as an object, or as an obstruction, is "nothingness" (akiñcanaṃ), and that very nothingness is "the base of nothingness" (ākiñcaññaṃ).

Rūpanimittassāti kasiṇarūpanimittassa. Na gahitāti sarūpato na gahitā, atthato pana gahitā eva. Tenāha – “sā suññā rāgenātiādivacanato sabbattha anupavitthāvā”ti.

"Of the form-sign" means of the kasiṇa form-sign. "Not grasped" means not grasped in its own form but is grasped in meaning. Therefore, he says: "Because of the statement, 'it is empty of lust,' etc., it has pervaded everywhere."

Nānāti saddavasena. Ekatthāti ārammaṇavasena ārammaṇabhāvena ekasabhāvā. Tenāha “appamāṇaṃ…pe… ekatthā”ti. Ārammaṇavasenāti ārammaṇassa vasena. Cattāro hi maggā, cattāri phalāni ārammaṇavasena nibbānapaviṭṭhatāya ekatthā ekārammaṇā. Aññasmiṃ pana ṭhāneti idaṃ visuṃ visuṃ gahetvā vuttaṃ appamāṇādi pariyāyavuttaṃ, nibbānaṃ ārabbha pavattanato. Tasmā “aññasmi”nti idaṃ tena tena pariyāyena tattha tattha āgatabhāvaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

"Diverse" is by way of the word. "Having a single meaning" is because, by way of the object, by being the object, they are of a single nature. Hence, he says, "the immeasurable... having a single meaning." "By way of the object" means by means of the object. The four paths and the four fruits have a single meaning and a single object because, by way of their object, they have entered Nibbāna. However, "in another place" means that this was said by taking 'immeasurable,' etc., separately, spoken as a synonym, because it proceeds in relation to Nibbāna. Therefore, "in another place" is said with reference to its occurrence in various places through that particular mode of expression.

Godattasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Godatta Sutta is completed.

8. Nigaṇṭhanāṭaputtasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta Sutta

350. Āgatāgamoti vācuggatapariyattidhammo. Viññātasāsanoti paṭividdhasatthusāsano. Tenāha “anāgāmī”tiādi. Naggabhogganti avasanabhāvena naggaṃ, kuṭilajjhāsayatāya bhoggaṃ, tato eva nissirikaṃ. Naggatāya hi so rūpena nissiriko, bhoggatāya cittena. Bhagavato saddhāyāti bhagavati saddhāya. Tasmiṃ saddahanā okappanā tassa saddhātipi vattabbataṃ labhati. Gacchāmīti āgacchāmi, bujjhāmīti attho. Etaṃ nigaṇṭhena pucchitamatthamāha.

350. ‘One who has mastered the scriptures’ (āgatāgamo) is the teaching that has come forth by word, the scripture. ‘One who understands the Dispensation’ (viññātasāsano) is one who has penetrated the Teacher’s Dispensation. Therefore, it is said, ‘a non-returner,’ and so on. ‘Naked and crooked’ (naggabhoggaṃ) means naked through being unclothed, and crooked due to a crooked disposition, hence utterly without glory. In nakedness, he is without glory in body; in crookedness, he is without glory in mind. ‘Through faith in the Blessed One’ (Bhagavato saddhāya) means through faith in the Blessed One. The conviction and acceptance in him can also be called his faith. ‘I go’ (gacchāmi) means ‘I come,’ that is, the meaning is ‘I understand.’ This states the meaning about which the Nigaṇṭha asked.

Kāyaṃ [V 2.0090] unnāmetvāti kāyaṃ abbhunnāmetvā. Kucchiṃ nīharitvāti piṭṭhiyā ninnamanena kucchiṃ purato nīharitvā. Gīvaṃ pasāraṇavasena paggayha paggahetvā [M 2.0378] sabbaṃ disaṃ pekkhamāno. Sabbamidaṃ nigaṇṭhassa pahaṭṭhākāradassanatthaṃ “idāni samaṇassa gotamassa upari vādaṃ āropetuṃ labbhatī”ti. Tenāha “vātaṃ vā so”tiādi. Sakāraṇāti yuttisahitā. Pañhamaggoti pañhasaṅkhāto vīmaṃsā, evaṃ bhavitabbanti citteneva parivīmaṃsā pañhā. Eko uddesoti ekaṃ uddisanaṃ atthassa saṃkhittavacanaṃ. Veyyākaraṇanti niddisanaṃ atthassa vicāretvā kathanaṃ. Evanti iminā nayena. Sabbatthāti sabbesu pañhuddesaveyyākaraṇesu attho vitthārato veditabbo.

‘Having raised the body’ means having lifted the body up. ‘Thrusting out the belly’ means bringing the belly forward by lowering the back. ‘Extending the neck’ means stretching it out and raising it high, looking around in all directions. All this was done by the Nigaṇṭha to display an excited demeanor, thinking, “Now it is possible to raise a dispute against the ascetic Gotama.” Therefore, it is said, “Is it the wind or…” etc. ‘With reason’ (sakāraṇā) means accompanied by justification. ‘The path of questioning’ (pañhamaggo) refers to investigation, which is called a question; thus, a question is an investigation by the mind itself, thinking, “How should this be?” ‘A summary’ (eko uddeso) means a single designation, a concise statement of meaning. ‘Explanation’ (veyyākaraṇaṃ) means an elaboration, a speaking after considering the meaning. ‘Thus’ (evaṃ) means in this manner. ‘In all respects’ (sabbatthā) means the meaning should be understood in detail in all questions, summaries, and explanations.

Nigaṇṭhanāṭaputtasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta Sutta is concluded.

9. Acelakassapasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Acelakassapa Sutta

351. Alaṃ samattho ariyabhāvāyāti alamariyo. Ñeyyajānanaṭṭhena ñāṇameva paccakkhato dassanaṭṭhena ñāṇadassanaṃ, soyeva atisayaṭṭhena ñāṇadassanaviseso. Pāvaḷā vuccati ānisadapadeso, taṃ pāvaḷaṃ rajoharaṇatthaṃ nipphoṭīyati etāyāti pāvaḷanipphoṭanā, morapiñchavatti.

351. ‘Sufficient for the noble state’ (alaṃ samattho ariyabhāvāya) means worthy of being noble. Knowledge is in the sense of knowing what is to be known, and vision is in the sense of directly seeing; this itself, in the sense of excellence, is the special knowledge and vision. ‘Pāvaḷa’ is said to be a sitting place. That by which the pāvaḷa is shaken for the purpose of removing dust is a ‘pāvaḷa-shaker’ (pāvaḷanipphoṭanā); it is a peacock-feather whisk.

Acelakassapasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Acelakassapa Sutta is concluded.

10. Gilānadassanasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Gilānadassana Sutta

352. Mattarājā nāma eko bhummadevo bhūtādhipati surāpotalarukkhanivāsī. Tena vuttaṃ “mattarājakāle”ti. “Osadhitiṇavanappatīsū”ti vatvā te yathākkamaṃ dassento “harītakā…pe… rukkhesu cā”ti āha. Patthanāvasena cittaṃ ṭhapehi. Samijjhissatīti yathādhippāyaṃ samijjhissati. Tena hīti yasmā taṃ devāpi āsannamaraṇaṃ maññanti, tasmā sā varameva bhavissati, taṃ tumhākaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya bhavissatīti adhippāyo.

352. Mattarāja is the name of a certain earth-deity, a lord of beings, dwelling in the Surāpotala tree. Therefore, it is said, ‘in the time of Mattarāja.’ Having stated, ‘in herbs, grass, and forests,’ and showing them in order, he said, ‘myrobalan… and in trees.’ ‘Establish the mind with aspiration’ means establish the mind by way of aspiration. ‘It will succeed’ means it will succeed according to one’s intention. ‘Therefore’ means: since even the deities consider that death is near for him, therefore, that boon will indeed be the best, and it will be for your long-term welfare and happiness—this is the meaning.

Gilānadassanasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Gilānadassana Sutta is concluded.

Cittasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Connected Discourses on the Mind is concluded.

8. Gāmaṇisaṃyuttaṃ

8. The Connected Discourses with Headmen

1. Caṇḍasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Caṇḍa Sutta

353. “Yena [M 2.0379][V 2.0091] midhekacco caṇḍo caṇḍotveva saṅkhaṃ gacchatī”ti evaṃ pañhapucchanena dhammasaṅgāhakattherehi caṇḍoti gahitanāmo. Pākaṭaṃ karotīti dasseti attano caṇḍabhāvaṃ.

353. “Because someone here is fierce, he goes by the designation ‘Caṇḍa’ (fierce)”—thus, through the asking of this question, the Elder compilers of the Dhamma took ‘Caṇḍa’ as his name. ‘Makes it evident’ means he displays his own fierce nature.

Caṇḍasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Caṇḍa Sutta is concluded.

2. Tālapuṭasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Tālapuṭa Sutta

354. Vāloti vuccati tālo, tassa tālapuṭaṃ nāma. Yathā āmalakīphalasamānakaṃ, so pana tālasadisamukhavaṇṇattā tālapuṭoti evaṃnāmako. Tenāha “tassa kirā”tiādi. Abhinīhārasampanno anekesu kappesu sambhatasāvakabodhisambhāro. Tathā hesa asītiyā mahāsāvakesu abbhantaro jāto. Sahassaṃ denti naccaṃ passitukāmā. Samajjavesanti nepaccavesaṃ. Kīḷaṃ katvāti naccakīḷitaṃ kīḷitvā, naccitvāti attho.

354. ‘Vāla’ is called ‘tāla’. His name is Tālapuṭa. He was so named ‘Tālapuṭa’ because the appearance of his face was similar to a tāla fruit. Hence it is said, ‘of him, indeed…’ etc. Endowed with aspiration, he had accumulated the requisites for the enlightenment of a disciple over many eons. Thus he was born among the eighty great disciples. They give a thousand, wishing to see the dance. ‘Samajjavesa’ means the attire for performances. ‘Having played’ means having played the dance-game; the meaning is ‘having danced’.

Pubbe tathāpavattavuttantadassane saccena, tabbipariyāye alikena. Rāgapaccayāti rāguppattiyā kāraṇabhūtā. Mukhato…pe… dassanādayoti ādi-saddena mukhato aggijālanikkhamadassanādike saṅgaṇhāti. Aññe ca…pe… abhinayāti kāmassādasaṃyuttānaṃ siṅgārahassaabbhutarasānañceva “aññe cā”ti vuttasantabībhaccharasānañca dassanakā abhinayā. Dosapaccayāti dosuppattiyā kāraṇabhūtā. Hatthapādacchedādīti ādi-saddena saṅgahitānaṃ ruddavīrabhayānakarasānaṃ dassanakā abhinayā. Mohapaccayāti mohuppattiyā kāraṇabhūtā. Evamādayoti ādi-saddena saṅgahitānaṃ karuṇāsantabhayānakarasānaṃ dassanakā abhinayā. Te hi rase sandhāya pāḷiyaṃ “ye dhammā rajanīyā, ye dhammā dosanīyā, ye dhammā mohanīyā”ti ca vuttaṃ.

Previously, in seeing the course of events as they unfolded, with truth, and in the opposite case, with falsehood. ‘Due to lust’ means being a cause for the arising of lust. ‘From the mouth… to seeing, etc.’: with the word ‘etc.,’ it includes seeing the emission of flames from the mouth and the like. ‘And others… acting’ (abhinayā) means the displays connected with the taste of sensual pleasure, and the sentiments of love, humor, and wonder, as well as the displays of the peaceful and disgusting sentiments, as stated by ‘and others.’ ‘Due to hatred’ means being a cause for the arising of hatred. ‘Cutting off hands and feet, etc.’: with the word ‘etc.,’ it includes displays connected with the furious, heroic, and terrifying sentiments. ‘Due to delusion’ means being a cause for the arising of delusion. ‘And so on’: with the word ‘etc.,’ it includes displays connected with the compassionate, peaceful, and terrifying sentiments. For with reference to these sentiments, it is said in the Pāli text, “things that are lustful, things that are hateful, things that are delusive.”

Naṭavesaṃ [M 2.0380] gahetvāva paccanti kammasarikkhavipākavasena. Taṃ sandhāyāti taṃ yathāvuttaṃ niraye paccanaṃ sandhāya. Etaṃ “pahāso nāma nirayo, tattha upapajjatī”ti vuttaṃ. Yathā loke atthavisesavasena sakammakānipi padāni akammakāni bhavanti “vimuccati puriso”ti, evaṃ idha atthavisesavasena akammakaṃ sakammakaṃ katvā vuttaṃ – “nāhaṃ, bhante, etaṃ rodāmī”ti. Ko [V 2.0092] pana so atthaviseso? Asahanaṃ akkhamanaṃ, tasmā na rodāmi na sahāmi, na akkhamāmīti attho. Rodanakāraṇañhi asahanto tena abhibhūto rodati. Tamevassa sakammakabhāvassa kāraṇabhūtaṃ atthavisesaṃ “na assuvimocanamattenā”ti vuttaṃ. Mataṃ vā, amma, rodantīti etthāpi mataṃ rodanti, tassa maraṇaṃ na sahanti, nakkhamantīti pākaṭoyamatthoti.

Having taken on the guise of an actor, they are tormented in accordance with the result being similar to the action. ‘With reference to that’ means with reference to the torment in hell as described above. This is stated: ‘There is a hell called “Laughter,” and one is reborn there.’ Just as in the world, words that are intransitive become transitive due to a special meaning, such as in ‘a person is liberated,’ so here, due to a special meaning, what is intransitive is made transitive: ‘I do not, venerable sir, weep at this.’ What then is that special meaning? It is unbearableness, intolerableness; therefore, ‘I do not weep’ means ‘I cannot bear it, I cannot endure it’—that is the meaning. For one who cannot bear the cause of weeping is overwhelmed by it and weeps. That very special meaning, which is the cause of its being transitive, is stated as ‘not merely by the shedding of tears.’ In the phrase ‘Mother, weeping for the dead,’ here too, ‘they weep for the dead’ means they are unable to bear their death, unable to endure it—this is the obvious meaning.

Tālapuṭasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Tālapuṭa Sutta is concluded.

3-5. Yodhājīvasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-5. Commentary on the Yodhājīva Sutta and other texts

355-357. Yujjhanaṃ yodho, so ājīvo etassāti yodhājīvo. Tenāha – “yuddhena jīvikaṃ kappanako”ti, ussāhaṃ vāyāmaṃ karotīti yujjhanavasena ussāhaṃ vāyāmaṃ karoti. Pariyāpādentīti maraṇapariyantikaṃ āpadaṃ pāpenti. Tenāha “maraṇaṃ paṭipajjāpentī”ti. Duṭṭhu ṭhapitanti duṭṭhākārena attano paresañca atthāvahabhāvaṃ na gataṃ paṭipannaṃ. Parehi saṅgāme jitā hatā ettha jāyantīti parajito nāma nirayo. Asidhanugadāyasaṅkucakkāni pañcāvudhāni. Taṃ sandhāyāti taṃ yodhājīvaṃ puggalaṃ sandhāya. Etaṃ “yo so”tiādi vuttaṃ. Catutthapañcamesūti hatthārohaassārohasuttesu. Eseva nayoti eso tatiye vutto eva atthato visesābhāvato.

355-357. One who fights is a warrior, and this is his livelihood; thus he is called a warrior by profession (yodhājīvo). Therefore, it is said: ‘He makes his living by warfare.’ ‘He exerts effort and strives’ means he exerts effort and strives by means of fighting. ‘They bring about destruction’ means they lead others to a misfortune ending in death. Therefore, it is said: ‘They cause others to proceed towards death.’ ‘Wickedly established’ means established in a wicked manner, not having undertaken a state that brings benefit to oneself or others. ‘Defeated by others in battle and slain, they are born here’—thus, the hell is named ‘Parajita’ (Defeated). The five weapons are: swords, bows, clubs, iron spears, and discs. ‘With reference to that’ means with reference to that person, the warrior by profession. This is stated in the phrase ‘whoever that…’ and so on. ‘In the fourth and fifth (suttas)’ means in the suttas on elephant riders and horse riders. ‘The same method applies’ means it is the same as what was stated in the third (sutta), because there is no difference in meaning.

Yodhājīvasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Yodhājīva Sutta and other texts is concluded.

6. Asibandhakaputtasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Asibandhakaputta Sutta

358. Pacchābhūmivāsinoti [M 2.0381] aparadesavāsino. Udakasuddhikabhāvajānanatthāyāti attano udakasuddhikabhāvaṃ jānanatthañceva lokassa ca udakena suddhi hotīti imassa atthassa jānanatthañca. Upari yāpentīti upari brahmalokaṃ yāpenti. Sammā ñāpentīti sammā ujukaṃyeva saggaṃ lokaṃ gamenti. Tenāha – “saggaṃ nāma okkāmentī”ti, avakkāmenti ogāhāpentīti attho. Anuparigaccheyyāti anuparito gaccheyya.

358. 'Pacchābhūmivāsino' means those who dwell in other lands. 'For the purpose of knowing the state of purification by water' means for the purpose of knowing one's own state of purification by water, and for the purpose of knowing the meaning of this: 'the world is purified by water.' 'Upari yāpentī' means they lead beings up to the Brahma world. 'Sammā ñāpentī' means they rightly and directly lead beings to the heavenly world. Therefore, it is said: 'They cause to enter heaven,' meaning they cause beings to descend and immerse themselves in it. 'Anuparigaccheyyā' means one should go all around.

Asibandhakaputtasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Asibandhakaputta Sutta is finished.

7. Khettūpamasuttavaṇṇanā

7. The Commentary on the Simile of the Field Sutta

359. Thaddhanti kathinaṃ lūkhaṃ. Ūsaranti ūsajātaṃ. Catūhipi oghehi anabhibhavanīyatāya [V 2.0093] ahaṃ dīpo. Sabbaparissayehi anabhibhavanīyatāya ahaṃ leṇo. Sabbadukkhaparittāsanato tāyanaṭṭhena ahaṃ tāṇaṃ. Sabbabhayahiṃsanato ahaṃ saraṇanti yojetabbaṃ.

359. 'Thaddha' means hard and rough. 'Ūsara' means saline land. I am an island because I cannot be overcome by the four floods. I am a shelter because I cannot be overwhelmed by all adversities. I am a protection because of protecting from all suffering and dread. I am a refuge from all fear and harm. Thus should it be connected.

Udakamaṇikoti mahantaṃ udakabhājanaṃ. Bahi vissandanavasena udakaṃ na haratīti ahārī, parito na paggharatīti aparihārī. Sakkaccameva desenti saddhammagāravassa sabbasattesu mahākaruṇāya ca buddhānaṃ samānarasattā. Catasso pana parisā satthugāravena attano ca saddhāsampannatāya saddahitvā okappetvā suṇanti, tasmā tā desanāphalena yujjanti. Kiccasiddhiyā satthu desanā tattha sakkaccadesanā nāma jātā.

'Udakamaṇika' means a large water vessel. Because it does not carry water away by leaking, it is called 'non-carrying' (ahārī); because it does not drip all around, it is called 'non-spilling' (aparihārī). The Buddhas teach respectfully out of reverence for the true Dhamma, great compassion for all beings, and because of the Buddhas' possession of a consistent nature. The four assemblies, out of reverence for the Teacher and their own accomplishment in faith, listen, having believed and placed trust. Therefore, they are joined with the fruit of the teaching. Through the fulfillment of the purpose, the Teacher's teaching there came to be called 'a respectful teaching'.

Khettūpamasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Simile of the Field Sutta is concluded.

8. Saṅkhadhamasuttavaṇṇanā

8. The Commentary on the Conch-Blower Sutta

360. “Yo koci puriso pāṇātipātī musāvādī, sabbo so āpāyiko”ti vatvā puna “yaṃbahulaṃ yaṃbahulaṃ karoti, tena [M 2.0382] duggatiṃ gacchatī”ti vadanto attanāva attano vādaṃ bhindati. Evaṃ santeti yadi bahuso katena pāpakammena āpāyiko, “yo koci pāṇamatipātetī”tiādivacanaṃ micchāti. Cattāri padānīti “yo koci pāṇamatipātetī”tiādinā nayena vuttā cattāro atthakoṭṭhāsā. Diṭṭhiyā paccayā honti “atthi kho pana mayā”tiādinā ayoniso ummujjantassa. Balasampannoti samattho. Saṅkhadhamakoti saṅkhassa dhamanakicce cheko. Adukkhenāti sukhena. Upacāropi appanāpi vaṭṭati ubhinnaṃ sāmaññavacanabhāvato. Appamāṇakatabhāvo labbhateva. Tathā hi taṃ kilesānaṃ vikkhambhanasamatthatāya dīghasantānatāya vipulaphalatāya ca “mahaggata”nti vuccati.

360. Having said, 'Whoever is a person who kills living beings and speaks falsely, all of them are destined for the states of woe,' and then saying, 'Whatever one does repeatedly, by that one goes to a bad destination,' he contradicts his own statement. If this is so, then if one is destined for the states of woe due to frequently committing evil deeds, the statement 'whoever kills a living being' and so on is false. 'Cattāri padāni' (the four phrases) means the four portions of meaning stated in the manner of 'whoever kills a living being' and so on. They are conditions for a view for one emerging improperly with 'there is indeed by me' and so on. 'Balasampanna' (endowed with strength) means capable. 'Saṅkhadhamaka' (conch-blower) means one skilled in the task of blowing a conch. 'Adukkhena' (without difficulty) means with ease. Both access concentration (upacāra) and attainment concentration (appanā) are suitable, because it is a common designation for both. The state of being immeasurable is indeed obtained. For it is called 'mahaggata' (exalted) because of its capability of suppressing defilements, because of its long continuity, and because of its abundant fruit.

Na ohīyatīti yasmiṃ santāne kāmāvacarakammaṃ, mahaggatakammañca katūpacitaṃ vipākadāne laddhāvasaraṃ hutvā ṭhitaṃ, tesu kāmāvacarakammaṃ itaraṃ nīharitvā sayaṃ tattha ohīyitvā attano vipākaṃ dātuṃ na sakkoti, mahaggatakammameva pana itaraṃ paṭibāhitvā attano vipākaṃ dātuṃ sakkoti garubhāvato. Tenāha “taṃ kāmāvacarakamma”ntiādi. Kilesavasenāti pāpakammassa mūlabhūtakilesavasena. Pāṇātipātādayo hi dosamohalobhādimūlakilesasamuṭṭhānā. Kilesavasenāti vā kammakilesavasena. Vuttañhetaṃ – “pāṇātipāto kho, gahapatiputta, kammakileso”tiādi (dī. ni. 3.245). Yathānusandhināva gatanti yathānusandhisaṅkhātaanusandhinā [V 2.0094] osānaṃ gataṃ saṃkilesasammukhena uṭṭhitāya vodānadhammavasena niṭṭhāpitattā.

'Na ohīyati' (It is not left behind) means that in a continuum where kamma pertaining to the sense sphere and exalted kamma have been performed and accumulated, and stand having obtained an opportunity to give a result, among these, the kamma pertaining to the sense sphere cannot push the other aside and itself remain there to give its own result; but exalted kamma, indeed, is able to repulse the other and give its own result because of its weightiness. Therefore, he said, 'that kamma pertaining to the sense sphere,' and so on. 'Kilesavasena' (By way of defilements) means by way of the defilements that are the root of evil kamma. For killing living beings and so on originate from defilements rooted in hatred, delusion, greed, and so on. Or, 'kilesavasena' means by way of kamma-defilements. For it was said: 'Killing living beings, son of a householder, is a kamma-defilement,' and so on (Dī. Ni. 3.245). 'Yathānusandhināva gata' (It has gone according to the connection) means it has reached its end through the connection known as 'yathānusandhi', because it was concluded by way of the purification-dhammas that arose in the face of defilement.

Saṅkhadhamasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Conch-Blower Sutta is concluded.

9. Kulasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Kula Sutta

361. Evaṃ pavattaīhitikāti evaṃ dvidhā pavattaīhitikā. Dvīhitikā dukkarajīvikapayogā. Salākamattaṃ vuttaṃ etthāti salākā vuttā, purimapade uttarapadalopo. Ubhatokoṭikanti yadi “kulānudayaṃ na vaṇṇemī”ti vadati, “bhūtā nikkaruṇā samaṇa tumhe”ti vādaṃ āropehi. Atha [M 2.0383] sabbadāpi “kulānudayaṃ vaṇṇemī”ti vadati. Evaṃ sante “kasmā evaṃ dubbhikkhe kāle mahatiyā parisāya parivuto janapadacārikaṃ carantā kulūpacchedāya paṭipajjathā”ti evaṃ ubhatokoṭikaṃ vādaṃ āropehīti gāmaṇiṃ uyyojesi.

361. 'Thus the livelihood proceeds' means the livelihood proceeds in two ways. The twofold livelihood is the practice of a difficult way of life. Here, 'a token's measure' is spoken of; 'a token' is mentioned, with the elision of the latter word in the former compound. 'With a double edge' means: if he says, 'I do not praise the prosperity of families,' then accuse him, saying, 'Ascetics, you are cruel and without compassion.' But if he always says, 'I praise the prosperity of families,' then in that case, accuse him, saying, 'Why then, during a time of famine, surrounded by a great assembly, do you travel through the countryside, acting so as to ruin families?' Thus, he instructed the village headman with a double-edged argument and sent him forth.

Dve anteti ubho koṭiyo. Bahi nīharitunti na vaṇṇemi vaṇṇemīti dve ante mocento taṃ pucchitamatthaṃ bahi nīharati nāma. Tattha dosaṃ datvā codento taṃ apucchaṃ karonto gilitvā viya anto paveseti nāma.

'The two extremes' means both ends. 'To bring it out' means that by releasing the two extremes—'I do not praise' and 'I praise'—one is said to bring out the meaning that was inquired into. By finding fault therein and accusing, one makes it a non-question and, as it were, swallows it and is said to cause it to enter within.

Ito so gāmaṇītiādi attano bhikkhūnaṃ aññesañca atthakāmānaṃ bhikkhappadānena aniṭṭhappattiabhāvadassanatthaṃ āraddhaṃ. Dānena sambhūtānīti dānamayena puññakiriyavatthunā sammadeva bhūtiṃ vaḍḍhiṃ pattāni. Saccena ariyavohārena sammadeva bhūtāni uppannāni saccasambhūtānīti āha “saccaṃ nāma saccavāditā”ti. Sesasīlanti aṭṭhavidhaariyavohārato aññasīlaṃ. Nihitanti tasmiṃ kule pubbapurisehi nidhānabhāvena nihitaṃ. Duppayuttāti kasivāṇijjādivasena duṭṭhu payuttā kammantā. Vipajjantīti nassanti. Kulaṅgāroti kulassa aṅgārasadiso vināsakapuggalo. Aniccatāti maraṇaṃ. Tasmiṃ kule padhānapurisānaṃ bhogānaṃ vā sabbaso vināso. Tenāha “hutvā abhāvo”tiādi.

The passage beginning 'From this, the village headman' was commenced to show that no undesirable outcome arises for his own monks and others who desire welfare through the giving of alms. 'Born of generosity' means that through the meritorious act of generosity, they have rightly attained growth and prosperity. 'Born of truth' means rightly arisen, born through noble speech; for it is said, 'Truth is indeed truth-speaking.' 'The remaining virtue' is virtue other than the eightfold noble speech. 'Placed' means deposited in that family as a treasure by ancestors. 'Ill-applied' refers to activities like farming and trade that are poorly engaged in. 'Perish' means they are destroyed. 'A family's ember' is an individual like a burning coal, a destroyer of the family. 'Impermanence' means death, or the complete destruction of the wealth of the leading men in that family. Therefore, it is said, 'Having been, it becomes non-existent,' and so on.

Kulasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Kula Sutta is concluded.

10. Maṇicūḷakasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Maṇicūḷaka Sutta

362. Taṃ [V 2.0095] parisanti taṃ rājantepure nisinnaṃ rājaparisaṃ. Nayaggāheti kutocipi asutvā kevalaṃ attano eva matiyā nayaggahaṇe ṭhatvā.

362. 'That assembly' means that royal assembly seated in the king's inner palace. 'Taking a viewpoint' means having stood in the taking of a viewpoint solely by one's own opinion, without having heard it from anywhere.

Kāretuṃ vaṭṭati sati sambhave paṭisaṅkhārassa, senāsanavināso na ajjhupekkhitabboti adhippāyo. Attano ettha kiccāvasāne yaṃ gihīnaṃyeva santakaṃ tāvakālikaṃ, taṃ gihivikatanti āha “gihivikataṃ katvā”ti. Na vadāmi pabbajjitāsāruppato.

'It is fitting to have it done' means that when there is a possibility for restoration, the destruction of a lodging should not be overlooked—this is the intention.
