Here, at the conclusion of one's own task, that which is temporarily the property of householders, that is what is meant by 'sold to a householder'; thus he says, 'having made it sold to a householder.' I do not speak contrary to what is suitable for one who has gone forth.

Maṇicūḷakasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Maṇicūḷaka Sutta is concluded.

11. Bhadrakasuttavaṇṇanā

11. Commentary on the Bhadraka Sutta

363. Evaṃnāmaketi [M 2.0384] mallā nāma jānapadino rājakumārā, nesaṃ nivāsatāya “mallā”icceva bahuvacanavasena laddhanāmattā evaṃnāmake janapade. Natthi etassa pattiyā kālantarasaññito kāloti akālo, so eva akāliko. Tenāha – “kālaṃ anatikkamitvā pattenā”ti. So pana “yaṃkiñci dukkhaṃ uppajjamānaṃ uppajjati, sabbaṃ taṃ chandamūlaka”nti evaṃ vutto dukkhassa chandamūlabhāvo, evaṃ chandamūlakassa dukkhassa kathitattā “imasmiṃ sutte vaṭṭadukkhaṃ kathita”nti vuttaṃ.

363. 'Of such a name' means in the country of such a name, so called because the Mallas, who are royal princes of that country, are so named, and the country received the name 'Mallas' in the plural due to their dwelling there. There is no time designated as 'another time' for its attainment, thus it is 'timeless' (akālo), and that itself is 'immediate' (akāliko). Therefore, it is said, 'by one who has attained without transgressing time.' Furthermore, since the fact that suffering is rooted in desire was stated thus, 'Whatever suffering arises, all of it is rooted in desire,' and since suffering rooted in desire was spoken of, it is said, 'In this discourse, the suffering of the round of existence is spoken of.'

Bhadrakasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Bhadraka Sutta is concluded.

12. Rāsiyasuttavaṇṇanā

12. Commentary on the Rāsīya Sutta

364. Rāsiṃ katvā mārapāsavasena, tatrāpi antarabhedena vibhajitvā pucchitabbapañhe ekato rāsiṃ katvā. Tapanaṃ attaparitāpanaṃ tapo, so etassa atthīti tapassī, taṃ tapassiṃ. So pana taṃ tapaṃ nissāya ṭhito nāma hotīti vuttaṃ “tapanissitaka”nti. So pana anekākārabhedena lūkhaṃ pharusaṃ jīvanasīlattā lūkhajīvī nāma. Tenāha “lūkhajīvika”nti. Majjhimāya paṭipattiyā uppathabhāvena avaniyā gandhabbāti antā, tato eva lāmakattā antā. Lāmakampi “anto”ti vuccati “antamidaṃ, bhikkhave, jīvikānaṃ (itivu. 91; saṃ. ni. 3.80), eko anto”ti evamādīsu (saṃ. ni. 2.15; 3.90). Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana aññamaññaādhārabhāvaṃ urīkatvā “koṭṭhāsā”ti vuttaṃ. Hīno gāmoti pāḷi. Gāma-saddo [V 2.0096] hīnapariyāyoti adhippāyenāha “gāmmo”ti. Gāme bhavoti gāmmo. Gāma-saddo cettha gāmavāsivisayo “gāmo āgato”tiādīsu viya. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana “gāmavāsīnaṃ dhammo”ti vuttaṃ, tesaṃ cārittanti attho. Atta-saddo idha sarīrapariyāyo “attantapo”tiādīsu viyāti āha “sarīradukkhakaraṇanti attho”ti.

364. Having made a heap by way of Māra's snares, and having also divided them by internal distinctions, the questions to be asked are made into one heap. Tormenting, self-torment, is austerity (tapo); he has that, thus he is an ascetic (tapassī). Moreover, it is said that he is established depending on that austerity, hence he is called 'one dependent on austerity' (tapanissitaka). Furthermore, due to living a rough and coarse life in various ways, he is called 'one of harsh livelihood' (lūkhajīvī). Therefore, it is said, 'one of harsh livelihood.' Because they are a wrong path from the middle way and are inferior, they are extremes; for that very reason of being base, they are extremes. The inferior is also called an 'extreme,' as in: 'This, monks, is an extreme of livelihoods' (Itiv. 91; SN 3.80), 'one extreme' (SN 2.15; 3.90), and so on. In the commentary, however, having accepted their mutual dependence, they are called 'sections.' The Pāli is 'a low village.' With the intention that the word 'village' is a synonym for 'low,' it says 'rustic.' 'What is in the village is rustic.' Here, the word 'village' refers to village-dwellers, as in 'the village has come,' etc. In the commentary, however, it is said, 'the custom of village-dwellers,' meaning their conduct. The word 'self' here is a synonym for the body, as in 'self-torment,' etc. Hence it says, 'the meaning is the causing of bodily suffering.'

Etthāti [M 2.0385] etasmiṃ tapanissitagarahitabbapade kasmā antadvayamajjhimapaṭipadāgahaṇaṃ? Attakilamathānuyogo tāva gayhatu idamatthitāyāti adhippāyo. Kāmabhogītapanissitakanijjaravatthūnaṃ dassane yathādhippetassa atthassa vibhajitvā kathanaṃ sambhavatīti te dassetvā adhippetattho kathito.

Here, in this blameworthy term 'one dependent on austerity,' why is there the taking up of the two extremes and the middle way? The intention is: let the devotion to self-mortification be taken up first, because this exists. Since, by showing the instances of one who enjoys sensual pleasures, one dependent on austerity, and the matter of annihilation, it is possible to explain the intended meaning by analyzing and stating it, having shown them, the intended meaning is stated.

Tamatthanti yo “kāmabhogītapanissitakesu garahitabbeyeva garahati, pasaṃsitabbeyeva ca pasaṃsatī”ti vutto attho, tamatthaṃ pakāsento. Sāhasikakammenāti ayuttena kammena. Dhammena ca adhammena cāti dhammikena adhammikena ca. Ayoniso pavattaṃ bāhirakaṃ sandhāya codako “katha”ntiādimāha. Itaro nayidaṃ tādisaṃ attaparitāpanaṃ adhippetaṃ, atha kho yoniso pavattaṃ sāsanikamevāti dassento “caturaṅgavīriyavasena cā”ti āha. Tattha “kāmaṃ taco ca nhāru ca aṭṭhi ca avasissatū”tiādinā (ma. ni. 2.184; saṃ. ni. 2.22.237; a. ni. 2.5) nayena vuttā sarīrenirapekkhavipassanāya ussukkāpanavasena pavattā vīriyabhāvanā “caturaṅgavīriyavasenā”ti vuttā. Tathā abbhokāsikanesajjikatapādinissitāva kilesanimmathanayogyā vīriyabhāvanā “dhutaṅgavasena cā”ti vuttāti. Ariyamaggena nissesakilesānaṃ pajahanā nijjarā. Sā ca attapaccakkhatāya sandiṭṭhikā tiṇṇaṃ mūlakilesānaṃ pajahanena “tisso”ti ca vuttā. Tenāha “ekopī”tiādi.

Explaining that meaning—the meaning stated as, 'Among those devoted to sensual pleasures and those dependent on austerity, one blames what should be blamed and praises what should be praised.' 'By reckless action' means by inappropriate action. 'By what is lawful and unlawful' means by what is righteous and unrighteous. Referring to external practice that proceeds unwisely, the questioner says, 'How?' and so on. The other, showing that such self-torment is not intended, but rather wise practice that proceeds within the Teaching, says, 'and by means of the fourfold effort.' Here, the cultivation of energy that proceeds by way of striving for insight with disregard for the body, expressed in the manner of 'Willingly, let only skin, sinews, and bones remain' (MN 2.184; SN 2.22.237; AN 2.5), and so on, is called 'by means of the fourfold effort.' Similarly, the cultivation of energy, relying on the open-air dweller's practice, the sitter's practice, austerity, and so on, which is suitable for crushing the defilements, is called 'and by means of the ascetic practices.' Annihilation (nijjarā) is the abandoning of all defilements by the noble path. And because it is directly visible to oneself, and through the abandoning of the three root defilements, it is also called 'threefold.' Therefore, it is said, 'Even one,' and so on.

Rāsiyasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Rāsiyasutta is concluded.

13. Pāṭaliyasuttavaṇṇanā

13. The Commentary on the Pāṭaliyasutta

365. “Māyañcāhaṃ pajānāmī”ti vacanaṃ kāmaṃ tesaṃ māyāvībhāvadassanaparaṃ, bhagavato pana māyāsāṭheyyādikassa sabbassa pāpadhammassa bodhimūle eva setughāto, tasmā sabbaso pahīnamāyo, sabbaññutāya māyaṃ aññe ca ñeyye sabbaso jānāti. Tena vuttaṃ “māyañcāhaṃ, gāmaṇi, pajānāmī”tiādi. Māyañca pajānāmīti na kevalamahaṃ māyaṃ eva jānāmi, atha kho aññampi idañcidañca jānāmīti.

365. 'I understand deceit'—this statement is indeed intended to show their deceitful nature. But for the Blessed One, the causeway of all evil qualities such as deceit and hypocrisy was destroyed at the very root of awakening. Therefore, he has entirely abandoned deceit. Through omniscience, he knows deceit and all other knowable things completely. Hence, it is said, 'I understand deceit, village headman,' and so on. 'I understand deceit' means: 'I do not only know deceit, but I also know this and that as well.'

Itthikāmehīti [M 2.0386][V 2.0097] itthīhi ceva tadaññakāmehi ca. Ekasmiṃ ṭhāneti ekasmiṃ padese. Ekekasseva āgantukassa gahaṭṭhassa vā pabbajitassa vā. Sattianurūpenāti vibhavasattianurūpena. Balānurūpenāti parivārabalānurūpena. Sattianurūpenāti vā saddhāsattianurūpena. Balānurūpenāti vibhavabalānurūpena. Dhammesu samādhi dasakusaladhammesu samādhānaṃ. Aggahitacittatā pariyuṭṭhakāritā. Tena lokiyasīlavisuddhi diṭṭhivisuddhi ca vuttā. Tathā cāha – “ko cādi kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ, sīlañca suvisuddhaṃ diṭṭhi ca ujukā”ti (saṃ. ni. 5.369). Tattha patiṭṭhitassa upari kattabbaṃ dassetuṃ “dhammasamādhismiṃ ṭhito”tiādi vuttaṃ. Ayaṃ paṭipadāti tassa kammaphalavādino satthu vacanaṃ sabbesañca ayaṃ mayhaṃ sīlasaṃvarabrahmavihārabhāvanāsaṅkhātāpaṭipadā anaparādhakatāya eva saṃvattati. Jayaggāhoti ubhayathāpi mayhaṃ kāci jāni natthi.

'With women's sensual pleasures' means with women and with other sensual pleasures. 'In one place' means in one region. 'For each one' means for each visitor, whether a householder or one gone forth. 'According to one's ability' means according to one's material ability. 'According to one's power' means according to the power of one's retinue. Or, 'according to one's ability' means according to the ability of one's faith. 'According to one's power' means according to the power of one's wealth. 'Concentration in the Dhamma' means concentration in the ten wholesome states. 'An unseized mind' means not being overwhelmed. By this, purification of mundane virtue and purification of view are stated. And so it is said: 'What is the beginning of wholesome states? Well-purified virtue and a straight view' (SN 5.369). To show what is to be done next by one established in that, it is said, 'established in concentration in the Dhamma,' and so on. 'This is the path' is the word of that Teacher who is a proponent of kamma and its fruit. And for all, this path of mine, described as restraint in virtue and the cultivation of the divine abidings, leads only to blamelessness. 'Taking the victory' means: 'In either case, there is no loss for me.'

Pañca dhammā dhammasamādhi nāma, vipassanāmaggaphaladhammamattaṃ vā. Tatiyavikappe sīlādivisuddhiyā saddhiṃ brahmavihārā yathāvuttatividhadhammāvahattā eva dhammasamādhi nāma. Pūrentassa uppannā cittekaggatāti vuttakhaṇikacittekaggatā. Sāpi cittassa samādhānato “cittasamādhī”ti vuttā, tassa paṭipakkhaṃ vikkhambhantī samucchindantī ca hutvā pavattā yathāvuttasamādhi eva visesena cittasamādhi nāma.

Five qualities are called 'concentration in the Dhamma,' or merely the qualities of insight, path, and fruition. In the third alternative, the divine abidings together with the purification of virtue and so on are called 'concentration in the Dhamma' because they bring about the three kinds of states as described. The one-pointedness of mind that has arisen for one who is fulfilling this is the aforementioned momentary one-pointedness of mind. That too, because it is a concentration of the mind, is called 'concentration of mind.' The aforementioned concentration, which proceeds by suppressing and eradicating its opposing factors, is specifically called 'concentration of mind.'

Pāṭaliyasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Pāṭaliyasutta is concluded.

Gāmaṇisaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Connected Discourses with Village Headmen is concluded.

9. Asaṅkhatasaṃyuttaṃ

9. The Connected Discourses on the Unconditioned

1. Paṭhamavaggo

1. The First Chapter

1-11. Kāyagatāsatisuttādivaṇṇanā

1–11. Commentary on the Discourses on Mindfulness Directed to the Body and Others

366-376. Asaṅkhatanti [M 2.0387][V 2.0098] na saṅkhataṃ hetupaccayeti. Tenāha “akata”nti. Hitaṃ esantenāti mettāyantena. Anukampamānenāti karuṇāyantena. Upādāyāti ādiyitvāti ayamatthotiāha “cittena pariggahetvā”ti. Aviparītadhammadesanāti aviparītadhammassa desanā, paṭipattimpi sāvakā viya garuko bhagavā. Dāyajjaṃ attano adhiṭṭhitaṃ niyyāteti.

366–376. 'Unconditioned' means not conditioned by causes and conditions. Hence, it is said, 'unmade.' 'Seeking what is beneficial' means practicing loving-kindness. 'Being compassionate' means practicing compassion. 'Having taken up' means having adopted; the meaning is, 'having encompassed with the mind.' 'The teaching of the unerring Dhamma' means the teaching of the Dhamma that is not in error. In practice, too, the Blessed One is a teacher, just as he is for his disciples. He entrusts the inheritance that he himself has determined.

Bhikkhāsampattikālādīnaṃ sattannaṃ sappāyānaṃ sampattiyā labbhanakāle. Vipattikāle pana ettha vuttavipariyāyena attho veditabbo. Bhāriyanti dukkhabahulatāya dāruṇaṃ. Amhākaṃ santikā laddhabbā. Tumhākaṃ anusāsanīti tumhākaṃ dātabbā anusāsanī.

At the time when the attainment of the seven suitable conditions—beginning with the time of the attainment of alms—is to be obtained. But at a time of failure, the meaning should be understood in the opposite way to what is stated here. 'Burdensome' means harsh due to the abundance of suffering. 'It is to be obtained from us.' 'Your instruction' means the instruction to be given by you.

Kāyagatāsatisuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourses on Mindfulness Directed to the Body and Others is concluded.

2. Dutiyavaggo

2. The Second Chapter

23-33. Asaṅkhatasuttādivaṇṇanā

23–33. Commentary on the Discourses on the Unconditioned and Others

377-409. Tattha ca natthi ettha taṇhāsaṅkhātaṃ nataṃ, natthi etasmiṃ vā adhigate puggalabhāvoti anataṃ. Anāsavanti etthāpi eseva nayo. Saccadhammatāya saccaṃ. Vaṭṭadukkhato pārametīti pāraṃ. Saṇhaṭṭhenāti sukhumaṭṭhena nipuṇaṃ. Tato eva duddasatāya. Ajajjaraṃ niccasabhāvattā. Natthi etassa nidassananti vā anidassanaṃ. Etasmiṃ adhigate natthi saṃsāre. Papañcanti vā nippapañcaṃ.

377–409. And herein, there is no 'inclination' (nataṃ), which is a term for craving; or, because when this is attained there is no individual existence, it is 'un-inclined' (anataṃ). The same method applies to 'taintless.' Because its nature is truth, it is 'truth.' Because it goes to the far shore from the suffering of the round of rebirth, it is the 'far shore.' In the sense of subtlety, it is 'subtle,' 'refined.' For that very reason, it is 'difficult to see.' Because of its permanent nature, it is 'undecaying.' Or, because there is no demonstration of it, it is 'invisible.' When this is attained, there is no saṃsāra. Or, because it is without proliferation, it is 'non-proliferated.'

Etasmiṃ [M 2.0388][V 2.0099] adhigate puggalassa maraṇaṃ natthīti vā amataṃ. Atappakaṭṭhena vā paṇītaṃ. Sukhahetutāya vā sivaṃ. Taṇhā khīyanti etthāti taṇhakkhayaṃ.

When this is attained, there is no death for a person, hence it is 'deathless.' Or, it is excellent because it is unsurpassed. It is auspicious because it is a cause of happiness. Herein, craving is destroyed, hence 'the destruction of craving.'

Aññassa tādisassa abhāvato vimhāpanīyatāya abhūtamevāti. Kutoci paccayato anibbattameva hutvā bhūtaṃ vijjamānaṃ. Tenāha “ajātaṃ hutvā atthī”ti. Natthi ettha dukkhanti niddukkhaṃ, tassa bhāvo niddukkhattaṃ. Tasmā anītikaṃ ītirahitaṃ. Vānaṃ vuccati taṇhā, tadabhāvena nibbānaṃ. Byābajjhaṃ vuccati dukkhaṃ, tadabhāvena abyābajjhaṃ. Paramatthato saccato suddhibhāvena. Kāmā eva puthujjanehi allīyitabbato ālayā. Esa nayo sesesupi. Patiṭṭhaṭṭhenāti patiṭṭhābhāvena vaṭṭadukkhato muccitukāmānaṃ dīpasadisaṃ oghehi anajjhottharaṇīyattā. Allīyitabbayuttaṭṭhenāti allīyituṃ arahabhāvato. Tāyanaṭṭhenāti saparatāyanaṭṭhena. Bhayasaraṇaṭṭhenāti bhayassa hiṃsanaṭṭhena. Seṭṭhaṃ uttamaṃ. Gatīti gandhabbaṭṭhānaṃ.

Because there is no other such thing, it is marvelous, hence 'unprecedented.' Having become unproduced from any condition, it is real and existent. Therefore, it is said, 'though unborn, it exists.' There is no suffering here, hence 'free from suffering,' and its state is freedom from suffering. Thus, it is free from affliction, devoid of misfortune. Craving is called 'vāna,' and due to its absence, it is Nibbāna. Affliction is called 'byābajjhaṃ,' and due to its absence, it is 'abyābajjhaṃ' (non-affliction). In the ultimate sense, by its nature of truth and purity. Sensual pleasures are 'attachments' because they are what ordinary people cling to. This same principle applies to the rest. In the sense of being a support, it is like an island for those wishing to be freed from the suffering of the round of rebirth, as it is unassailable by the floods. In the sense of being worthy of clinging, it is because it is worthy of being clung to. In the sense of being a shelter, it is a shelter for oneself and others. In the sense of being a refuge from fear, it is in the sense of destroying fear. It is the best, the supreme. 'Destination' means the state of a gandhabba.

Asaṅkhatasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition of the Discourses on the Unconditioned and Others is concluded.

Asaṅkhatasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition of the Connected Discourses on the Unconditioned is concluded.

10. Abyākatasaṃyuttaṃ

10. The Connected Discourses on the Undeclared

1. Khemāsuttavaṇṇanā

1. The Commentary on the Khemā Sutta

410. Bimbisārassa [M 2.0389][V 2.0100] upāsikāti bimbisārassa orodhabhūtā upāsikā. Paṇḍiccaṃ sikkhitabhāvena. Veyyattiyaṃ visāradabhāvena. Visāradā nāma tihetukapaṭisandhisiddhasābhāvikapaññā, tāya samannāgatā.

410. 'A female lay follower of Bimbisāra' means a female lay follower who was part of Bimbisāra’s harem. 'Learnedness' (paṇḍiccaṃ) is due to her having learned. 'Cleverness' (veyyattiyaṃ) means being confident (visāradabhāvena). 'Confident' (visāradā) means the natural wisdom perfected through a triple-rooted rebirth-linking; she was endowed with this.

Acchiddakagaṇanāya kusaloti navantagaṇanāya kusalo. Aṅgulimuddāya gaṇanāya kusaloti aṅgulikāya eva gaṇanāya kusalo seyyathāpi pādasikā. Piṇḍagaṇanāyāti saṅkalanapaṭuppannakārino piṇḍavasena gaṇanā. Tathāgatoti khīṇāsavo, tathāgataṃ sandhāya pucchatīti khīṇāsavoti cassa arahattaphalavasibhāvitakhandhe upādāya ayaṃ paññatti hoti. Tesu khandhesu sati khīṇāsavā sattasaṅkhātā hontīti vohārena paññapetuṃ sakkā bhaveyya, asantesu na sakkā, tasmā paraṃ maraṇāti vuttattā tesaṃ abhāvā “abyākatameta”nti vuttaṃ. Yadi evaṃ tesaṃ abhāvato “na hoti tathāgato paraṃ maraṇā”ti puṭṭhāya “āmā”ti paṭijānitabbā siyā, taṃ pana sattasaṅkhātassa pucchitattā na paṭiññātanti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Yena rūpenāti sattatathāgate vuttarūpaṃ sabbaññutathāgate paṭikkhipituṃ “taṃ rūpa”ntiādi vuttaṃ. Yaṃ upādāyāti yaṃ khandhapañcakaṃ upādāya. Tadabhāvenāti tassa khandhapañcakassa abhāvena. Tassā paññattiyāti sattapaññattiyā abhāvaṃ. Niruddhaṃ na nidasseti.

Skilled in counting without omission means skilled in counting without remainder. Skilled in counting by finger-signs means skilled in counting by finger alone, like a counter. Counting by lumps means counting by way of aggregates by those who perform summation and calculation. The Tathāgata is one whose cankers are destroyed. Since the question concerns the Tathāgata, this designation 'one whose cankers are destroyed' is made in dependence upon the aggregates that have been developed through mastery of the fruit of Arahantship. When these aggregates exist, it is possible to conventionally designate a being as 'one whose cankers are destroyed.' When they do not exist, it is not possible. Therefore, because 'after death' was said, implying their absence, it was said to be 'undeclared.' If so, due to their absence, when asked, 'Does the Tathāgata not exist after death?' it would be proper to affirm 'yes.' However, it should be understood that this is not affirmed because the question was asked about one designated as a 'being.' 'By which form'—this was said in order to reject, in the case of the omniscient Tathāgata, the form that is spoken of in relation to a Tathāgata as a conventional being; thus it is said, 'that form,' and so on. 'Dependent on which' means dependent on the five aggregates. 'Due to its absence' means due to the absence of that pentad of aggregates. 'Of that designation' means the absence of the designation of a being. It does not indicate 'ceased.'

Khemāya theriyā vuttaṃ paṭhamaṃ suttaṃ bhagavato seṭṭhatthadīpanato aggapadāvacaraṃva hotīti vuttaṃ “aggapadasmi”nti.

The first discourse spoken by the Therī Khemā, because it illuminates the supreme meaning of the Blessed One, is as if it pertains to the supreme state; thus it is said, 'in the supreme state.'

Khemāsuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Khemā Sutta is finished.

2. Anurādhasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Anurādha Sutta

411. Idha saḷāyatanavagge saṅgāyanavasena saṅgītikārehi vuttaṃ.

411. Here in the Saḷāyatanavagga, this was stated by the compilers of the canon in accordance with the recitation.

3-8. Paṭhamasāriputtakoṭṭhikasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-8. Commentary on the First Sāriputta-Koṭṭhika Discourse and Others

412-417. Rūpamattanti [M 2.0390][V 2.0101] ettha matta-saddo visesanivattiattho. Ko pana so visesoti? Yo bāhiraparikappito idha tathāgatoti vuccamāno attā. Anupalabbhiyasabhāvoti anupalabbhiyattā.

412-417. In 'mere form,' the word 'mere' has the meaning of removing a special quality. And what is that special quality? It is the self, externally imagined and here referred to as 'the Tathāgata.' 'Of a nature that cannot be apprehended' means because it is a self that cannot be apprehended.

Paṭhamasāriputtakoṭṭhikasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the first Sāriputta Koṭṭhika discourse and others is completed.

9. Kutūhalasālāsuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Kutūhalasālā Sutta

418. Nānāvidhanti taṃtaṃdiṭṭhivādapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ aññampi vā nānāvidhaṃ tiracchānakathaṃ. Bahūnaṃ kutūhaluppattiṭṭhānatoti yojanā. Yāva ābhassarabrahmalokā gacchatīti agginā kappavuṭṭhānakāle gacchati, taṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Imañca kāyanti imaṃ rūpakāyaṃ. Cuticittena nikkhipatīti cuticittena bhijjamānena nikkhipati. Cuticittassa hi oraṃ sattarasamassa cittassa uppādakkhaṇe uppannaṃ kammajarūpaṃ cuticittena saddhiṃ nirujjhati, tato paraṃ kammajarūpaṃ na uppajjati. Yadi uppajjeyya, maraṇaṃ na siyā, cuticittaṃ rūpaṃ na samuṭṭhāpeti, āhārajassa ca asambhavo eva, utujaṃ pana vattateva. Yasmā paṭisandhikkhaṇe satto aññataraṇāya upapajjati nāma, cutikkhaṇe paṭisandhicittaṃ aladdhaṃ aññataraṇāya, tasmā vuttaṃ “cutikkhaṇe…pe… hotī”ti.

418. 'Various kinds' refers to various kinds of worldly talk connected with this or that view and doctrine, or other such things. The explanation is that it is a place where curiosity arises for many. 'As far as the Ābhassara Brahmā world' means that it goes there at the time of the world-cycle's destruction by fire; this was said in reference to that. 'This body' refers to this material body. 'Laid down by the death-consciousness' means it is laid down as it is being broken up by the death-consciousness. For the kamma-born matter that arose at the arising-moment of the seventeenth consciousness prior to the death-consciousness ceases together with the death-consciousness; after that, no kamma-born matter arises. If it were to arise, there would be no death. The death-consciousness does not generate matter, and nutriment-born matter is impossible, but temperature-born matter continues. Because at the moment of rebirth-linking a being is indeed reborn into some state, whereas at the moment of death, a rebirth-linking consciousness for some state has not been obtained, therefore it is said: 'at the moment of death... and so on... it is so.'

Kutūhalasālāsuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Kutūhalasālā Sutta is concluded.

10. Ānandasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Ānanda Sutta

419. Tesaṃ laddhiyāti tesaṃ sassatavādānaṃ laddhiyā saddhiṃ etaṃ “atthattā”ti vacanaṃ ekaṃ abhavissa. Tato eva anulomaṃ taṃ nābhavissa ñāṇassāti asāraṃ etanti adhippāyo. Api nu metassāti me etassa anattāti vipassanāñāṇassa anulomaṃ api nu abhavissa, vilomakameva tassa siyāti attho.

419. 'According to their belief' means that for those eternalists, this statement 'there is a self' would be consistent with their view. Therefore, it would not be in conformity with knowledge; the meaning is that this is without substance. 'Would this be in conformity with mine?' means: would this be in conformity with my insight-knowledge of non-self? It would be contrary to it—this is the meaning.

Ānandasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Ānanda Sutta is concluded.

11. Sabhiyakaccānasuttavaṇṇanā

11. Commentary on the Sabhiya Kaccāna Sutta

420. Yassapa’ssāti [M 2.0391][V 2.0102] pāṭhassa ayaṃ piṇḍattho “āvuso”tiādi. Tatthāyaṃ sambandho – āvuso, yassapi puggalassa tīṇi vassāni vuṭṭho, ettakena kālena “hetumhi sati rūpītiādipaññāpanā hoti, asati na hotī”ti ettakaṃ byākaraṇaṃ bhaveyya, tassa puggalassa ettakameva bahu, ko pana vādo atikkante! Ito atikkante dhammadesanānaye vādoyeva vattabbameva natthīti therassa pañhabyākaraṇaṃ sutvā paribbājako pītisomanassaṃ pavedesi.

420. Of the text 'Whosever...' this is the condensed meaning: 'Friend,' and so on. Herein, this is the connection: Friend, for whatever person has lived the life for three rains-retreats, in that much time there would be this much of an explanation: 'When there is a cause, the designation "form," etc., occurs; when there is not, it does not occur.' For that person, this much is a great deal; what then to say of one who has gone beyond! For one who has surpassed this, in the method of the Dhamma teaching, there is nothing at all to be said by way of debate. Having heard the elder's explanation of the question, the wanderer expressed his joy and gladness.

Sabhiyakaccānasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Sabhiya Kaccāna Sutta is concluded.

Abyākatasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Connected Discourses on the Undeclared is concluded.

Sāratthappakāsiniyā saṃyuttanikāya-aṭṭhakathāya

Sāratthappakāsinī, the commentary on the Saṃyutta Nikāya.

Saḷāyatanavaggavaṇṇanāya līnatthappakāsanā samattā.

The Exposition of the Hidden Meanings for the Commentary on the Section on the Six Sense Bases is completed.
